The King was never man of mine, nor should have been had I any say in the matter." She half closed her eyes again. "Doubtless had it been otherwise the King would have constrained me by threats and tortures to forswear myself. I am as I was when I came to Dover. As the King saw me so he left me. Yet do I maintain and avow it was rather because he feared alliance with my brother's party than for any foulness of my person."

Katharine passed her hands over her eyes.

"I do feel myself a thief and a cozener," she said.

"Ye be none," the Queen said; "ye take no more than what I least prize of this world. Had it not been thee it might have been a worse; for assuredly I was not made to foot it with this King."

"Nevertheless—" Katharine began. But the Queen was no more content to listen to her.
===Chinese===
她又望了望凯瑟琳，头一遭像是专门在对她说话：

“我看得出，你是个见不得世界不平、手痒要伸手匡正的女子。干这营生向来男多于女，可我从来不是那路人。国王从不曾是我的人；这事倘有我置喙的余地，他也断不会是。”她又半阖了眼睛。“无疑，若非如此，国王早就会用威吓与苦刑逼我发伪誓了。我如今还是我在多佛（Dover）登岸时的模样；国王怎样见我，便怎样撇下了我。可我仍然坚持，并且声言：那与其说是因为我身体丑陋，毋宁说是因为他畏惧同我家兄一党结盟。”

凯瑟琳用手掩了掩眼睛。

“我真觉得自己是个贼、是个诓人的骗子，”她说。

“你不是，”王后说；“你拿去的，不过是这世上我最不看重的东西。倘不是你，换了旁人兴许更糟；因为我断然不是生来与这位国王对舞共步的人。”

“然而——”凯瑟琳刚开口。王后却再不肯听她了。
===Original===
"Ye are as some I have known," she said; "they scruple to take what they very much crave, though it hang ready to drop into their hands; because they much crave it, therefore they scruple." She had a small golden bullet beneath her clasped hands, and she cast it into a basin of silver that stood on a tripod beside her skirts. At the silvery clash and roll of the ball's running sound on the metal, doors opened along the gallery, and servitors came in bearing Rhenish wine in glass flagons and, upon great salvers, cakes in the forms of hearts or twisted into truelove-knots of pastry.

Katharine noted these things as being worthy of imitation.

"It is no more to me," the Queen said, "to lose the other things to you than to lose to you the wine that you shall drink or a pile of cakes." Nevertheless she left Katharine upon her knees till she had taken her cup, for it pleased her that her servitors should see her treated with due worship.
===Chinese===
“你同我认识的某些人一个样，”她说；“极想得到一样东西，明知它悬垂在手边、转眼便要落进掌心，却偏要踌躇不取；正因为想得切，所以才踌躇。”她交叠的双手底下原压着一枚小小的金丸，此刻随手把它抛进裙边三足架上承着的一只银盆。丸子在银器上铿然一撞、辘辘滚转；随着这银响，长廊沿线的门开了，侍者们捧着玻璃长颈瓶盛的莱茵葡萄酒鱼贯而入，大银盘上则供着心形的糕饼，或是扭作同心结（truelove-knots）样式的酥点。

凯瑟琳把这些一一记在心上，以为都值得效法。

“把旁的东西输给你，在我同把你要喝的酒、一堆糕饼输给你，并无分别，”王后说。虽然如此，她仍让凯瑟琳跪着，直到自己接过酒盏才罢——因为她乐意让侍从们亲眼看见，她受人以应得的尊礼相待。

### VII / 七

===Original===
It was noon of that day when Katharine Howard set out again from Richmond to ride back to Hampton Court; and at noon of that day Throckmorton’s barge shot dangerously beneath London Bridge, hastening to Hampton Court. At noon Thomas Culpepper passed over London Bridge, because a great crowd pressed across it from the south going to see a burning at Smithfield; at noon, too, or five minutes later, the young Poins galloped furiously past the end of the bridge and did not cross over, but sped through Southwark towards Hampton Court. And at noon or thereabouts the King, dressed in green as a husbandman, sat on a log to await a gunfire, in the forest that was near to Richmond river path opposite Isleworth. He had given to Katharine a paper that she was to deliver to the master gunner of Richmond Palace in case the Queen Anne did satisfy her that the marriage was no marriage. So that, when among the green glades where the great trees let down their branches near the sward and shewed little tips of tender green leaves, he heard three thuds come echoing, he sprang to his feet, and, smiting his great, green-clothed thigh, he cried out: “Ha! I be young again!” He pulled to his lips the mouth of the English horn that was girdled across his shoulder and under his arm; he set his feet wide apart, filled his lungs with air, and blew a thin, clear call. At once there issued from brakes, thickets and glades the figures of men, dressed like the King in yeoman’s green, bearing bows over their shoulders, horns at their elbows, or having straining dogs in their leashes.

“Ho!” the King said to his chief verderer, a man of sixty with a grey beard, but so that all others could hear; “be it well understood that I will have you shew some ladies what make of thing it is to rule over jolly Englishmen.” He directed them how he would have them drive the deer at the end of the glade; he saw to the setting up of white wands of peeled willows and, taking from his yeoman-companion, that was the Earl of Surrey, his great bow, he shot a mighty shaft along the glade, to shew how far away he would have the deer to pass like swift ghosts between the aisles of the trees.

But the palace of Hampton lay deserted and given up to scullions, who lay in the sunlight and took their rare ease. For a great many lords that could shoot well with the bow were gone to play the yeoman with the King; and a great many that had sumptuous and gallant apparel were gone to join the ladies riding back from Richmond; and the King’s whole council, together with many lords that were awful or reverend in their appearance, were gone to sit in the scaffold to see the burning of the friar that had denied the King’s supremacy of the Church and the burnings of the six Protestants that had denied the presence of Christ’s body in the Sacrament. Only Privy Seal, who had ordered these things, was still walking in his gallery where he so often had walked of late.
===Chinese===
正是那天晌午，凯瑟琳·霍华德（Katharine Howard）再次从里士满（Richmond）启程，策马返回汉普顿宫（Hampton Court）；也是那天晌午，斯罗克莫顿（Throckmorton）的平底快船险险地从伦敦桥（London Bridge）桥洞底下掠过，急急赶赴汉普顿宫。晌午时分，托马斯·卡尔佩珀（Thomas Culpepper）打伦敦桥上过了——一大群人从桥南拥上桥来，要去看史密斯菲尔德（Smithfield）的火刑；也是晌午，或是迟上五分钟，小波因斯（Poins）纵马狂奔，从桥尾一冲而过，却不曾过桥，只取道萨瑟克（Southwark）直奔汉普顿宫。约莫也是晌午，国王一身庄稼汉的绿衣，坐在一截木头上等候一声炮号，身处那片林子——林子挨着里士满的河畔小径，与艾尔沃思（Isleworth）隔水相望。他已交予凯瑟琳一纸文书，倘若安妮王后果真向她证实那桩婚事算不得婚事，她便把这纸文书送交里士满宫的炮长。于是，当他在绿荫满地的林间空地上——大树垂枝近草，梢头绽出嫩生生的绿芽尖——听见三声闷响回荡而来，他霍地一跃而起，拍着裹在绿衣里的粗壮大腿，高声喊道：「哈！我又年轻了！」他把斜挎肩头、掖在臂下的英吉利号角吹口凑到唇边，两脚大大分开，吸满一口气，吹出一声细而清亮的号令。霎时间，灌木丛、密林与林间空地里都钻出人来，个个同国王一样身穿约曼（yeoman）的绿衣，肩上背着弓，肘边挂着号角，或是手里牵着皮带绷得笔直、跃跃欲试的猎犬。

「喂！」国王对他的总林务官（chief verderer）说——那是个六十岁上下、须发斑白的老者——却故意教旁人都听得见；「都给我记明白了：我要叫你们让几位夫人小姐瞧瞧，统领快活的英格兰汉子是何等滋味。」他吩咐众人当如何在那片空地尽头驱赶鹿群；又亲自照看剥了皮的柳木白杖如何竖立；随后从他那位约曼装束的同伴——正是萨里伯爵（Earl of Surrey）——手里取过大弓，沿着空地射出一支有力的长箭，好教众人明白，他要鹿群在多远之外、像迅疾的幽魂一般穿行于树木夹立的廊道之间。

可汉普顿的宫邸却空空落落，只剩下厨下杂役，横七竖八躺在太阳地里，偷得浮生半日闲。凡弓马娴熟的贵族老爷，多半跟了国王去扮约曼；凡有华服鲜衣的，多半策马去迎从里士满回来的女眷们；而国王的满朝枢密，连同许多仪表威严可畏的老爷，都去刑场坐定了，看那位否认国王教会至尊之位的修士受火刑，也看六名否认圣餐礼中有基督圣体临在的新教徒受焚。唯有降下这一切的御玺大人（Privy Seal），还在他那条长廊里踱步——近来他常在此踱步。

===Original===
He had with him Wriothesley, whose face was utterly downcast and abashed; he walked turning more swiftly than had been his wont ever before. Wriothesley hung down his great bearded, honest head and sighed three times.

“Sir,” he said at last, “I see before us nothing but that ye make to divorce the Queen Anne.” And the words seemed to come from him as if they cost him his heart’s blood.

Cromwell paused before him, his hands behind his back, his feet apart.

“The weighty question,” he said, “is this: Who hath betrayed me: of Udal; of the alewife that he should have had the papers of; or Throckmorton?”
===Chinese===
随他同行的有赖奥思利（Wriothesley），满脸沮丧羞惭；克伦威尔踱步转身，比往常任何时候都来得急促。赖奥思利垂下他那颗蓄着大胡子的忠厚脑袋，连叹了三口气。

「大人，」他终于开口，「依我看，摆在眼前的，无非是您要休弃安妮王后。」这话从他嘴里吐出来，字字都像是从心头剜出来的血。

克伦威尔在他面前站定，两手负在背后，两脚分开。

「要紧的问题，」他说，「是这个：是谁出卖了我：是乌达尔（Udal），是他本该从其手里拿到文书的那个酒娘（alewife），还是斯罗克莫顿？」

===Original===
He had that morning received from Cleves, in the letter of his agent there, the certain proof that the Duke had written to the Emperor Charles making an utter submission to save his land from ruin, and as utterly abjuring his alliance with the King his brother-in-law and with the Schmalkaldner league and its Protestant princes. Cromwell had immediately called to him Wriothesley that was that day ordering the horses to take him back to Paris town. He had given him this news, which, if it were secret then, must in a month be made known to all the world. To Wriothesley the Protestant this blow was the falling in of the world; here was Protestantism at an end and dead. There remained nothing but to save the necks of some to carry on the faith to distant days. Therefore he had brought out his reluctant words to urge Privy Seal to the divorce of Anne of Cleves. There was no other way; there was no other issue. Privy Seal must abjure Cleves’ Queen, and the very savour of a desire for a Protestant league.

But for Privy Seal the problem was not what to do, a thing he might settle in a minute’s swift thought, but the discovery of who had betrayed him—for his whole life had been given to bringing together his machine of service. You might determine an alliance or a divorce between breath and breath; but the training of your instruments, the weeding out of them that had flaws in their fidelities; the exhibiting of a swift and awful vengeance upon mutineers—these were the things that called for thinking and long furrowing of brows. He considered of this point whilst Wriothesley spoke long and earnestly.

It was expedient before all things that Privy Seal keep the helm of the State; it was very certain that the King should not long keep to his marriage with the lady from Cleves; lamentable it was that Cleves had fallen away from Protestantism and from the league that so goodly had promised for truth in religion. But so, alas that the day had come! so it was. The King was a man brave and royal in his degree, but unstable, so that to keep him to Protestantism and good government a firm man was earnestly needed. There was none other man than Privy Seal. Let him consider earnestly that if it tasted ill with his conscience to move this divorce, yet elsewise such great ills should strike the kingdom, that far better it were to deaden his conscience than to sacrifice for a queen of doubtful faith the best hope that they had then, all of them, in the world. He spoke for many minutes in this strain, for twice the clock struck the half-hour from the tower above the gallery.

Finally, long-bearded, solemn, and richly attired as he was, Wriothesley went down upon one knee, and, laying his bonnet on the ground, stretched out a long hand.
===Chinese===
就在那天早晨，他从克莱夫斯（Cleves）收到当地代理人的信，内中铁证如山：公爵已致书皇帝查理（Emperor Charles），为保疆土不致覆亡，情愿彻底俯首称臣，并且同样彻底地背弃同他那位妹婿国王的同盟，背弃施马尔卡尔登同盟（Schmalkaldner league）及其新教诸侯。克伦威尔立即召来赖奥思利——赖奥思利那天正打点马匹，预备动身回巴黎城去。他把这消息告知了赖奥思利；此讯眼下虽还是机密，不出一月必得传遍天下。对于身为新教徒的赖奥思利，这一击便是天塌地陷：新教就此寿终正寝、烟消云散。剩下的无非是保全几个人的脖颈，好把这信仰捎到渺远的岁月里去。因此他才迫不得已吐出那些话，力劝御玺大人休弃克莱夫斯的安妮（Anne of Cleves）。除此之外别无他途；除此之外别无出路。御玺大人必须弃绝克莱夫斯的王后，弃绝对新教同盟求之若渴的那一点滋味。

但对御玺大人而言，问题不在当作何事——那不过是他一念之间便可断定的——而在查明究竟是谁背叛了他；因为他毕生的心血，都用在把他那部差役机器拢作一体之上。缔结一桩同盟，定下一桩离婚，不过一呼一吸之间的事；可调教你的工具，剔除忠诚有瑕疪之人，对叛徒昭示迅疾可怖的报复——这些才须得苦思冥想、长锁眉头。赖奥思利长篇恳切陈说之际，他思量的正是这一层。

头一等要紧的，是御玺大人须把定国柄的船舵；而国王断不会长久维持同克莱夫斯小姐的那桩婚事，这也再明白不过；克莱夫斯既已背弃新教、背弃那桩曾为信仰真理许下多么美好前程的同盟，实在令人痛心。可是事已如此——唉，这一天竟会到来！——事已如此。国王这人，就其本分而言也算英勇威武，却素无定见；故而要他守着新教、行善政，非有一个铁腕之人恳切辅弼不可。此人舍御玺大人而外别无他人。请他扪心细想：倘若促成这桩离婚于良心有碍，可若不然，滔天大祸便要落在举国头上；与其为一个信仰可疑的王后，牺牲他们当下举世唯一最好的指望，倒不如把良心麻木了的好。他如此这般说了好几分钟——长廊上方的钟楼里，报时钟已两次敲过半点。

末了，赖奥思利虽是长髯垂胸、仪态威严、衣着华贵，却单膝跪了下去，把帽子搁在地上，伸出一只长手。

===Original===
“My lord,” he said, “I do beseech you that you stay with us and succour us. We are a small band, but zealous and well-caparisoned. Bethink you that you put this land in peril if by maintaining this Queen ye do endanger your precious neck. For I were loath to take arms against the King’s Majesty, and we are loyal and faithful subjects all; yet sooner than ye should fall—”

Cromwell stood over him, looking at him dispassionately, his hands still behind his back.

“Well, it is a great matter,” he uttered elusively. He moved as if to walk off, then suddenly turned upon his heel again. “Ye do me more ill by speaking in that guise than ever Cleves or Gardiner or all my enemies have done. For assuredly if rumours of your words should reach the King when he was ill-affected, it should go hardly with me.”

He paused, and then spoke gently.
===Chinese===
「大人，」他说，「我恳求您同我们站在一起，扶助我们。我们人数虽少，却满腔热忱、装备齐整。请您细想：您若为护这位王后而危及自己宝贵的脖颈，便是把这江山置于险地。我本不愿兴兵干犯王上的威仪，我们个个都是忠贞不贰的臣仆；然而宁可——」

克伦威尔站在他跟前，冷眼打量着他，两手依旧负在背后。

「唔，这可是一件大事。」他说话闪闪烁烁，教人捉摸不透。他抬脚像要走开，却又猛地转过身来。「你这样说话，比克莱夫斯、加德纳（Gardiner）以及我的一切仇家加在一起，害我还狠。因为毫无疑问，你这些话的风声若传到心怀芥蒂的国王耳朵里，我的日子便不好过了。」

他顿了顿，随后放缓了口气。

===Original===
“And assuredly ye do me more wrong than ill,” he said. “For this I swear to you, ye have heard evil enow of me to have believed some. But there is no man dare call me traitor in his heart of them that do know me. And this I tell you: I had rather die a thousand deaths than that ye should prop me up against the majesty and awe of government. By so doing ye might, at a hazard, save my life, but for certain ye would imperil that for which I have given my life.”

Again he paused and paced, and again came back in his traces to where Wriothesley knelt.

“Some danger there is for me,” he said, “but I think it a very little one. The King knoweth too well how good a servant and how profitable I have been to him. I do think he will not cast me away to please a woman. Yet this is a very notable woman—ye wot of whom I speak; but I hope very soon to have one to my hand that shall utterly cast down and soil her in the eyes of the King’s Highness.”

“Ye do think her unchaste?” Wriothesley asked. “I have heard you say—”
===Chinese===
「而且可以断言，你们与其说是害我，不如说是冤枉我。」他说。「这一点我对你起誓：我的恶名你听得尽够，信上几分也是有的。可在知我之人当中，没有谁敢在心里骂我叛臣。我告诉你：我宁可死上千回万回，也不肯教你们把我抬举出去，同朝廷的威权与凛威相抗。这样做，你们兴许侥幸保得住我的性命，却必定要断送我拿性命去换的那样东西。」

他又顿住，踱了几步，又循着原路踱回赖奥思利跪着的地方。

「危险自然有一点，」他说，「可我看那不过是一点点。国王深知我是个多好的仆人，给他带来过多大的好处。我想他不至于为了讨一个女人的欢心便把我抛开。不过这女人也确实非同等闲——我说的是谁，你心里有数；但我指望很快便能到手一个人，好教她在国王陛下眼中声名扫地、污秽不堪。」

「您是认定她不贞了？」赖奥思利问道。「我听您说过——」

===Original===
“Knight,” Cromwell answered; “what I think will not be revealed today nor tomorrow, but only at the Day of Judgment. Nevertheless, so do I love my master’s cause that—if it peril mine own upon that awful occasion—I so will strive to tear this woman down.”

Wriothesley rose, stiff and angular.

“God keep the issue!” he said.

“Why, get you gone,” Cromwell said. “But this I pray you gently: that ye restrain your fellows’ tongues from speaking treason and heresy. Three of your friends, as you know, I must burn this day for such speakings; you, too—you yourself, too—I must burn if it come to that pass, or you shall die by the block. For I will have this land purged.” His cold eyes flamed dangerously for a minute. “Fool!” he thundered, “I will have this land purged of treasons and schisms. Get you gone before I advise further with myself of your haughty and stiff-necked speeches. For learn this: that before all creeds, and before all desires, and before all women, and before all men, standeth the good of this commonwealth, and state, and King, whose servant I be. Get you gone and report my words ere I come terribly among ye.”
===Chinese===
「骑士，」克伦威尔答道，「我心里作何想，今天不会揭开，明天也不会，唯有到末日审判那一日方见分晓。尽管如此，我对主上基业的热爱如此之深——纵使在那可畏的日子它连我自身的基业也要搭上——我仍要奋力把这女人扳倒。」

赖奥思利站起身来，浑身僵直，棱角分明。

「愿上帝保守结局！」他说。

「好了，你去吧，」克伦威尔说。「只有一件事，我好好求你：管住你那些伙伴的舌头，不许妄谈叛逆与异端。你的三个朋友，你是知道的，今天就因这类言语，非由我烧掉不可；你也是——你本人也是——事情若到那步田地，我也非烧你不可，不然便叫你丧命斧钺之下。因为这江山，非涤荡干净不可。」他一双冷眼霎时凶光灼灼。「蠢货！」他雷霆般喝道，「我要把这江山洗尽叛逆、涤清分裂。趁我还没细与你计较你这套傲慢顽固的言辞，快快去。你且记牢这一条：在一切信条之上，在一切欲念之上，在一切女人之上，在一切男人之上，高悬着这个共和邦国、这个国家、这位国王的福祉——我便是他的仆人。趁我还没凶暴地临到你们中间，快去把我的话传下去。」

===Original===
Making his desultory pacings from end to end of the gallery, Cromwell considered that in that speech he had done a good morning’s work, for assuredly these men put him in peril. More than one of these dangerous proclaimings of loyalty to him rather than to the King had come to his ears. They must be put an end to.

But this issue faded from his mind. Left to himself, he let his hands twitch as feverishly as they would. Cleves and its Duke had played him false! His sheet anchor was gone! There remained only, then, the device of proving to the King that Katharine Howard was a monster of unchastity. For so strong was the witness that he had gathered against her that he could not but try his Fate once more—to give the King, as so often he had done, proof of how diligently his minister fended for him and how requisite he was, as a man who had eyes in every corner of this realm.

To do that it was necessary that he should find her cousin; he had all the others under lock and key already in that palace. But her cousin—he must come soon or he would come too late!

Privy Seal was a man of immense labours, that carried him to burning his lamp into hours when all other men in land slept in their beds. And, at that date, he had a many letters to indite, because the choosing of burgesses for the Parliament was going forward, and he had ado in some burghs to make the citizens choose the men that he bade them have. He gave to each shire and burgh long thought and minute commands. He knew the mayor of each town, and had notebooks telling him the opinions and deeds of every man that had freedom to elect all over England. And into each man he had instilled the terror of his vengeance. This needed anxious labours, and it was the measure of his concern that he stayed now from this work to meditate a full ten minutes upon this matter of bringing Thomas Culpepper before the King.

Thus, when, after he had for many hours been busy with his papers, Lascelles, the gentleman informer of the Archbishop’s, came to tell him that he had seen Thomas Culpepper at Greenwich that dawn and had followed him to the burning at Smithfield, whence he had hastened to Hampton, the Lord Privy Seal took from his neck his own golden collar of knighthood and cast it over Lascelles’ neck. In part this was because he had never before been so glad in his life, and in part because it was his policy to reward very richly them that did him a chance service.
===Chinese===
克伦威尔在长廊两头漫无章法地踱着，心中暗忖：方才那一番话，算是半日里办成的一桩要务，因为这班人确凿把他置于险地。这般危险的话——声言效忠于他而非效忠于国王——入耳的已不止一遭。这风气必须根绝。

可这桩心事旋即从他心头淡去。独自一人时，他任凭两只手抽搐不止，狂乱得没了章法。克莱夫斯同它的公爵把他卖了！他最后的倚仗去了！那么剩下的只有一条计策：向国王证明凯瑟琳·霍华德是个淫乱成性的怪物。他罗织在她身上的证据如此厚实，叫他不能不再赌一回自己的命运——像往常屡屡做过的那样，让国王看个明白：他的臣子如何勤勉地为他挡风遮雨，他这个人如何不可或缺——这人在这国土的每一个角落都长着眼睛。

要做到这一点，他必须寻到她那位表兄；其余的人，他早已一概锁进那座宫里，钥匙在握。可她表兄——他须得快些来，不然便要来得太迟！

御玺大人是个操劳无度的人，劳碌逼得他挑灯夜战，直到举国上下都已在床榻上睡熟。而其时，他还有许多书信要写，因为各地正推选出席国会的市民代表（burgesses），在几座自治城里，他还得费些周章，好教市民们选出他吩咐的人选。每一郡、每一城，他都倾注了长久的思虑与细密的指令。他熟知每座城池的市长，手头的记事簿记着全英格兰凡有选举权之人的见解与行状。而每个人心里，他都灌进了他报复的恐惧。这需要焦灼的劳碌；而他眼下竟撇下这摊子事，单单为把托马斯·卡尔佩珀带到国王面前这一桩，整整沉思了十分钟——这便是他何等挂怀的尺度了。

于是，当他埋首文牍数个时辰之后，拉斯塞勒斯（Lascelles）——大主教麾下那位绅士密探——前来禀报，说他那天黎明在格林尼治（Greenwich）望见了托马斯·卡尔佩珀，一路尾随到史密斯菲尔德的火刑场，随即又急急赶到汉普顿，御玺勋爵大人便从自己颈上解下那圈骑士金项环，套上拉斯塞勒斯的脖子。这一半是因为他平生从未这样欢喜过，一半是因为凡是撞上运气替他效了劳的人，他都从丰厚重赏——这正是他的驭下之道。

===Original===
“Sir,” he said, “I grudge that ye be the Archbishop’s man and not mine, so your judgment jumps with mine.”

And indeed Lascelles’ judgment had jumped with Privy Seal’s. He was the Archbishop’s confidential gentleman; he swayed in many things the Archbishop’s judgments. Yet in this one thing Cranmer had been too afraid to jump with him.

“To me,” Lascelles said, “it appeared that the sole thing to be done was to strike at the esteem of the King for Kat Howard, and the sole method to strike at her was through her dealings with her cousin.”

“Sir,” Cromwell interrupted him, “in this ye have hit upon mine own secret judgment that I had told to no man save my private servants.”

Lascelles bent his knee to acknowledge this great praise.
===Chinese===
「先生，」他说，「你偏偏是大主教的人，不是我的人，叫我好生惋惜——你的见识竟同我的如此相合。」

说也确实，拉斯塞勒斯的见识与御玺大人的正相契合。他是大主教的机要亲随，许多事情上都能左右大主教的裁断。唯独这一桩，克兰麦（Cranmer）怕得厉害，不敢附和他。

「依我之见，」拉斯塞勒斯说，「唯一可做的事，是打击国王对凯特·霍华德（Kat Howard）的敬重；而打击她的唯一法门，便是借着她同她表兄的那段瓜葛。」

「先生，」克伦威尔打断他，「这一层，正搔着我心头秘而不宣的裁断——除了贴身仆役，我不曾告诉过任何人。」

拉斯塞勒斯屈膝一躬，领受这般隆重的褒奖。

===Original===
“Very gracious lord,” he said, “his Grace of Canterbury opines rather that this woman must be propitiated. He hath sent her books to please her tickle fancy of erudition; he hath sent her Latin chronicles and Saxon to prove to her, if he may, that the English priesthood is older than that of Rome. He is minded to convince her if he may, or, if he may not, he plans to make submission to her, to commend her learning and in all things to flatter her—for she is very approachable by these channels, more than by any other.”

In short, as Lascelles made it appear to Cromwell’s attentive brain, the Archbishop was, as always, anxious to run with the hare and hunt with the hounds. He was a schismatic bishop, appointed by the King and the King’s creature, not the Bishop of Rome’s. So that if with his high pen and his great gift of penning weighty sentences, he might bring Kat Howard to acknowledging him bishop and archbishop, he was ready so to do. If he must make submission to her judgment, he was ready so to do.

“Yet,” Lascelles concluded, “I have urged him against these courses; or yet not against these courses, but to this other end in any case.” For it was certain that Kat Howard would have no truck with Cranmer. She would make him go on his knees to Rome and then she would burn him; or if she did not burn him she would make him end his days with a hair shirt in the cell of an anchorite. “I hold it manifested,” Lascelles said, “that this lady is such an one as will listen to no reason nor policy, neither will she palter, for whatever device, with them that have not lifelong paid lip-service to the arch-devil whose seat is in Rome.”

Cromwell nodded his head once more to commend the Archbishop’s gentleman with a perfect acquiescence.
===Chinese===
「仁慈无比的大人，」他说，「坎特伯雷大主教大人（his Grace of Canterbury）的看法却是：这位女子须得怀柔。他曾送书给她，迎合她那点沾沾自喜的博雅趣味；也曾送她拉丁文与撒克逊文的编年史，想要——若能如愿——向她证明，英格兰的圣品制度比罗马的更古老。他打定主意：能说服她便说服她；若说服不得，便预备向她低头，称道她的学问，事事处处奉承她——因为走这条路子最易叩开她的门，比别的路子都强。」

总而言之，正如拉斯塞勒斯让克伦威尔留神听明白的那样，大主教素来巴不得一手随兔而奔、一手纵犬而猎，两头讨好。他是个裂教的主教，由国王任命，是国王的人，而非罗马主教的人。所以，倘若凭他那支如椽之笔、凭他笔下千钧的才具，能让凯特·霍华德承认他是主教、是大主教，他便乐意如此；倘若必须向她的裁断低头，他也乐意如此。

「然而，」拉斯塞勒斯归结道，「我曾力劝他莫走这些路子；或者说，倒不是劝他莫走这些路子，而是劝他无论如何也要朝另一头使劲。」因为凯特·霍华德断不肯与克兰麦干休，这是明摆着的。她要叫他向罗马跪地求饶，然后把他烧死；纵或不烧他，也要叫他穿上苦衣，在隐修士的斗室里终了残生。「依我看，」拉斯塞勒斯说，「情形再明白不过：这位小姐是那样一种人——道理听不进，权谋笼络不了；凡是终生不曾口头上臣事罗马那位魔王的人，她无论使什么盘算，也决不同他们虚与委蛇。」

克伦威尔又颔了颔首，对大主教手下这位先生嘉许一番，全然首肯。

===Original===
It had chanced that that morning Lascelles had gone to Greenwich to fetch for the Archbishop some books and tractates. The Archbishop was minded to lend them to the Bishop Hugh Latimer of Worcester; that day he was to dispute publicly with the friar Forest that was cast to be burned. And, coming to Greenwich, still thinking much upon Katharine Howard and her cousin, at the dawn, Lascelles had seen the tall, drunken, red-bearded man in green, with his squat, broad gossip in grey, come staggering up from the ship at the public quay.

“I did leave my burden of books,” he said; “for what be Bishop Hugh Latimer’s arguments from a pulpit to a burning priest to the pulling down of this woman?” He had dogged Thomas Culpepper and his crony; he had seen him burst open windows, cast meat about in the mud and feed the populace of the Greenwich hamlet.

“And for sure,” he said, “if the King’s Highness should see this man’s filthiness and foul demeanour, he will not be fain to feed after such a make of hound.”

Coming to Smithfield, where Culpepper stayed to cheer on the business, Lascelles had very swiftly begged the Archbishop, where, behind Hugh Larimer’s pulpit, he sat to see Friar Forest corrected—had very swiftly begged the Archbishop to give him leave to come to Hampton.

“Sir,” Lascelles said, “with a great sigh he gave me leave; for much he fears to have a hand in this matter.”

“Why, he shall have no hand,” Cromwell said. He clapped his hands, and told the blonde pageboy that appeared to send him very quickly Viridus, that had had this matter in his care.
===Chinese===
事情也凑巧：那天早晨，拉斯塞勒斯到格林尼治去替大主教取几部书和几册论说。大主教有意把它们借给伍斯特主教休·拉蒂默（Bishop Hugh Latimer of Worcester）；那一天，拉蒂默要同那被判火刑的弗雷斯特修士（friar Forest）当众辩难。拉斯塞勒斯一路惦记着凯瑟琳·霍华德同她表兄的事，走到格林尼治，天光黎明时分，看见那红胡子的高个子醉汉身穿绿衣，同他那个矮墩墩、宽肩膀、一身灰衣的酒友，摇摇晃晃从公共码头边的船上下来。

「我便撂下了那担子书，」他说；「休·拉蒂默主教站上讲坛、对一个正要受火刑的神父摆出的那些道理，同扳倒这个女人有什么相干？」他盯住了托马斯·卡尔佩珀和他的酒友；眼见他砸开窗子，把肉块扔得满地泥泞，喂饱了格林尼治村子的那一帮子百姓。

「而且拿得准，」他说，「国王陛下若亲眼见了这人的龌龊行藏、下流做派，断不会再乐意豢养这等一条猎狗。」

到了史密斯菲尔德——卡尔佩珀留在那里给这场刑罚喝彩助威——拉斯塞勒斯赶忙向大主教告便；那时大主教正坐在休·拉蒂默的讲坛后头，看弗雷斯特修士受惩创。他匆匆恳请大主教准许他到汉普顿来。

「大人，」拉斯塞勒斯说，「他长叹一声，准了我的假；因为这桩事，他怕沾手怕得厉害。」

「哼，横竖不叫他沾手，」克伦威尔说。他拍了两下手，吩咐应声而来的金发小侍童速去请维里杜斯（Viridus）——这桩事原是交在维里杜斯手上照管的。

===Original===
Lascelles recounted shortly how he had set four men to watch Thomas Culpepper till he came to Hampton, and very swiftly to send word of when he came. Then the spy dropped his voice and pulled out a parchment from his bosom.

“Sir,” he said, “whilst Culpepper was in the palace of Greenwich I made haste to go on board the ship that had brought him from Calais, being minded if I could to discover what was discoverable concerning his coming.”

He dropped his voice still further.

“Sir,” he began again, “there be those in this realm, and maybe very close to your own person, that would have stayed his coming. For upon that ship lay a boy, sore sick of the sea and very beaten, by name Harry Poins. Wherefore, or at whose commands, he had done this I had no occasion to discover, since he lay like a sick dog and might not see nor hear nor speak; but this it was told me he had done: in every way he sought to let and hinder T. Culpepper’s coming to England with so marked an importunity that at last Culpepper did set his crony to beat this boy.” He paused again. “And this too I discovered, taking it from the boy’s person, for in my avocations and service to his Grace, whom God preserve and honour! I have much practised these abstractions.”

Lascelles held the parchment, from which fell a seal like a drop of blood.

“Sir,” he said, “this agreement is sealed with your own seal; it is from one Throckmorton in your service. It maketh this T. Culpepper lieutenant of barges and lighters in the town and port of Calais. It enjoineth upon him to stay diligently there and zealously to persevere in these duties.”
===Chinese===
拉斯塞勒斯简短叙说：他如何派了四个人盯住托马斯·卡尔佩珀，直盯到他到汉普顿，又如何一俟其到埠便飞马报信。随后这密探压低了嗓音，从怀里掏出一卷羊皮纸。

「大人，」他说，「卡尔佩珀还在格林尼治宫里的时候，我便赶着上了那艘载他从加来（Calais）来的船，想着但凡他的来路能打探出什么，便打探个明白。」

他把嗓音压得更低了。

「大人，」他又开口，「这国土上有那么些人——说不定就贴近您左右——本想拦住他不叫他来。因为那条船上躺着一个小子，晕船晕得死去活来，身上还遍体鳞伤，名叫哈里·波因斯（Harry Poins）。他为什么这样做、奉谁的号令，我没有机会查明，因为他躺得像条病犬，看不见也听不着也说不出；只是有人告诉我他干过这样的事：他千方百计阻挠托·卡尔佩珀（T. Culpepper）来英格兰，那份纠缠执拗太惹眼了，末了卡尔佩珀竟叫他的酒友把这小子打了一顿。」他又顿了顿。「还有一样东西也是我发现的，从那小子身上搜了来——因为我伺候大主教大人——愿上帝保佑他、尊荣他！——在我这些营生里，这种顺手牵羊的勾当做得熟极了。」

拉斯塞勒斯擎着那卷羊皮纸，纸下垂着一枚封印，红得像一滴血。

「大人，」他说，「这纸委任状盖的是您自己的印；出自您手下一个人，斯罗克莫顿。它委任这个托·卡尔佩珀充当加来城与加来港的驳船轻艇副官（lieutenant of barges and lighters）。还谆谆嘱咐他勤谨留驻彼处，克尽厥职，孜孜不倦。」

===Original===
Cromwell neither started nor moved; he stood looking down at the floor for a minute space; then he held out his hand for the parchment, considered the seal and the subscription, let his eyes course over the lines of Throckmorton’s handwriting that made a black patch on the surface soiled with seawater and sweat, and uttered composedly:

“Why, it is well; it is monstrous well that you have saved this parchment from coming to evil hands.”

He rolled it neatly, placed it in his belt, and four times stamped his foot on the floor.

There came in at this signal, Viridus, the one of his secretaries that had first instructed Katharine Howard as to her demeanour. Since then, he had had among his duties the watching over Thomas Culpepper. Calm, furtive, with his thin hands clasped before him, the Sieur Viridus answered the swift, hard questions of his master. He was more attached and did more services to the Chancellor of the Augmentations, whom he kept mostly mindful of such farms and fields as Privy Seal intended should be given to benefit his particular friends and servants; for he had a mind that would hold many details of figures and directions.

Thus, he had sent two men to Calais and the road Paris-ward with injunctions to meet Thomas Culpepper and tell him tales of Katharine Howard’s lewdness in the King’s Court; to tell him, too, that the farms in Kent, promised him as a guerdon for ridding Paris of the Cardinal Pole, were deeded and signed to him, but that evil men sought to have them away.

“Ye sent no boy to stay him at Calais with lieutenancy of barges?” Cromwell asked, swiftly and hard in voice.

“No boy ne no man,” Viridus answered.
===Chinese===
克伦威尔既不惊跳，也不动弹；他立着朝地板望了一小会儿；然后伸手接过那卷羊皮纸，细看那封印和落款，目光扫过斯罗克莫顿的笔迹——一行行墨字在浸了海水与汗渍的纸面上洇成一块黑斑——随后声色不动地说道：

「嗯，很好；这卷文书不曾落入歹人之手，真是好到了不得。」

他把它卷得整整齐齐，插进腰带，然后把脚在地板上连跺四下。

应着这道信号进来的，是维里杜斯——他手下的书记里，当初头一个调教凯瑟琳·霍华德习学进退仪节的便是此人。从那以后，监视托马斯·卡尔佩珀也列入了他的职分。维里杜斯老爷（the Sieur Viridus）安详而鬼祟，一双瘦手交叠在身前，一一应对主上急促而严厉的问话。他倒是更贴附着岁入增益法庭大臣（Chancellor of the Augmentations）、更多为其奔走效劳——他时时提醒那位大臣，御玺大人有意把哪些田庄田亩赐予他特相厚爱的亲友仆从；因为他这副头脑，装得下数字与指令的无数细目。

于是他派了两个人去加来、走上巴黎的大道，嘱咐他们迎着托马斯·卡尔佩珀，把凯瑟琳·霍华德在国王宫廷里如何淫佚的闲话讲给他听；还告诉他，肯特（Kent）的几处田庄——本为酬谢他把波尔枢机（Cardinal Pole）逐出巴黎而许给他的谢礼——地契早已签画妥当归了他，只是有奸人图谋把它们夺走。

「你可曾打发个小小子到加来，拿一纸驳船副官的委任把他绊在那儿？」克伦威尔追问道，声音急促而严厉。

「没打发小子，也没打发人。」维里杜斯答道。

===Original===
He had acted by the card of Privy Seal’s injunctions; men were posted at Calais, at Dover, at Ashford, at Maidstone, at Sandwich, at Rochester, at Greenwich, at all the landing places of London. Each several one was instructed to tell Thomas Culpepper some new story that, if Culpepper were not already hastening to Hampton, should make him mend his paces. If he were hastening to Hampton they were to leave him be. All these things were done as Privy Seal had directed.

“What witnesses have ye here from Lincolnshire?” Cromwell asked.

In his monotonous singsong Viridus named these people: Under lock and key in the King’s cellary house, five from Stamford that had heard Culpepper swear Kat Howard was his leman—these had really heard this thing, and called for no priming; under instruction in the Well Ward gate chamber, four that should swear a certain boy was her child—these needed to have their tales evened as to the night the child was born, and how it had been brought from the Lord Edmund’s house wrapped in a napkin. In his own pantry, Viridus had three under guard and admonition of his own—these should swear that whenas they served the Lord Edmund they had seen at several times Culpepper with her in thickets, or climbing to her window in the night, or at dawn coming away from her chamber door. These needed to be instructed as to all these things.

Cromwell listened with little nods, marking each item of these instructions.
===Chinese===
他行事一板一眼，全遵御玺大人的训令：加来、多佛（Dover）、阿什福德（Ashford）、梅德斯通（Maidstone）、桑威治（Sandwich）、罗切斯特（Rochester）、格林尼治，以及伦敦所有上岸的埠头，处处都布了人。每一处都受有嘱咐，要对托马斯·卡尔佩珀讲一桩新鲜的故事——卡尔佩珀若还不及赶奔汉普顿，听了好教他加快脚步；若已在赶奔汉普顿的路上，便由他去，不必理会。桩桩件件，都照御玺大人的吩咐办了。

「你从林肯郡（Lincolnshire）带到此地的人证有哪些？」克伦威尔问道。

维里杜斯拖着他那平板单调的调子，一一点出这些人：锁在国王酒窖房（cellary house）里的五个，来自斯坦福德（Stamford），曾亲耳听见卡尔佩珀赌咒说凯特·霍华德是他的情妇——这些人当真听过这话，无须事先调教；关在威尔沃德门（Well Ward gate）门房里受训的四个，要他们指天誓日说某个娃娃是她的孩子——这几个人还须把口供理整齐了：孩子落生在那一夜，又如何裹在一方襁布之中从埃德蒙勋爵（Lord Edmund）宅里抱出来。还有三个收在维里杜斯自家的膳房里，归他自己看守管教——要他们起誓说，当年伺候埃德蒙勋爵的时候，曾几次三番撞见卡尔佩珀同她在灌木丛里厮混，或是夜里往她窗口攀爬，或是破晓时分从她闺房门口出来。这几个人则须把这些情节一一教会。

克伦威尔听着，频频颔首，把这些嘱咐一条一条记下。

===Original===
“Listen now to me,” he said; “give attentive ear.” Viridus dropped his eyes to the floor, as one who lends all his faculties to be subservient to his hearing. “At six or thereabouts T. Culpepper shall reach this Court. Ye shall have men ready to bring him straightway to thee. At seven or thereabouts shall come the Lady Katharine to her room; with her shall come the King’s Highness, habited as a yeoman. Be attentive. Next Katharine Howard’s door is the door of the Lady Deedes. Her I have this day sent to other quarters. Having T. Culpepper with you, you shall go to this room of the Lady Deedes. You shall sit at the table with the door a little opened, so that ye may see when the King’s Highness cometh. But you shall sit opposite T. Culpepper that he may not see.” Viridus remained like a statue carved of wood, motionless, his head inclined to the ground. Lascelles had his head forward, his mouth a little open. “Whilst you wait you shall have with you the deeds giving to T. Culpepper his farms in Kent. These ye shall display to him. Ye shall dilate upon the goodness of the fields, upon the commodity in barns and oasthouses, upon the sweetness of the water wells, upon the goodliness of the air. But when the King shall be entered into the Lady Katharine’s room you shall give T. Culpepper to drink of a certain flagon of wine that I shall give to you. When he hath drunk you shall begin to hint that all is ill with the lady he would wed; as thus you shall say: ‘Aye, your nest is well lined, but how of the bird?’ And you shall talk of her having consorted much with a large yeoman. And when you shall observe him to be much heated with the subtle drug and your hintings, you shall say to him, ‘Lo, next this door is the door of the Lady Katharine. Go see if perchance she have not even now this yeoman with her.’ ”

Viridus nodded his head once up and down; Lascelles clapped his hands twice for joy at this contrivance. Cromwell added further injunctions: that Viridus should have in the corner of the gallery a man that should come hastening to him, the Lord Privy Seal, where he walked in the gallery; another who, at his own signal, should hastily bring the witnesses prepared against Kat Howard; another who should bring the engrossment of a command to behead T. Culpepper that night in the King’s Tower House, and yet another who should bring up guards and captains. All these, in their separate companies, should be set in the great room abovestairs next the King’s chapel, so that they might swiftly and without hindrance or accident come down the little stair to the Lady Katharine’s room. Again Viridus once bowed his head, moving his lips the while repeating these commands in words as they were uttered.

Cromwell paused again to think, then he added:
===Chinese===
「如今且听我说，」他说；「竖起耳朵来。」维里杜斯垂目望地，一副把周身官能都用来伺候耳朵的模样。「六点钟上下，托·卡尔佩珀到达这座宫廷。你要备好人手，把他即刻带到你跟前。七点钟上下，凯瑟琳小姐（Lady Katharine）回到她的房里；随她同来的还有国王陛下，一身约曼打扮。须要留神。凯瑟琳·霍华德的隔壁，是迪迪斯夫人（Lady Deedes）的房门。这位夫人我今天已打发到别处去住。你带着托·卡尔佩珀，到迪迪斯夫人那间房里去。你们坐在桌边，房门微微开一线，好教你们望得见国王陛下何时驾到。可你得坐在托·卡尔佩珀对面，教他瞧不见门外。」维里杜斯像一尊木雕的像，纹丝不动，头低垂向地面。拉斯塞勒斯头向前伸，嘴巴微张。「你们等候的时候，手边要有那几份把肯特田庄授予托·卡尔佩珀的地契。要一件件展给他看。要絮絮细说田亩如何肥美，谷仓与烘麦炉房（oasthouses）如何齐备，井水如何甘冽，空气如何宜人。等到国王进了凯瑟琳小姐的房门，你们便拿我交给你的某一壶酒与托·卡尔佩珀斟上。他喝了之后，你们便话里藏话，说那位他想娶的小姐事情不妙；要这样说：『是啊，你那窝铺垫得厚厚实实，可那鸟儿呢？』又要拿话点她同一个高大的约曼往来稠密。待你看出那药性微妙的药酒同你们的话头把他烧得坐不住了，便对他说：『瞧，这扇门隔壁便是凯瑟琳小姐的房门。去瞧瞧，说不定她这会儿正同那约曼在一处呢。』」

维里杜斯把头上下点了一回；拉斯塞勒斯为这条妙计欢喜得拍了两次手。克伦威尔又添了几道指令：长廊一角须有一名手下，好随时飞奔来报正在廊中踱步的他御玺勋爵大人；另一名，一俟他发出信号，便火速押上预备好了的对付凯特·霍华德的人证；又一名，须捧来那道誊定成文的处决令，当晚便把托·卡尔佩珀枭首于国王的塔楼宫室（King’s Tower House）；再一名，须带上卫队与队官。这些各成一股的人手，一律安置在楼上国王礼拜堂近旁的大厅里，以便他们抄小梯而下，直抵凯瑟琳小姐的房门口，又快又稳，不致受阻、不出差错。维里杜斯又把头一俯，一面嘴唇翕动，把方才一句句颁下的指令低声复述。

克伦威尔又停下来想了想，随后补上一句：

===Original===
“I will set this gentleman, Lascelles, to bring T. Culpepper to you. And because I will make very certain that this man shall not touch the person of the King, I will have this gentleman to stay with you in the room where you be, to follow with you T. Culpepper into the Lady Katharine’s room. He shall run with you betwixt T. Culpepper and the King; but if T. Culpepper be minded to fall upon the Lady Katharine, ye shall not either of you stay him. It were best if he might stab her dead. Doubtless he shall.”

“Before God!” Lascelles cried out, “would I were a king to have so masterful and devising a minister as Privy Seal!”

“Get you gone,” Privy Seal said to Viridus. “I ha’ no need to tell you that if ye do faithfully and to a good issue carry out this play, you shall be greatly rewarded so that few shall hold their heads higher than you in the land. Ye know how I befriend my friends. But know too this: that if this scheme miscarry, either of your fault or another’s, either through inattention or ill chance, either through treason or dullness of the brain of man, down to the least pin of it, ye shall not this night sleep in your bed, nor ever more shall you be seen in daylight above the earth.” He pointed suddenly from the window to the low sun. “Have a care that ye so act as ye shall see that disc again!”

Viridus spoke no word, but having waited a minute to hear if Privy Seal had more to enjoin, noiselessly and with his hands folded before him as they had been when he came, moved away over the shining floor. He went to tell the old, shivering Chancellor of the Augmentations that he must absent himself upon their common master’s errands. “I misdoubt some heads will fall tonight,” he added as he went; “our lord’s nose for treasons is sharpened again.” And that creature of Privy Seal’s shook beneath the furs that he wore, though it was already April; for the Chancellor had his private reasons to dread Privy Seal’s outbursts of suspicion.

In the gallery, Privy Seal still spoke earnestly with Lascelles.

“I give this part of honour and privilege to thee,” he said; “for though I was well prepared in all things, I trow I may trust thee better than another person.”
===Chinese===
「我要差这位先生拉斯塞勒斯，把托·卡尔佩珀带到你们跟前。我务必叫此人万无一失，碰不着国王的圣体，所以我要这位先生同你一道待在你那间房里，再同你一道跟着托·卡尔佩珀进凯瑟琳小姐的房。他要在你们之间奔走，一边挡住托·卡尔佩珀，一边挡住国王；可托·卡尔佩珀若存心扑向凯瑟琳小姐，你们两个都不得阻拦。他若能把小姐一剑刺死，那再好不过。毫无疑问，他是会的。」

「对天起誓！」拉斯塞勒斯喊道，「但愿我是个国王，也好有这样一位运筹帷幄、神机妙算的臣子，像御玺大人！」

「你去吧，」御玺大人对维里杜斯说。「不必我多嘴：你若尽心竭力，把这出戏演到底、演出好收场，赏赐必然隆重，普天之下没几颗头颅能抬得比你更高。我怎样厚待我的朋友，你是知道的。可你也要知道这一条：这盘棋若走砸了，或是你的过，或是旁人的过，或是疏忽大意，或是时运不济，或是存心叛逆，或是人的脑子愚钝——哪怕只坏了一根小钉子的分量——今夜你就别想睡在自己的床上，往后白日里也再休想叫人见你活在世上。」他忽然从窗口指向低垂的落日。「你给我放仔细些行事，务必叫你自己还看得见那轮圆盘再升起来！」

维里杜斯一言不发，只等了一小会儿，看御玺大人还有没有别的嘱咐，然后便不出一点声响，双手仍照来时那样交叠在身前，从光可鉴人的地板上悄悄退了出去。他要去告诉那位老迈瑟缩的岁入增益法庭大臣：他须得撇下自己那摊子事，为他们共同的主上跑腿当差。「只怕今夜要有几颗脑袋搬家，」他一面走一面添上一句；「咱们老爷那只嗅叛逆的鼻子，又磨利了。」御玺大人的这个奴才裹着身上的皮裘直打哆嗦，虽然时令已交四月；因为这位大臣自有他私底下的缘故，惧怕御玺大人那疑心的发作。

长廊上，御玺大人还在同拉斯塞勒斯恳切交谈。

「这份荣耀与特典之职，我交给你，」他说；「因为虽说万事我早已布置停当，可我想，你总比别人更信得过。」

===Original===
Lascelles was to watch for Culpepper, to hasten to Viridus, to attend upon the pair of them as the pilot-fish attendeth upon the ghostly and silent shark, not to leave them till the work was accomplished, or, upon the least sign of treason in Viridus or another, to come hastening as never man hastened, to Privy Seal.

“For,” Cromwell ended, “ye have felt like me how, if this realm is to be saved, saved it shall be by this thing alone.”

Lascelles, who had had no opening to speak, opened now his lips. Great ferreter as he was, he had discovered former servants of the Duchess of Norfolk, that were ready, for consideration of threats, to swear that they had seen the Lady Katharine when a child in her grandmother’s house to be over familiar with one Francis Dearham. He himself had these witnesses earmarked and attainable, and he was upon the point of offering them to Privy Seal. But he recollected that Privy Seal had witnesses enow of his own. Tomorrow was also a day; and the King, if he would not now listen to tales against Kat Howard, might be brought to give ear to those and others added in a year’s time, or when he began to tire of his woman as all men tire of women. Therefore he once more closed his lips. And Cromwell spoke as if his thoughts of a truth jumped together with Lascelles’.

“Sir,” he said, “I would willingly bribe you from the service of his Grace of Canterbury to come into mine. But it may be that I shall not long outlive these days. Therefore I enjoin upon you these things: Serve well your master; guide him, for he needeth guidance, subtly as today ye would have guided him. I will not take you from him for this cause, that there is little need in one house of two that think alike. One sufficeth. For two houses with like minds are stronger than one that is bicephalous. Therefore serve you well Cranmer as in my day I served well the great Cardinal; so at his death, even as I at Wolsey’s, ye may rise very high.”

He went swiftly into his little cabinet, and returning, had in his hand a little book.

“Read well in this,” he said, “where much I have read. You shall see in it mine own annotations. This is *Il Principe* of Macchiavelli; there is none other book like it in the world. Study of it well: read it upon your walks. I am a simple man, yet hath it made me.”
===Chinese===
拉斯塞勒斯的差使是：盯住卡尔佩珀的来路，飞报维里杜斯，随后贴身伺候这两人，如同领航鱼（pilot-fish）伺候那鬼魅般悄无声息的鲨鱼；差事未了，不得离身；而维里杜斯或旁人但凡露出一丝叛逆的苗头，便要飞奔回御玺大人跟前——天下没人跑得那样快。

「因为，」克伦威尔收尾道，「你也同我一样有此一感：这江山若得保全，必定单单靠这一桩事保全。」

拉斯塞勒斯一直没得开口的空隙，这时才张了嘴。他纵然是个搜罗密闻的老手，早已寻出诺福克公爵夫人（Duchess of Norfolk）府里从前的几个仆人——只要威逼一番、许些好处，他们便肯起誓，说亲眼见过凯瑟琳小姐幼年在她祖母宅中，同一个名叫弗朗西斯·迪尔汉姆（Francis Dearham）的人过于亲昵。这几个人证，他早已打点妥帖、随传随到，正要把他们献到御玺大人面前。可他转念一想，御玺大人自己的人证尽够了。明日也还是日子；国王此刻若不肯听构陷凯特·霍华德的话，过一年半载，待他像天下男人厌倦女人那样厌倦了他的女人，自然肯侧耳来听这些，外加旁的新账。于是他重又闭上了嘴。而克伦威尔开了口，倒像是他的心思果真同拉斯塞勒斯的不谋而合。

「先生，」他说，「我本情愿出重金把你从坎特伯雷大人麾下挖过来，归我麾下。只是兴许，我这条命熬不过这些日子了。所以我把这几句话交托给你：好好伺奉你的主上；指引他——他是需要指引的——就像今天你本要指引他的那样，做得精微不着痕迹。我不为这个缘故把你从他身边夺走：同一栋屋子里，心思相同的两个人，用处不大。一个尽够了。两栋屋子若同心，胜过一栋长着两个脑袋的屋子。所以你好生伺奉克兰麦，一如我当年好生伺奉那位伟大的枢机；那么到他撒手的那一天，你也便能像我于沃尔西（Wolsey）死后那样，青云直上。」

他快步走进那间小小的私室，转回来时，手里多了一本小书。

「好生读这部书，」他说，「我在里面读过的东西多得很。你会看到我亲笔的批注。这是马基雅维利的《君主论》（*Il Principe*）；普天之下没有第二部书能同它相比。细细研读：散步时也带在身边读。我本是个简单人，却是它造就了我。」

===Original===
Shadows were falling into the gallery, for the descending sun had come behind the dark, tall elms beyond the river.

“Upon my faith,” Cromwell said, “and as I hope to enter into Paradise by the aid of Christ the King that commended faithful servants, I tell you I had great joy when you told me this woman’s cousin had come into these parts. But greater joy than any were mine could I discern in this land a disciple that could carry on my work. As yet I have seen none; yet ponder well upon this book. God may work in thee, as in me, great changes by its study. … Get you gone.”

He continued long to pace the gallery, his hands behind his back, his cap pulled over his narrow eyes; it grew dusk so that his figure could scarce be seen where it was at the further end. He looked from the casement up into the moon, small and tenuous in the pale western skies. He had been going over in his mind the details of how he had commanded Culpepper to be brought before the King. And at the last when he considered again that Culpepper might well strike his cousin dead at his feet, and that then she would have no tongue to stand against calumnies withal, he uttered the words:

“I think I hold them.”

And, pondering upon the wonderful destiny that had brought him up from a trooper in Italy to these high places, he saluted the moon with his crooked forefinger—for the moon was the president at his birth.
===Chinese===
长廊里影子渐渐浓了，因为西沉的太阳已落到河对岸那些黑黢黢的高榆树背后去了。

「我凭我的信德起誓，」克伦威尔说，「也凭我指望靠基督我王的恩助进入天堂——他曾嘉许过忠心的仆人——我对你说：你来告诉我这女人的表兄已到这一带时，我心里的欢喜着实不小。可若能在这国土上看出一个能承续我事业的门徒，那才是天大的欢喜。至今我还没有见着；但你要把这部书放在心里细细掂量。上帝或能在你身上，如同在我身上，借这部书的研读成就大变化。……你去吧。」

他久久在长廊里踱步，两手负在背后，帽子低低压住一双细长的眼睛；暮色四合，他走在长廊尽头，身影已几乎望不见了。他从窗棂间仰望月亮——在那苍白的天西挂着，小小的，淡淡的。他心里正把如何吩咐人把卡尔佩珀提到国王面前的那一套细节翻来覆去。末了，他重又想起卡尔佩珀很可能当着他的面把他表妹一剑刺死，那时她便再没有舌头来同诽谤分辩，于是他吐出了这样一句话：

「我想，我把他们攥在手心里了。」

他思忖着那趟奇异的命运——它把一个在意大利当过骑兵的汉子，一路提携到这般高位——随后伸出弯曲的食指，向月亮致意；因为月亮正是主持他降生的主辰。

===Original===
“Why,” he uttered aloud, “I have survived four queens’ days.”

For Katharine of Aragon he had seen die; and Anne Boleyn had died on the scaffold; and Jane Seymour was dead in childbed; and now, with the news from Cleves, Anne’s reign was over and done with.

“Four queens,” he repeated.

And, turning swiftly to the door, he commanded that Throckmorton be sent him at once when he came to the archway.
===Chinese===
「嘿，」他高声说道，「四位王后的日子，我都一一熬过来了。」

阿拉贡的凯瑟琳（Katharine of Aragon），他是眼看着死去的；安妮·博林（Anne Boleyn）死在断头台上；简·西摩（Jane Seymour）死在产褥之上；而如今，随着克莱夫斯传来的消息，安妮的朝代也已曲终人散。

「四位王后，」他重复道。

随即他霍地转身向着房门，传下话去：斯罗克莫顿一到拱门，即刻带他来见。

### I / 一

===Original===
In the great place of Smithfield, towards noon, Thomas Culpepper sat his horse on the outskirts of the crowd. By his side Hogben, the gatewarden, had much ado to hold his pikestaff across his horse's crupper in the thick of the people.

The pavement of heads filled the place—bare some of them, some of them covered, according as their owners had cast their caps on high for joy at the Bishop of Worcester's words against the Papist that was to be burned, or as they pressed their thumbs harder down in disfavour and waited to shew their joy at the hanging of the three Protestants that should follow. In the centre towered on high a great gallows from which depended a chain; and at the end of the chain, half-hidden by the people, but shewing his shoulders and his head, a man in a friar's cowl. And, towering as high as the gallows, painted green as to its coat and limbs, but gilt in the helmet and brandishing a great spear, was the image called David Darvel Gatheren that the Papist Welsh adored. This image had been brought there that, in its burning, it might consume the friar Forest. It gazed, red-cheeked and wooden, across the sunlight space at the pulpit of the Bishop of Worcester in his white cassock and black hat, waving his white arms and exhorting the man in the gallows to repent at the last moment. Some words of Latimer might now and again be heard; the chained friar stood upon the rungs of a ladder set against the gallows post; he hung down his head and shook it, but no word could be heard to come from his lips.

“Damnable heretic and foul traitor!” Latimer's urgings came across the sea of heads. “Here sitteth his Majesty's council—” At these words went up a little buzz of question, but sufficient from all that great crowd to send as it were a wind that blew away the Bishop's words. For the style “his Majesty” was so new to the land that people were questioning what new council this might be, or what lord's whose style they did not know. Latimer waved his arm behind him, half turning, to indicate the King's men. These ministers, bravely bonneted so that the jewels sparkled, habited in brown so that the red cloth covering their tiers of seats shewed between their arms and shoulders, sat, like a gay bank of flowers above the lake of heads, surrounded by many other lords and ladies in shining colours. They sat there ready to sign the pardon that was prepared if the friar would be moved by fear or by the Bishop's argument to hang his head and recant.

===Chinese===
走向晌午，在史密斯菲尔德（Smithfield）那片大场子上，托马斯·卡尔佩珀（Thomas Culpepper）骑着马停在人群的外缘。他身旁，守门吏霍格本（Hogben）在稠人密攒之中好不费力，才把长戟横按在马屁股上。

满场铺着一片人头的路面——有的头光着，有的头罩着——全凭各人的心地：有的听了伍斯特主教（Bishop of Worcester）斥骂那将受火刑的旧教徒的言论，满心欢喜，把帽子抛上了天；有的不以为然，把大拇指压得更紧，只等着随后绞死那三个新教徒时，再亮出他们的欢欣。场子中央高耸着一座大绞架，垂下一条铁链；链子头上，半藏在人堆后面、只露出肩膀和头颅的，是个身穿修士兜帽袍的汉子。另有一尊偶像，高与绞架齐，袍服四肢涂作绿色，头盔却是鎏金的，手中挥舞一杆大矛，名字唤作大卫·达维尔·加瑟伦（David Darvel Gatheren），为旧教威尔士人所崇奉。把这尊像抬到此处，为的是放火焚烧它时，好教它把福雷斯特修士（Friar Forest）一并烧化。那偶像红着两颊，木然呆立，隔着一片阳光照临的空场，直望着伍斯特主教的讲道坛：主教一身白色法袍，头戴黑帽，挥舞着两条白臂，劝那绞架上的人在最后时刻悔罪。拉蒂默（Latimer）的片言只语时或可闻；那戴着锁链的修士立在一架靠住绞架柱的梯级上，垂着头颅摇了又摇，却听不见他唇边吐出半个字。

「该下地狱的异端，卑污的叛徒！」拉蒂默的劝诫隔着人头的海洋传来。「当今陛下的枢密重臣，就在此地列座——」这话一出，人群里起了一阵疑问的嗡嗡声，然而单凭这偌大人海，这点嗡嗡声便已汇成一阵风，把主教的话音吹散了。只因「陛下」这称衔在国中还太新，人人都在犯疑：这许是什么新设的枢密会议？「陛下」又算哪位老爷的名号？他们竟无从知道。拉蒂默半转过身，朝身后挥臂，指点国王的众臣。这一班大臣，帽冠华美，宝石生辉；身着褐衣，两臂两肩之间，露出座席上蒙着的红呢。他们坐在那里，宛如人头湖泊上缘一带明艳的花丛，四周环列着许多衣彩鲜亮的勋贵命妇。他们坐在那儿，专候签署那道预备下的赦书——只消那修士慑于刑威，或为主教的辩才所动，肯垂首悔过、撤销前言。

===Original===
The friar, truly, hung his head, clung to the rungs of the ladder, trembled so that all men might see, and once caught furiously at the iron chain and shook it; but no word came from his lips. Culpepper was bursting with pride and satisfaction because he was a made man and would have all the world to know it. He swung his green bonnet round his red head and called for huzzays when the friar shewed fear. Hogben called for huzzays for Squahre Tom of Lincoln, and many men cheered. But the silence dropped again, and the Bishop's words, raised now very high, dominated the sunlight and eddied around the tall faces of the house fronts behind.

“Here have sat the nobles of the realm and the King's Majesty's most honourable council only to have granted pardon to you, wretched creature, if but some spark of repentance would have happened in ye.” Hanging his cowled poll beneath the beam that reached gigantic and black across the crowd, the friar shook his head slowly. “Declared to you your errors I have,” cried Latimer. “Openly and manifestly by the scriptures of God, with many and godly exhortations have I moved you to repentance. Yet will you neither hear nor speak—”

“Bones of St. Nairn!” Culpepper cried; “here is too much speaking and no work. Huzzay! 'e caitiffs. Burn. Burn. Burn. For the honour of England.” And, starting from his figure at the verge of the crowd, cries went up of “Huzzay!” of “Burn!” and “St. George for London!” and unquiet rumours and struggles and waving in the crowd of heads, so that the Bishop's voice was not heard any more that day.

===Chinese===
那修士果然垂下头去，紧攀梯级，浑身颤抖，教人人都看得见；还一度狂怒地抓住铁链摇撼；可他唇边终没有一个字。卡尔佩珀却是满怀骄矜、得意欲狂：他如今是成了体面的人物，还恨不得普天下都知道这一点。修士一露怯，他便抡着绿帽绕着他那颗红脑袋挥舞，高叫众人欢呼。霍格本却喊人为林肯的方汤姆叫好，许多人跟着起哄。可寂静重又垂落，主教把嗓音抬到极高，压过了满场日色，在身后高耸的房屋立面上盘旋回荡。

「全国的勋贵，国王陛下最尊荣的枢密重臣，亲临此地坐下，只为要赦免你——你这可哀的受造之物——只要你身上肯生出一星悔意。」修士把兜帽下的头颅垂在横梁底下——那横梁黑沉沉、庞然地横跨人海之上——缓缓地摇。「你的种种错谬，我已向你指明，」拉蒂默高呼。「凭着上帝的圣经，公开昭然；又用许多敬虔的劝诫，劝你悔改。可你偏是不听，也不开口——」

「圣奈恩的圣骨在上！」卡尔佩珀大叫，「净是空话，不见行事。好哇！你们这些贱骨头。烧。烧。烧。为了英格兰的荣誉。」他这一嗓子自人群边缘炸开，四下里便腾起了「好哇！」「烧！」「圣乔治保佑伦敦！」的喊声，人海中人头攒动、推挤骚乱、纷纷摇荡，那天主教的话再没有人听见了。

===Original===
But through the crowd a silence fell as the image slowly and totteringly moved forward, ankle deep only in the crowd. Ropes from the figure's neck ran out and tightened—some among the crowd began to sing the song against Welsh Papists that ran—

> “David Darvel Gatheren

 As sayeth the Welshmen

 Fetched outlaws out of hell!”

and the burden of it rose so loud that the image swayed over and fell unheard. At that too a silence fell, and presently there came the sound of axes chopping. The friar, swaying on his ladder, looked down and then made a great sign of the cross. The Bishop in his pulpit, raising his white arms in horror and imprecation, seemed to be giving the signal for new uproars.

===Chinese===
可人海中忽然静了下来：那尊偶像正蹒跚着缓缓向前挪动，在人堆里不过没到脚踝。系在偶像颈上的绳索牵直了、绷紧了——人群中有人唱起那支骂威尔士旧教徒的歌，歌词道——

> 「大卫·达维尔·加瑟伦，

> 威尔士人都这样唱——

> 从地狱里勾出亡命徒来！」

副歌的声浪轰轰腾起，那偶像便晃晃悠悠倒了下去，坠地的声息全教歌声吞没了。这一倒，人群又静了片刻，跟着便响起斧头砍斫之声。修士在梯级上摇晃着，低头望了一眼，随即划了一个大大的十字。讲道坛上的主教高高举起两条白臂，惊骇交加、连声诅咒，倒像是在发号令，招来又一轮的喧腾。

===Original===
Whilst he shouted with delight, Culpepper felt a man catch at his leg. He kicked his foot loose, but his hand on the bridle was clutched. There was a fair man at his horse's shoulder that bore Privy Seal's lion badge upon his chest. His face was upturned, and in the clamour he spoke indistinguishable words. Culpepper struck towards the mouth with his fist; the man shrank back, but stood, nevertheless, close still in the crowd. When the silence fell again, Culpepper could hear amongst the swift chopping of the axes the words—

“I rede ye ride swiftly to Hampton. I am the Lord Cromwell's man.”

Culpepper brought his excited mind from the thought of the burning and the joy of the day, with its crowd and its odour of men, and sunshine and tumult.

“Ye say? swine,” he shouted. “Come aside!” He caught at the man's collar and kicked his horse and pulled at its jaws till it drew them out of the thin crowd to a street's opening.

“Sir,” the man said—he had a goodly cloth suit of dark green that spoke to his being of weight in some household—“ye are like to lose your farms at Bromley an ye hasten not to Master Viridus, who holdeth the deedings to you.”

Culpepper uttered an inarticulate roar and smote his patient horse on the side of the head for two minutes of fierce blows, digging with his heels into the girthings.

===Chinese===
卡尔佩珀正喊得起劲，忽觉一个人揪住了他的腿。他一脚踢脱，可握着缰绳的手又被人攥住。马肩旁站着个眉目清朗的汉子，胸前佩着御玺大人（Privy Seal）的狮子徽记。他仰着一张脸，在喧嚣里说的话字字听不分明。卡尔佩珀朝那张嘴挥拳打去；那汉子缩身躲过，却仍旧站在人丛里，贴得紧紧的。待喧嚣再静下来，卡尔佩珀才在斧子急促的斫击声里听清了那句话——

「小人劝您快马赶去汉普顿（Hampton）。小人是克伦威尔（Cromwell）勋爵手下的人。」

卡尔佩珀把那颗兴奋的头脑，从火刑的念头、从这一天的欢腾里——人潮、人的汗气、日光与喧嚷——拽了回来。

「你说什么？蠢猪，」他吼道。「到一边去！」他一把揪住那汉子的领口，踢马夹腿，硬扯着马口嚼子，直把两人从稀落的人丛里拖到街口。

「先生，」那汉子说——他一身墨绿的体面呢服，看得出是哪家府里有些分量的人——「您若不赶紧去见维里杜斯（Viridus）先生，布罗姆利（Bromley）的田庄您只怕就要丢了。田契文书都在他手里攥着呢。」

卡尔佩珀迸出一声不成语言的咆哮，抡起拳头朝那匹耐性的马的头侧一顿猛擂，足足捶了两分钟，鞋跟死命往马肚带里蹬。

===Original===
“Sir,” the man said again, “some lord will have these lands an ye come not to Hampton ere six of the clock. I know not the way of it that be a servant. But Master Viridus sent me with this message.”

Already a thin swirl of blue smoke was ascending past the friar's figure to the bright sky; it caressed the beam of the gallows and Culpepper's bloodshot eye pursued it upwards.

“Before God!” he muttered, “I was set to see this burning. Ye have seen many; I never a one.” A new spasm of rage caught him: he dragged at his horse's head, and shouting, “Gallop! gallop!” set off into the dark streets, his crony behind his back.

In the Poultry he knocked over a man in a red coat that had a gold chain about his neck; on the Chepe he jumped his horse across a pigman's booth—it brought down Hogben, horse and pike; three drunken men were fighting in Paternoster Street—Culpepper charged above their bodies; but very shortly he came through Temple Bar and was in the marshes and fields. Well out between the hedgerows he was aware that one galloped behind him. He drew a violent rein where the Cow Brook crossed the deep muddied road and looked back.

“Sir,” he called, “this night I will hold a mouse on a chain above a coal fire. So I will see a burning, and my cousin Kat shall see it with me.” He spurred on again.

===Chinese===
「先生，」那汉子又说，「您若不在六点钟前赶到汉普顿，这田地便要归了不知哪位老爷。小人是个当差的，里头的门道小人不懂得。只是维里杜斯先生差小人来传这个话。」

这时已有一缕青蓝的细烟，贴着修士的身影袅袅升向明净的天空；烟丝爱抚着绞架的横梁，卡尔佩珀一双充血的眼睛追着它直望上去。

「上帝在上！」他咕哝道，「我铁了心要来看这场火刑。你看过的多了；我一回也没看过。」一腔怒火又发作起来：他猛拽马头，口里喊着「飞跑！飞跑！」，冲进黑黢黢的街市，他的伙伴紧跟在后。

在家禽街（the Poultry），他撞翻了一个红衣金链的汉子；到了齐普赛街（the Chepe），他纵马从猪贩子的摊棚上跃过——把霍格本连人带马带戟都掀翻了；帕特诺斯特街（Paternoster Street）上三个醉汉正斗殴——卡尔佩珀贴着他们的身子冲了过去；不多时他已穿过圣殿栅门（Temple Bar），到了泽野田间。行到树篱夹道深处，他察觉身后有人纵马追来。牛溪（Cow Brook）横过泥泞深路的地方，他狠狠一勒缰绳，回头望去。

「先生，」他喊道，「今晚俺要把一只耗子拴在链子上，吊在炭火上方。这么着俺也看一回火烧，还教俺的表妹凯特陪俺一块儿看。」说罢又加鞭赶路。

===Original===
By the time he was come to Brentford four men, habited like the first, rode behind him. When he stayed to let his horse drink from the river opposite Richmond Hill, he was aware that across the stream a pageant with sweet music marched a little beyond the further bank. He could see the tops of pikes and pennons amid the tree trunks.

He muttered that such a pageant he would very soon make for himself; for, filled with the elation of his new magnificence, since Privy Seal was his friend and Viridus was earnest to do him favour, he imagined that no captain nor lord in that land soon should overpass him. For that any lord should desire his new lands troubled him little; only he hastened to cut that lord's throat and to kiss his cousin Kat.

It was a quarter before six when he drew rein in the green yard that lay before the King's arch in Hampton. There befell the strangest scuffle there; flaring for a moment and gone out like the gunpowder they sometimes lit in saucers for sport. A man called Lascelles came slowly from under the arch to meet him, and then, running over the green grass from the little side door, came the young Poins in red breeches, pulling off a red coat that he had had but half the time to don and tugging at his sword whose hilt was caught in the sleeve hole. Even as he issued, Lascelles, walking slowly, began to run and to call. Four other men of Privy Seal's ran from under the arch, and the four men that had followed behind him so far, closed their horses round his. The boy had his sword out and his coat gave as he ran. Lascelles closed near him on the grass, stretched out a foot to trip, and the boy lay sprawling, his hands stretched out, his sword three yards before him. The four men that had run from the arch had him up upon his feet and held his arms when Culpepper had ridden the hundred yards from the gate to them.

“Why,” said Culpepper, gazing upon the boy's face, “it was thee wouldst have my farms.” He spat in the boy's face and rode complacently under the archway where were many men of Privy Seal's in the side chambers and on the steps that ran steeply to the King's new hall.

===Chinese===
到布伦特福德（Brentford）时，已有四个与头一个同样装束的人骑马缀在他身后。他在河畔停下，让马饮水——那里正对着里士满山（Richmond Hill）——只见溪流那一侧，一队华彩的仪仗伴着悦耳的乐声，正行进在远岸之外不远处。树干之间，望得见矛杆梢头与燕尾旗的影子。

他咕哝着，说这样的仪仗他很快也要给自己置办一份；他新得了这份荣华，得意得发飘，自想御玺大人既与他相熟，维里杜斯又一心要提携他，这国里没有哪个统领、哪位勋贵，不久还能压他一头。哪位老爷若想要他那几处新田庄，他毫不在意——只消赶紧割了那老爷的喉咙，再去亲亲他表妹凯特便是。

五点三刻，他勒住缰绳，停在汉普顿御前国王门拱前那片绿茵院子里。那里起了一场顶古怪的扭打，火光似的一闪便灭，倒像人们有时盛在碟子里点着玩的火药。一个唤作拉塞尔斯（Lascelles）的男子从门洞下缓缓出来迎他；跟着，小庞兹（the young Poins）穿着红裤子，从侧边小门奔过绿草而来，一面扒那件仓促间只来得及穿上一半的红上衣，一面拽他的剑，剑柄却卡在袖孔里。他刚一冒头，缓步而行的拉塞尔斯便拔腿跑起来，高声呼喝。门洞下又窜出御玺大人手下的四个人；而一路尾随他到此的那四个人，也将马匹围拢到他马的四近。那小子已把剑拔在手，跑动间上衣绷裂了。拉塞尔斯在草地上逼近，伸出一脚去绊，那小子便扑倒在地，双臂前伸，剑甩在身前三码开外。卡尔佩珀骑完从大门到那儿的一百码赶到时，从门洞跑出的那四个人已把他架起来站定，架住了他的双臂。

「哦嗬，」卡尔佩珀端详着那小子的脸，「原来想要我田庄的是你。」他朝那小子脸上啐了一口，扬扬得意地骑进门洞。门洞里，偏房中、陡直通往国王新厅的台阶上，到处是御玺大人的人。

===Original===
“I do conceive now,” Culpepper, in descending from his horse, spoke to Lascelles, “wherefore that knave would have had me stay in Calais and be warder of barges. 'A would have my lands here.”

Word was given him that he must without delay go to the Sieur Viridus, and in a high good humour he followed the lead of Lascelles through the rabbit warren of small and new passages of the palace. In them it was already nearly dark.

It was in that way that, landing at the barge stage, a little stiff with the cold of his barge journey, Throckmorton came upon the young Poins in his scarlet breeches, his face cut and bleeding in his contact with the earth, his sword gone. Privy Seal's men that had fallen upon him had kicked him out of the palace gates. They had no warrant yet to take him; the quarrel was none of theirs. The boy was of the King's Guard, it was true, but his company lay then at the Tower.

Throckmorton cursed at him when he heard his news; and when he heard that Culpepper was then in the palace where window lights already shone before him, he ran to the archway. He had no time for reflection save as he ran. Word was given him in the archway itself that Privy Seal would see him instantly and with great haste and urgency. He asked only for news where Thomas Culpepper was, and ran, upon the disastrous hearing that Viridus had taken him up the privy stairway. And, in that darkness, thoughts ran in his head. Disaster was here. But what? Privy Seal called for him. He had no time for Privy Seal. Culpepper was gone to Kat Howard's room. Viridus there had taken him. There was no other room up the winding staircase to which he could go. Here was disaster! For whether he stayed Culpepper or no, Privy Seal must know that he had betrayed him. As he ran swiftly the desperate alternative coursed in his mind. Rich, the Chancellor of the Augmentations, and he had their tale pat, that Privy Seal was secretly raising the realm against the King. He himself had got good matter that morning listening to the treasonable talking of the printer Badge.

Several men in the stair angle would have stopped him when at last he was at foot of the winding stairs. He whispered:

“I be Throckmorton upon my master's business,” and was through and in the darkness of the stairway.

===Chinese===
「我如今可明白了，」卡尔佩珀下马时对拉塞尔斯说，「那泼才为什么一心要留我在加莱做驳船的看守。他是存心要吞我这里的田地。」

有人传话与他，教他即刻去见维里杜斯先生。他兴高采烈，跟着拉塞尔斯引路，穿行过王宫里那座兔窟般纵横窄小的新廊道。廊道之中，天色已近乎黑了。

也就在这时辰，斯罗克莫顿（Throckmorton）在驳船码头上了岸——坐船一路江风，周身冻得有些发僵——正撞见小庞兹穿着猩红裤子，脸上带着扑地时磕破的血痕，剑也没了。扑上去的御玺大人那班人把他踢出了宫门。他们尚无拘拿他的凭据；这场干系本与他们无涉。这小子诚然是国王卫队的人，可他那队人马其时正驻在伦敦塔。

斯罗克莫顿听了他的消息便咒骂起来；又听说卡尔佩珀此刻已在宫里——他眼前宫窗的灯火已经亮成一片——便向门洞奔去。他来不及细想，只能边跑边思量。就在门洞里，有人传下话来：御玺大人即刻便要见他，事急如火。他只打听了一句托马斯·卡尔佩珀的下落，便又奔跑起来——听得维里杜斯已领他上了密梯，这凶信不啻灭顶。黑暗之中，念头在他头脑里飞转。大祸临头了。可是什么祸？御玺大人召见他。他哪有工夫去见御玺大人。卡尔佩珀去了凯特·霍华德的房间。维里杜斯在那儿把他截住了。盘梯上去再没有别的房间可去。大祸就在眼前！因为无论他拦得住拦不住卡尔佩珀，御玺大人总归要知道他背叛了自己。他疾奔之间，那孤注一掷的盘算在心里奔涌：增益法院庭长（Chancellor of the Augmentations）里奇（Rich）同他两个，那套说辞早已背得滚瓜烂熟——御玺大人正暗中鼓动举国反叛国王。就在今天早晨，他偷听印书商巴奇（Badge）谋逆的言论，还捞到了极好的把柄。

待他终于奔到盘梯脚下，梯角处有几个人起身拦他。他耳语道：

「我是斯罗克莫顿，替我家主人办差，」人已挤过身去，没入梯道的黑暗之中。

===Original===
Why was there no cresset? Why were there these men? It came into his mind that already the King had heard Culpepper. Already Katharine was arrested. He groaned as he mounted the stairs. For in that case, with those men behind him, he was in a gaol already. He paused to go back; then it came to him that, if he could win forward and find the King, who alone, by giving ear, could save him, he would yet not know first how Katharine had fared. He had a great stabbing at his heart with that thought, and once more mounted.

From the door next hers there streamed a light. Hers was closed. He ran to it and knocked, leaning his head against the panels to listen. There was no sound, no sound at all when he knocked again. It was intolerable. He thrust the door open. No woman was there and no man. He went in. He thought: “If the room be in disorder—”

He made out in the twilight that the room stood as always; the chair loomed where it should; there was a spark on the hearth; the books were ordered on the table; no stool was overturned. He stood amid these things, his heart beating tumultuously, his ears pricked up, stilling his breathing to listen, in the blue twilight, like a wild beast.

===Chinese===
为什么没有火盆灯？为什么有这些人？他心里蓦地闪过一个念头：国王兴许已经听过卡尔佩珀的话了。凯瑟琳兴许已经被捕了。他上楼时呻吟了一声。因为果真如此，身后又有那些人堵着，他早已身在牢狱之中了。他停步想退回去；可转念又想，就算他能闯上去找到国王——唯有国王肯俯听，才能救他——他头一件也无从知道凯瑟琳究竟怎样了。这念头像一把利刃扎进他心口，他重又向上攀去。

她邻室的门里透出灯光；她自己那扇门关着。他奔过去敲门，把头抵在门板上谛听。没有声音；再敲一遍，依然死寂。这叫人怎能忍受。他猛力推门。屋里既没有女人，也没有男人。他走进去。他心想：「这屋子若是凌乱不堪——」

昏暝之中他辨出，屋里的一切都还在原处：椅子端然在该在的地方隐隐立着；炉床上剩着一点火星；书在桌上排列齐整；没有一张凳子翻倒。他立在这些物事中间，心跳如擂，双耳直竖，屏住呼吸谛听，在青蓝的暝色里，宛如一头野兽。

===Original===
A voice said:

“Body o' God! Throckmorton!” beneath its breath, the light of the next door grew large and smaller again; he caught from there the words: “It is Throckmorton.” And at the sound Throckmorton loosened his dagger in its sheath. Some glimmering of the plan reached him; they were awaiting Katharine's coming, and a great load fell from his mind. She was not yet taken.

He paused to stroke his beard for fear it was disordered, pulled from over his shoulder the medallion on the chain; it had flown there as he ran. He pushed ajar the next door a minute later, having thought many thoughts and appearing stately and calm.

He replaced the door at its exact angle and gazed at the three silent men. Thomas Culpepper, his brows knotted, his lips moving, was holding his head askew to see the measurements upon a map of his farm at Bromley. That Lascelles had gone out and come back saying that one Throckmorton was in the next room was nothing to him. The next room was nothing to him; he was there to hear of his farms.

Viridus, silent, dark and enigmatic, gazed at a spot upon the table; Lascelles, his mouth a little open, his eyes dilated, had his hands upon it.

Without speaking, Throckmorton noted that the room was empty save for the table and benches; the hangings had been taken down; all the furnishings were gone. That morning the room had been well filled, warm, and in the occupancy of the Lady Deedes. Therefore Cromwell had worked this change. No other had this power. They waited, then, those three, for the coming of Katharine Howard or the King. Lascelles shewed fear and surprise at his being there; therefore Lascelles was deeply concerned in this matter. Lascelles was in the service of Cranmer that morning; now he sat there. Thus he, too, for certain, was in this plan; he was a new servant to Privy Seal—and new servants are zealous. With Viridus he had had some talk of events. Therefore Lascelles was the greatest danger.

===Chinese===
一个声音说道：

「上帝的圣体！斯罗克莫顿！」那声音压着嗓子；隔壁门里的灯光涨大了，又收小了；他从那边捕得几个字：「是斯罗克莫顿。」一听这声，斯罗克莫顿便把匕首在鞘里松了松。那盘算透给他些许微光：原来他们在等凯瑟琳到来。他心上顿时卸下千斤重担。她还没有被抓走。

他停了一停，捋捋胡子，怕它乱了；又把挂在链上的徽章从肩后拽回胸前——奔跑时它甩到了背后。一分钟后，诸多念头在心中理定，他显出一副雍容沉静的气度，把隔壁那扇门轻轻推开一条缝。

他把门掩回原先的角度，凝望着那三个缄默的人。托马斯·卡尔佩珀眉头拧结，嘴唇翕动，歪着头在辨认他布罗姆利田庄图上的丈量数字。拉塞尔斯出去过一趟又回来，说隔壁屋里有个叫斯罗克莫顿的——这话于他如同无物。隔壁的房间于他毫无干系；他来此是为了听他的田庄。

维里杜斯沉默、幽沉、莫测高深，眼睛钉在桌面上的一点；拉塞尔斯嘴巴微张，双目圆睁，两手按在桌上。

不动声色之间，斯罗克莫顿看在眼里：这屋子已搬空了，只剩一张桌子和几条板凳；帷幔拆了；一切陈设都不见了。就在今天早晨，这屋子还是满腾腾暖烘烘的，是迪兹夫人（the Lady Deedes）的居处。那么这场变故是克伦威尔的手笔。别人没有这个权柄。这么说，他们这三个是在此等候凯瑟琳·霍华德（Katharine Howard）或是国王驾到。拉塞尔斯见他在此，又惧又诧；可见拉塞尔斯与此事干系极深。今天早晨拉塞尔斯还在为克兰麦（Cranmer）办事；这会儿却坐在这里。那么他必定也参与了这盘算；他是御玺大人新收的仆人——新仆人总是分外卖力。他又曾同维里杜斯议论过时局。如此说来，拉塞尔斯便是最大的危险。

===Original===
Throckmorton moved slowly behind Culpepper and sat down beside him; in his left hand he had his small dagger, its blue blade protruding from the ham; Culpepper beside him was at his right. He said very softly in Italian to Lascelles:

“Both your hands are upon the table; if you move one my dagger pierces your eye to the brain. So also if you speak in the English language.”

Lascelles muttered: “Judas! *Traditore!*” Viridus sat motionless, and Culpepper moved his finger across the plan of the farm.

“Here is the mixen,” he appealed to Viridus, who nodded.

It was as if Throckmorton, with his slow manner and low voice, was a friend who had come in to speak to Lascelles about the weather or the burnings. He was no concern of Culpepper's, nor was Lascelles who had spoken no word at all.

===Chinese===
斯罗克莫顿缓缓从卡尔佩珀身后绕过，在他身旁坐下；左手攥着那柄小匕首，蓝幽幽的刃锋从腿弯后探出半截；右手边便是卡尔佩珀。他用意大利语对拉塞尔斯柔声说道：

「你的两只手都按在桌上；你若挪动一只，我的匕首便从你眼窝直刺进脑髓。你若用英语开口，也是一样。」

拉塞尔斯从牙缝里挤出一句：「犹大！*Traditore!*（叛徒！）」维里杜斯纹丝不动地坐着，卡尔佩珀的手指在田庄图上移动。

「这儿是粪堆，」他向维里杜斯求证，维里杜斯点了点头。

斯罗克莫顿那副慢条斯理的做派、那副低低的嗓音，倒仿佛是个进来看拉塞尔斯、同他聊聊天气或火刑的朋友。此人于卡尔佩珀毫不相干；那个自始至终一字未发的拉塞尔斯，也与他无涉。

===Original===
Throckmorton kept his head turned towards Lascelles as if he were still addressing him, and spoke in the same level voice, still in Italian.

“Viridus, to thee I speak. This is a very great matter.” Unconsciously he used the set form of words of Privy Seal. “Consider well these things. The day of our master is nigh at an end. Rich, Chancellor of the Augmentations, thy crony and master, and my ally, hath made a plan to go with me to the King this night with witnesses and papers accusing Privy Seal of raising the land against his Highness. Will you join with us, or will you be lost with Privy Seal?”

Viridus kept his eyes upon the same spot of the table.

“Tell me more,” he said. “This matter is very weighty.” His tone was level, monotonous and still. He too might have been saying that the sunshine that day had been long.

===Chinese===
斯罗克莫顿的头仍朝向拉塞尔斯，仿佛还在同他说话，用的却还是意大利语，声调一样平稳。

「维里杜斯，我这句话是对你说的。这是一桩天大的事。」他不自觉地用了御玺大人惯用的断语套式。「这些事你要细细想来。我们主公的大限之期，眼看到头了。增益法院庭长里奇——他是你的靠山、你的主子，又是我的盟友——已定下计划，今夜与我同去觐见国王，带着人证文书，告御玺大人煽动举国反叛他圣上。你是与我们合谋，还是同御玺大人一道覆灭？」

维里杜斯的目光仍不离桌上原来那一点。

「说下去，」他说。「此事非同小可。」他的声调平板、单调、沉静，倒像是在说今天日头照得久。

===Original===
“A fad to talk Latin of ye courtiers,” Culpepper said with uninterested scorn. “Ye will forget God's language of English.” He slapped Throckmorton on the sleeve. “See, what a fine farm I have for my deserts,” he said.

“Ye shall have better,” Throckmorton said. “I have moved the King in your behalf.” But he kept his eyes on Lascelles.

Culpepper cast back his cap from his eyes and leant away the better to slap Throckmorton on the back.

“Ye ha' heard o' my deeds,” he said.

“All England rings with them,” Throckmorton said. He interjected, “Still! hound!” to Lascelles in Italian, and went on to Culpepper: “I ha' moved the King to come this night to thy cousin's room hard by for I knew ye would go to her. The King is hot to speak with thee. Comport thyself as I do bid thee and art a made man indeed.”

Culpepper laughed with hysterical delight.

===Chinese===
「你们这班廷臣，说起拉丁文来成癖，」卡尔佩珀兴味索然地讥诮道。「连上帝用的英文都要忘光了。」他拍拍斯罗克莫顿的衣袖。「瞧，论我的功劳，我得了多好的一处田庄，」他说。

「你还会得更好的，」斯罗克莫顿说。「我已为你在国王面前进过言。」可他的眼睛始终盯着拉塞尔斯。

卡尔佩珀把帽子往后一推，离开眼前，身子更往后靠，好腾出手来拍斯罗克莫顿的背。

「我的事迹，你总听人说起过？」他说。

「全英格兰都传遍了，」斯罗克莫顿说。他用意大利语朝拉塞尔斯插一句：「别动！狗！」又接着对卡尔佩珀说：「我已说动国王，今夜就到你表妹近旁这间屋里来，因为我知道你必去寻她。国王急切想同你说话。你只依我吩咐行事，便是十足十的出头人了。」

卡尔佩珀乐得忘乎所以，哈哈大笑。

===Original===
“By Cock!” he shouted. “Master Viridus, thou art naught to this. Three farms shall not content me nor yet ten.”

Throckmorton's eyes shot a glance at Viridus and back again to Lascelles' face.

“If you speak I slay you,” he said. Lascelles' eyes started from his head, his mouth worked, and on the table his hands jerked convulsively. But Throckmorton had seen that Viridus still sat motionless.

“By Cock!” Culpepper cried. “By Guy and Cock! let me kiss thee.”

“Sir,” Throckmorton said, “I pray you speak no more words, not at all till I bid you speak. I am a very great lord here; you shall observe gravity and decorum or never will I bring you to the King. You are not made for Courts.”

“Oh, I kiss your hands,” Culpepper answered him. “But wherefore have you a dagger?”

===Chinese===
「上帝在上！」他喊道。「维里杜斯先生，这算得了什么。三处田庄满足不了我，十处也嫌少。」

斯罗克莫顿的目光朝维里杜斯一掠，又落回拉塞尔斯脸上。

「你敢出声，我就宰了你，」他说。拉塞尔斯眼珠暴出眶外，嘴唇牵动，按在桌上的两手痉挛地一抽一抽。可斯罗克莫顿看得分明：维里杜斯依旧纹丝不动。

「上帝在上！」卡尔佩珀嚷道。「凭圣盖伊、凭上帝起誓！让我亲亲你。」

「先生，」斯罗克莫顿说，「求你少说几句，不，在我叫你说之前一个字也别说。我在此地是个顶有体面的大人；你须稳重守礼，不然我永不带你见国王。你不是吃朝堂这碗饭的人。」

「嗳嗳，我吻您的手还不成么，」卡尔佩珀回答他。「可你带着匕首做什么？」

===Original===
“Sir,” Throckmorton said again, “I will have you silent, for if the King should pass the door he will be offended by your babble.” He interjected to Viridus, speaking in Italian, “Speak thou to this fool and engage him to think. I can give you no more grounds, but you must quickly decide either to go with Rich the Chancellor and myself or to remain the liege of the Privy Seal.”

Never once did he take his eyes from Lascelles, and the sweat stood upon his forehead. Once when Lascelles moved he slid the dagger along the table with a sharp motion and a gasping of breath, as a pincer pressed to the death will make a faint. Yet his voice neither raised itself nor fell one shade.

“And if I will aid you in this, what reward do I get?” Viridus asked. He too spoke low and unmovedly, keeping his eyes upon the table.

“The one-half of my enrichments for five years, the one-half of those of the Chancellor, and my voice for you with the King and with the new Queen.”

“And if I will not go with you?”

“Then when the King passeth this door I do cry out ‘Treason! treason!’ and you, I, and this man, and this shall tonight sleep in the King's prison, not in Privy Seal's. And I will have you think that I am sib and rib with Kat Howard who shall sway the King if her cousin be induced not to play the beast.”

===Chinese===
「先生，」斯罗克莫顿又说，「我要你噤声，因为国王若打门前经过，你这一张碎嘴要触怒圣驾的。」他又用意大利语向维里杜斯插话：「你去同这个蠢材说话，引着他去想他的事。我没有更多的道理可给你了，但你必须赶快决断：或是随里奇庭长与我同走，或是仍旧做御玺大人的臣仆。」

他的眼睛一次也不曾离开拉塞尔斯，额头沁出汗珠。有一回拉塞尔斯动了一动，他唰地一声把匕首顺着桌面滑过去，带一声倒抽的气音，恰如一把钳螯被压到将死，还会轻轻一颤。可他的声调既不高一分，也不低半度。

「我若助你成此一事，我得什么报酬？」维里杜斯问。他的话也说得又低又平静，眼睛不离桌面。

「五年之内我得了的好处分你一半，庭长所得的一半也归你；我还在国王与新王后跟前替你进言。」

「我若不与你们同走呢？」

「那么国王打这门前走过时，我便高呼『谋逆！谋逆！』，你、我，这位，还有这位，今夜都得睡到国王的监狱里去，不是御玺大人的。你还要明白：我同凯特·霍华德是至亲至契；只要她那表兄能被人劝住不撒野，左右国王圣意的便是她。」

===Original===
Viridus spoke no word; but when Culpepper, idle and gaping, reached out his hand to take the black flagon of wine that was between them under the candles on the table, Viridus stretched forth his hand and clasped the bottle.

“It is not expedient that you drink,” he said.

“Why somever then?” Culpepper asked.

“That neither do you make a beast of yourself if you come before the King's great majesty this night,” Viridus said in his cold and minatory voice, “not yet smell beastly of liquors when you kiss the King his hand.”

Culpepper said:

“By Cock! I had forgot the King's highness.”

===Chinese===
维里杜斯一言不发；只是当卡尔佩珀百无聊赖、张着嘴伸手去够桌上烛台底下他们中间那只黑酒瓶时，维里杜斯却伸过手去，把瓶颈攥住了。

「你此刻喝酒不相宜，」他说。

「那又是什么缘故？」卡尔佩珀问。

「这样，你今夜觐见国王的赫赫天威时，才不会弄出一副醉鬼模样，」维里杜斯用他冷冷的、带着警告意味的嗓音说，「你吻国王圣手的时候，也不至于满身酒腥气。」

卡尔佩珀说：

「上帝在上！我把国王的圣威给忘了。」

===Original===
“See that you kneel before him and speak not; see that you raise your eyes not from the floor nor breathe loudly; see that when the King's high and awful majesty dismisses you you go quietly.” Throckmorton spoke. “See that you speak not with nor of your cousin. For so dreadful is a king, and this King more than others; and so terrible his wrath and desire of worship—and this King's more than others—that if ye speak above a whisper's sound, if ye act other than as a babe before its preceptor's rod, you are cast out utterly and undone. You shall never more have farms nor lands; you shall never more have joyance nor gladness; you shall rot forgotten in a hole as you had never done brave things for the King's grace.”

“By Cock!” Culpepper said, “it seems it is easier to talk of a king than with one.”

“See that you remember it,” Throckmorton said, “for with great trouble have I brought this King so far to talk with you!”

He moved his dagger yet nearer to Lascelles' form and held his finger to his lip. Viridus had never once moved; he stayed now as still as ever. Culpepper crammed his hand over his lips.

===Chinese===
「记住跪在他面前，不许开口；记住两眼不许离了地面，出气不许粗声；记住至高至威的国王陛下恩准你退下时，你要悄没声地退。」这是斯罗克莫顿的声音。「记住不许同你表妹交谈，也不许提起她。为王的何等可畏，这一位尤胜诸王；他的雷霆之怒、他对于崇敬威仪的欲求何等可怖——这一位尤胜诸王——你若说话高过一丝耳语，举止不像婴孩立在塾师戒尺之前，你便被弃绝净尽，一败涂地。田庄地业，再与你无份；欢愉喜乐，再与你无份；你将在一个地窟里烂掉、湮没无闻，仿佛你从来不曾替国王的恩典立过一件壮举。」

「上帝在上！」卡尔佩珀说，「看来议论国王容易，同国王说话倒难。」

「你给我记牢这个，」斯罗克莫顿说，「我费了多少周折，才把这国王请到肯同你说话这一步！」

他把匕首又朝拉塞尔斯那边挪近了些，一手竖指抵唇。维里杜斯自始至终一动未动；此刻仍安然静坐如故。卡尔佩珀拿手死死捂住了自己的嘴。

===Original===
For from without there came the sound of voices and, in that dead silence, the rustle of a woman's gown, swishing and soft. A deep voice uttered heavily:

“Aye, I know your feelings. I have had my sadness.” It paused for a moment, and mouthed on: “I can cap your Lucretius too with ‘*Usque adeo res humanas vis abdita—*’ ” It seemed that for a moment the speaker stayed before the door where all three held their breaths. “I have read more of the Fathers, of late days, than of the writers profane.”

They heard the breathing of a heavy man who had mounted stairs. The voice sounded more faintly:

===Chinese===
因为门外传来了人语声；在那一片死寂里，又有一件女人长裙窸窣轻软的摩挲声。一个深沉的嗓音笨重地吐字：

「是了，我明白你的心境。我自己也有过忧伤。」那声音顿了片刻，又一板一眼地续道：「你引卢克莱修，我也接得上一句——『*Usque adeo res humanas vis abdita—*』」说话的人似乎就在那扇门外停留了片刻，门里三人个个屏息。「近来我读教父的著述，倒比读那些俗世文人多了。」

他们听得一个沉重的身躯攀过楼梯，喘息未定。那嗓音已渐远，更加低微：

===Original===
“Now you have naught further to think of than the goodly words of Ecclesiastes: ‘*Et cognovi quod non esset melius, nisi laetare et. …*’ ” The voice died dead away with the closing of the door. And as a torch passed, Throckmorton knew that the King had waited there whilst light was being made in Katharine's room. He said softly to Viridus:

“Whilst I go unto them you shall hold this dagger against this fool's throat. We gain as many hours as we may hold him from blabbing to Privy Seal. And consider that we must bring to the King Rich and Udal and many other witnesses this night.”

“Throckmorton,” Viridus said, “before thou goest thou shalt satisfy me of many things. I have not yet given myself into thy hands.”
===Chinese===
「如今你们再别无可想，只消想想《传道书》（Ecclesiastes）的美好言语便是：『*Et cognovi quod non esset melius, nisi laetare et. …*』」话音随着关门之声寂然而灭。仿佛一支火把倏然照过，斯罗克莫顿登时明白：方才国王在门外驻足，是等人在凯瑟琳的房里点灯。他低柔地对维里杜斯说：

「我过去那边，你便把这匕首抵在这蠢材的咽喉上。我们能拖他多久不向御玺大人饶舌，便多赢得几个时辰。还要想到：今夜我们须把里奇、乌达尔（Udal）和许多别的人证带到国王面前。」

「斯罗克莫顿，」维里杜斯说，「你走之前，还有许多事须叫我心服。我还没有把自己交到你手里。」

### II / 二

===Original===
A weary sadness had beset Katharine Howard ever since she had knelt before Anne of Cleves at Richmond, and it was of this the King had spoken outside the door whilst they had waited for light to be made.

All Anne’s protesting that willingly she rendered up a distasteful crown could not make Katharine hugely glad with the manner of her own taking it. And, when a messenger, dressed as a yeoman in green, had come into the bright gallery to beg the Queen and that fair lady the Lady Katharine Howard to come a-riding side by side and witness the sports that certain poor yeomen made in the woods upon Thames-side, she felt a sinking in her heart that no Rhenish of the Queen’s could relieve. She desired to be alone and to pray﻿—or to be alone with Henry and speak out her heart and devise how they might atone to the Queen. But she must ride at the Queen’s right hand with the Duke of Suffolk at her left. It was so between their captives that the Caesars had ridden into Rome after the taking of barbaric kings. But she had waged no war.

She did not, in her heart, call shame upon the King; she knew him to be a heavy man with bitter sorrows who must in these violentnesses and brave shows find refuge and surcease; it was her province to endure and to find excuse for him. But to herself she quoted that phrase of Lucretius that the King again repeated: there was a hidden destiny that tamed the shows of the great; and she was the mutest of that throng that upon white horses, all with little flags flying and horns blowing, cantered to see the yeomen shoot. For the ladies and knights, avid of these things, loved above all good bowmanry and wagered with outstretched hands for the marksmen that most they deemed to have skill or that usually seemed to enjoy the fortunate favours of chance and the winds.

===Chinese===
自从在里士满（Richmond）跪见过克莱夫斯的安妮（Anne of Cleves），凯瑟琳·霍华德（Katharine Howard）心头便一直盘踞着一种疲惫的哀伤；方才在门外候着掌灯的当儿，国王说的正是这桩心事。

安妮口口声声说，这顶她本自嫌恶的王冠，她是甘心奉还的；可这总不能教凯瑟琳对自己承受它的方式感到欢畅。后来，一名扮作绿衣侍卫的信使走进明亮的长廊，恭请女王并那位淑媛凯瑟琳·霍华德小姐并辔出游，去观赏泰晤士河畔林间一班穷苦弓手设下的游艺；她心头便一阵往下沉，连女王的莱茵酒也宽解不得。她只想独自一人祈祷——或者同亨利（Henry）独处，把心里的话都倾吐出来，商量他们该怎样向女王补过。可她必须乘骑在女王的右手，左手边是萨福克公爵（Duke of Suffolk）。罗马诸帝降服了蛮邦君王之后，便是这般夹着俘虏凯旋入罗马的。可她并没有兴过刀兵。

她心里并不斥国王可耻；她知道他是个心事沉重、悲苦深重的人，非得在这等暴烈行径、堂皇排场里寻个遁避，讨些止息；忍受并为他开脱，原是她的本分。可她暗自总念着那句国王也一再重提的卢克莱修（Lucretius）的话：有一种隐秘的命运，驯服着伟人们的排场荣光；于是，在那一片人马之中——白马轻骑，小旗招展，号角齐鸣，小跑着去看弓手较射——她成了最沉默的一个。因为夫人们与骑士们于此道兴味最浓，百般技艺里最钟爱神射，个个伸长了手臂争相下注，押他们眼中最有本领的射手，或是押那些似乎素来最得机运与长风垂青的人。

===Original===
But, being alone with the King﻿—(for when the Queen rode back to Richmond the notable bowman in green walked, holding Katharine’s stirrup, back to Hampton at her saddlebow)﻿—she could not stay herself from venting her griefs.

“*Et cognovi quod non esset melius nisi laetari et facere bene in vita sua*”﻿—Henry finished his quotation when they were within her room. He sat himself down in her chair and stretched his legs apart; being tired with his long walk at her saddle bow, the more boisterous part of his great pleasure had left him. He was no more minded to slap his thigh, but he felt, as it was his favourite image of blessedness to desire, like a husbandman who sat beneath his vine and knew his harvesting prosper.

“Body of God!” he said, “this is the best day of my life. There doth no cloud remain. Here is the sunburst. For Cleves hath cut himself adrift; I need have no more truck with Anne; you have no more cause nor power to bend yourself from me; tomorrow the Parliament meets, such a Parliament to do my will as never before met in a Republic; therefore I have no more need of Cromwell.” He snapped his thumb and finger as if he were throwing away a pinch of dust, and when she fell to her knees before his chair, placed his hand upon her head and, smiling, huge and indulgent, spoke on.

===Chinese===
可是等到与国王独处——女王骑马回里士满去了，那位出名的绿衣神射手便牵着凯瑟琳的马镫，在鞍旁步行，一路送她回汉普顿（Hampton）——她再也按捺不住，把满腹的愁苦倾吐出来。

“*Et cognovi quod non esset melius nisi laetari et facere bene in vita sua*（人生在世，莫善于欢乐行善）”——进了她的房间，亨利才把这句引文念完。他在她的椅子上坐下来，叉开两腿；在鞍旁走了这半日的长路，人也乏了，心头大喜里那股喧腾的兴头已经退了。他不再想拍着大腿，只觉得自己——恰如他心目中至福的光景——像个庄稼汉坐在自家的葡萄藤下，眼见收成兴旺。

“上帝的圣体！”他说，“这是我一生里顶好的日子。再没有一片残云了。看哪，日光迸射。克莱夫斯已自断了缆索；我再不必同安妮有什么瓜葛；你也没有了缘由、没有了力量来违拗我；明日国会开会——这样一届惟我旨意是从的国会，自有邦国以来还不曾有过——所以我再不需要克伦威尔（Cromwell）了。”他拇指中指一弹，仿佛弹去一撮灰尘；待她拜倒在椅子跟前，他便把手放在她头上，脸上带着宏大而纵容的笑，说了下去。

===Original===
“This is such a day as seldom I have known since I was a child.” He leaned forward to stroke her dusky and golden hair and laid his hand upon her shoulder, his fingers touching her flushed cheek.

“On other days I have said with Horace, who is more to my taste than your Lucretius: ‘That man is great and happy who at day’s end may say: Today I have lived, what of storms or black clouds on the morrow betide.’ ”﻿ ﻿…

He crossed his great legs encased in green, set his heavy head to one side and, though he could see she was minded to pray to him, continued to speak like a man uttering of his memories.

“Such days as that of Horace I have known. But never yet such a day as today, which, good in itself, leadeth on to goodness and fair prospects for a certain morrow.” He smiled again. “Why, I am no more an old man as I had thought to be. I have walked that far path beside thy horse.” It pleased him for two things: because he had walked with little fatigue and because he had been enabled to show her great and prodigal honour by so serving her for groom. “This too I set to thy account as my good omen.
