And that thou art. No woman shall have such honours as thou in this land, save only the Mother of God.” And, after touching his green and jewelled bonnet, he cast it from his head on to the table.

===Chinese===
“自打孩提时起，这样的日子我也少有遇见。”他俯身抚着她那一头深金色的秀发，手搭上她的肩头，指尖碰着她绯红的面颊。

“别些日子里，我同贺拉斯（Horace）一道说过——贺拉斯比你的卢克莱修更合我的口味：‘那人是伟大而幸福的，他能在一天终了时说：今日我已生活过了，任凭明日来了风暴，来了黑云。’”……

他交叠起裹在绿衣里的长腿，把沉重的头颅歪向一边；虽然看得出她正要求恳他，他仍像追述往事似的说个不停。

“贺拉斯那样的日子，我是遇见过的。可从没有像今天这样的日子——它本身是善的，又引向善，为一个确凿的明日带来美好的指望。”他又笑了。“嗨，我并不像我原以为的那样老。你马旁那条长路，我是走下来了。”这事叫他得意，一则是因为他走得竟不怎样乏，二则是因为他得以这样给她充任马夫，向她表示了盛大而慷慨无度的荣宠。“这一件，我也记在你的账上，当作我的吉兆。而你正是我的吉兆。在这片国土上，除了天主之母，再不会有女子得享你这样的尊荣。”说着，他碰了碰那顶缀宝的绿帽，随手把它从头上丢到桌上。

===Original===
“Sir,” she cried out, and clasping her hands uttered her words in anguish and haste. “Great kings and lords upon their affiancing day have ever had the habit of granting their brides a boon or twain﻿—as the conferring of the revenues of a province, or the pardoning of criminals.”

“Why, an thou come not to me to pardon Privy Seal﻿—” he began.

“Sir,” she cut in on his words, “I crave no pardon for Privy Seal; but let me speak my mind.”

He said tenderly:

“Art in the mood to talk! Talk on! for I know no way to hinder thee.”

===Chinese===
“陛下，”她喊出声来，十指相扣，话里满是焦灼与仓促。“大国王、大领主们在订聘之日，素来有给新娘颁赐一两样恩典的规矩——或是赐一省的岁入，或是赦免罪犯。”

“怎么，你莫不是来求我赦免御玺大人的——”他起了个头。

“陛下，”她截住他的话头，“我不求赦御玺大人；只求容我把心里的话说出来。”

他温柔地说：

“你正有讲话的兴致！讲吧！我横竖拦你不住。”

===Original===
“Sir,” she said, “I ask thee no pardon for Privy Seal, neither his goods ne his life. I maintain this man hath well served thee and is no traitor; but since that he hath ground the faces of the poor, hath made thee to be hated by bringing of false witness, hath made the thirsty earth shrink from drinking of blood, hath cast down the Church﻿—since that this man in this way hath brought peril upon the republic and upon the souls of poor and witless folk, this man hath wrought worse treasons than any that I wot of. If ye will adjudge him to die, I am no fool to say: No!”

Henry wrinkled his brows and said:

“Grinding the faces of the poor is in law no treason. Yet I may not slay him save upon the occasion of treason. I would a man would come to me that could prove him traitor.”

===Chinese===
“陛下，”她说，“我不求赦御玺大人，既不求赦他的家财，也不求赦他的性命。我一直认定这个人忠心事你，并非叛贼；可是，他既把穷人的脸面碾进尘土，既用伪证害得你招人怨恨，既教干渴的大地都畏缩着不敢饮血，既推倒了教会——这个人既这样把危难加于邦国、加于贫弱愚蒙小民的心灵，他犯下的叛逆就比我所知道的任何叛逆都重。你若断他死罪，我也不是傻子，不会说一声‘不行’！”

亨利皱起眉头，说道：

“把穷人的脸面碾进尘土，在律法上算不得叛逆。可若不以叛逆为名，我不能杀他。但愿有个人能到我跟前来，证明他就是叛贼。”

===Original===
Kneeling before the King she grasped each of his knees with one of her hands.

“Sir,” she said, “this is your occasion, none of mine. I would ye would reconcile it to your conscience so to act to him as I would have you, for his injustice to the poor and for his cogged oaths. But yet grant me this: to cog oaths for the downfall of Privy Seal upon the occasion of treason ye must have many other innocents implicated with him; such men as have had no idea, no suspicion, no breath of treason in their hearts. Grant me their lives. Sir, let me tell you a tale that I read in Seneca.” She moved her body nearer to him upon the floor, set her hands upon his two arms and gazed, beseeching and piteous, up into his face.

“Sir,” she said, “you may read it in Seneca for yourself that upon the occasion of Cinna’s treachery being made known to the Emperor Augustus, the Emperor lay at night debating this matter in his mind. For on the one side, says he in words like this: ‘Shall I pardon this man after that he hath assailed my life, my life that I have preserved in so many battles by sea and by land, after I have stablished one single peace throughout the globe into all the corners thereof? Shall he go free who has considered with himself not only to slay me but to slay me when I offered sacrifice, ere its consummation, so that I may be damned as well as slain? Shall I pardon this man?’ And, upon the other side, the Emperor Augustus, lying in the black of the night, being a prince, even as thou art, prone to leniency, said such words as these: ‘Why dost thou, Augustus, live, if it is of import to so many people that thou diest? Shall there never be an end to thy vengeance and thy punishments? Is thy single life of such worth that so much ruin shall forever be wrought to preserve it?’ ”

“Why, I have had these thoughts,” Henry said. “Speak on. What did this Emperor that thought like me?”

“Sir,” Katharine continued, and now she had her hands upon his shoulders, “the Empress Livia his wife lay beside him and was aware of these his night sweats and his anguishes. ‘And the counsels of a woman; shall these be listened to?’ she spoke to him. ‘Do thou in this what the Physicians follow when their accustomed recipes are of no avail to cure. They do try the contrary drugs. By severity thou hast never, sire, profited from the beginning to this very hour that is; Lepidus has followed to death Savidienus; Murena, Lepidus; Caepio followed Murena; Eynatius, Caepio. Commence to essay at this pass how clemency shall act in cure. Cinna is convicted: pardon him. Further to harm thee he hath no power, and it shall forever redound to thy glory.’ ”

===Chinese===
她跪在国王面前，一手搂住他一条腿。

“陛下，”她说，“这原是你的由头，不是我的。我只望你抚着良心，为着他对穷人的不义、为他那些弄假的誓言，照我的心愿去处置他。可是还求你应许我一件事：要以叛逆为名、凭弄假的誓言扳倒御玺大人，就必得有许多别的无辜之人牵连在他一并问罪；那样的人，心里从没有过一点念头、一丝疑心、半缕叛逆的气息。求你饶他们的性命。陛下，容我给你讲一段我在塞涅卡（Seneca）书里读到的故事。”她在地板上把身子朝他挪近，两手搭上他的两条胳膊，仰起脸，满眼哀恳与凄楚地凝望着他。

“陛下，”她说，“你自己也能在塞涅卡书里读到：秦纳（Cinna）谋逆的事传到奥古斯都大帝耳中之后，皇帝夜里躺着，心中反复盘算。一面，他这样说道：‘此人既已谋刺我的性命，我难道还要赦他么？我这性命，是历经多少水陆战阵保全下来的；我已在普天之下建立了独一的太平，遍及四极。此人不但蓄意杀我，还要拣我献祭未完之际下此毒手，好教我不但丧命，而且沉沦——这样的人难道放他自由？我难道要赦他？’另一面，奥古斯都大帝躺在漆黑的夜里——他原是同你一样心性宽仁的君王——又说出这样的话来：‘奥古斯都啊，你若死了，于众人倒成要紧事，你又何必活着？你的复仇与刑罚，难道永无终期？你一条性命竟这样金贵，值得永远毁掉这许多人的性命去保全么？’”

“嗨，这些心思我也动过，”亨利说。“说下去。这位同我一样心思的皇帝，后来怎样了？”

“陛下，”凯瑟琳接着说，此刻她的双手已搭上他的双肩，“皇后李维娅（Livia）睡在他身侧，知道他夜里的盗汗与苦楚。‘那么妇人的建言，可也值得一听么？’她对他说。‘这件事上，你只消照医士们的成法去办：惯用的方子医不好病了，他们就改试相反的药。从严酷里，陛下，你自始至终、直到此刻这一时，何曾得过一分好处？雷必达（Lepidus）步了萨维狄努斯（Savidienus）的后尘赴死，穆瑞纳（Murena）步了雷必达，凯皮奥（Caepio）步了穆瑞纳，埃纳提乌斯（Eynatius）又步了凯皮奥。事到这一步，且着手试一试，看宽仁如何奏效医人。秦纳罪名已定：赦了他吧。他再没有加害你的力量了，而这件事将永远添入你的荣光。’”

===Original===
She leaned upon him with all her weight, having her arms about his neck.

“Sir,” she said, “the Emperor Augustus listened to his wife, and the days that followed are styled the Golden Age of Rome, he and the Empress having great glory.”

Henry scratched his head, holding his beard back from her face that lay upon his chest; she drew herself from him and once more laid her hands upon his knees. Her fair face was piteous and afraid; her lips trembled.

“Dear lord,” she began tremulously, “I live in this world, and, great pity ’tis! I cannot but have seen how many have died by the block and faggots. Yet is there no end to this. Even today they have burnt upon the one part and the other. I do know thy occasions, thy trials, thy troubles. But think, sir, upon the Empress Livia. Cromwell being dead, find then a Cinna to pardon. Thou hast with thy great and princely endeavourings given a Roman peace to the world. Let now a Golden Age begin in this dear land.”

===Chinese===
她用全身的分量偎倚着他，双臂环住他的脖颈。

“陛下，”她说，“奥古斯都大帝听了妻子的话，此后的岁月便称为罗马的黄金时代，帝后二人同享赫赫荣名。”

亨利搔着头皮，把胡须向后拢住，不教它挨着她那伏在他胸前的脸；她从他怀里退出来，再一次把手搭上他的双膝。她秀美的脸上满是凄楚与惶惧，嘴唇发颤。

“尊贵的主上，”她声音发颤地开了口，“我活在这个世上，说来可叹！断头台上、火刑堆上死了多少人，我不能不都看在眼里。可这事情没有个尽头。就在今天，这一派那一派还都烧死了人。你的处境、你的试炼、你的忧烦，我样样知道。可是想一想吧，陛下，想一想那位李维娅皇后。待克伦威尔死了，那时再找一个秦纳来赦免不迟。你以帝王气象的雄图勤苦，已经把罗马式的太平赐给了世界。如今且让黄金时代在这片可爱的国土上开启吧。”

===Original===
She rose to her feet and stretched out both her hands.

“These be the glories that I crave,” she said. “I would have the glory of advising thee to this. Before God I would escape from being thy Queen if escape I might. I would live as the Sibyls that gave good counsel and lived in rocky cells in sackcloth. So would I fainer. But if you will have me, upon your oaths to me of this our affiancing, I beseech you to give me no jewels, neither the revenue of provinces for my dower. But grant it to me that in after ages men may conceive of me as of such a noble woman of Rome.”

Henry leaned forward and stroked first one knee and then the other.

“Why, I will pardon some,” he said. “It had not need of so many words of thine. I am sick of slaughterings when you speak.” A haughty and challenging frown came into his face; his brows wrinkled furiously; he gazed at the opening door that moved half imperceptibly, slowly, in the half light, after the accustomed manner, so that one within might have time to cry out if a visitor was not welcome. For, for the most part, in those days, ladies set bolts across their doors.

===Chinese===
她站起身来，伸出双手。

“这才是我求的荣光，”她说。“我要的正是劝你行这件事的荣耀。上帝在上，若能脱免，我情愿不做你的王后。我情愿学那些女先知（Sibyls），专意进献良言，身披粗麻，栖身岩窟。那样我更情愿。可你若定要娶我，那么凭你我订聘之时你对我立下的誓言，我求你：不要给我珠宝，也不必以一省的岁入作我的妆奁。只求你应许我，教后世之人提起我来，如同提起罗马那等高贵的女子。”

亨利俯身向前，先抚了抚一条膝盖，又抚了抚另一条。

“嗨，有的人我是要赦的，”他说。“何用你说这许多话。你一开口，我便对杀戮厌倦了。”他脸上浮起一道高傲而挑斗的蹙容，眉心狠狠拧起；他盯着那扇正在开启的门——那门在半明半暗里依着惯常的规矩缓缓开动，几乎看不出动静，好教屋里的人若不欢迎来客，还来得及喊出一声。因为那时候，女子的房门多半是横着上闩的。

===Original===
Throckmorton stood there, blinking his eyes in the candlelight, and, slowly, he fell upon his knees.

“Majesty,” he said, “I knew not.”

The King maintained a forbidding silence, his green bulk inert and dangerous.

“This lady’s cousin,” Throckmorton pronounced his words slowly, “is new come from France whence he hath driven out from Paris town the Cardinal Pole.”

The King lifted one hand from his thigh, and, heavily, let it fall again.

===Chinese===
斯罗克莫顿（Throckmorton）站在那里，在烛光里眨着眼睛，随即慢慢地双膝跪倒。

“陛下，”他说，“我不曾知晓。”

国王守着一片凛然难犯的沉默，那一大团绿色的身躯僵然不动，透着凶险。

“这位小姐的表兄，”斯罗克莫顿一字一字说得极慢，“刚从法兰西回来；正是他，把波尔枢机（Cardinal Pole）逐出了巴黎城。”

国王从腿上抬起一只手，又沉沉地落了回去。

===Original===
Throckmorton felt his way still further.

“This lady’s cousin would speak with this lady in cousin-ship. He was set in my care by my lord Privy Seal. I have brought him thus far in safety. For some have made attacks upon him with swords.”

Katharine’s hand went to her throat where she stood, tall and half turning from the King to Throckmorton. The word “Wherefore?” came from her lips.

“Wherefore, I know not,” Throckmorton answered her steadily. His eyes shifted for a moment from the King and rested upon her face. “But this I know, that I have him in my safe keeping.”

“Belike,” the King said, “these swordsmen were friends of Pole.”

“Belike,” Throckmorton answered.

===Chinese===
斯罗克莫顿又试探着往前说。

“这位小姐的表兄，想以表亲的名分求见这位小姐。他是御玺大人交在我手里照管的。我一路平安把他带到了这里，因为曾有人持剑行刺于他。”

凯瑟琳亭亭立着，半转过身，目光从国王那边移到斯罗克莫顿身上，一只手抚上了咽喉。“为什么？”她脱口而出。

“为什么，我可不知道，”斯罗克莫顿不慌不忙地答她。他的目光从国王身上移开片刻，落在她的脸上。“可我知道的是：此人正在我妥帖的看管之中。”

“多半是，”国王说，“这些持剑的人是波尔的朋友。”

“多半是，”斯罗克莫顿应道。

===Original===
He fingered nonchalantly the rim of his cap that lay beside his knees.

“For his sake,” he said, “it were well if your Grace, having rewarded him princely for this deed, should send him to a distant part, or to Edinbro’ in the Kingdom of Scots, where need for men is to lie and observe.”

“Belike,” the King said. “Get you gone.” But Throckmorton stayed there on his knees and the King uttered: “Anan?”

“Majesty,” Throckmorton said, “I would ye would see this man who is a poor, simple swordsman. He being ill made for courts I would have you reward him and send him from hence ere worse befall him.”

===Chinese===
他漫不经心地捻弄着搁在膝旁的帽子边沿。

“为他自身着想，”他说，“陛下为这桩功劳丰厚地赏过他之后，顶好把他打发到远地方去，或者打发到苏格兰王国的爱丁堡（Edinbro'）去——那地方正用得着伏下人去窥伺探听。”

“多半是，”国王说。“你退下吧。”可斯罗克莫顿仍跪在那里，国王便道：“嗯？”

“陛下，”斯罗克莫顿说，“我望你召见这个人。他是个穷苦粗直的剑客，天生吃不得宫廷饭；我望你赏了他，趁大祸未临，打发他离开此地。”

===Original===
The King raised his brows.

“Ye love this man well,” he said.

“Here is too much beating about the bush,” burst from Katharine’s lips. She stood, tall, winding her hands together, swaying a little and pale in the half light of the two candles. “This cousin of mine loves me well or over well. This gentleman feareth that this cousin of mine shall cause disorders﻿—for indeed he is of disordered intervals. Therefore, he will have you send him from this Court to a far land.”

“Why, this is a monstrous sensible gentleman,” Henry said. “Let us see this yokel.” He had indeed a certain satisfaction at the interrupting, for with Katharine in her begging moods he was never certain that he must not grant her his shirt and go a penance to St. Thomas’ shrine.

===Chinese===
国王扬起眉毛。

“你很疼惜这个人，”他说。

“这弯子绕得也忒大了，”这句话从凯瑟琳唇间冲了出来。她亭亭立着，两手绞在一处，在两支烛光的半明半暗里微微摇晃，面色苍白。“我这位表兄疼我，或是疼得过了头。这位先生怕的是我这位表兄要惹出乱子——他那份心思，本就有错乱的间歇。所以他才要你把他从宫廷打发到远地方去。”

“嗨，这位先生真是通情达理得吓人，”亨利说。“叫这乡巴佬进来见见。”这一打岔倒真叫他受用，因为凯瑟琳一开口求恩典，他就拿不准自己是不是非得把贴身衬衣都给她、再到圣托马斯的圣龛去朝拜赎罪不可。

===Original===
Katharine stayed with her hand upon her heart, but when her cousin came his green figure in the doorway was stiff; he trembled to pass the sill, and looking never at her but at the King’s shoes, he knelt him down in the centre of the floor. The words coming to her in the midst of anguishes and hot emotions, she said:

“Sire, this is my much-loved cousin, who hath bought me food and dress in my days of poverty, selling his very farms.”

Culpepper grunted over his shoulder:

“Hold thy tongue, cousin Kat. Ye know not that ye shall observe silence in the awful presence of kings.”

===Chinese===
凯瑟琳一手按着心口，怔在当地；她表兄进来的时候，门口那道绿色的身影直挺挺的，跨门槛时直打颤，眼睛不望她，只望着国王的鞋，一径跪到屋子正中。她在煎迫与灼热的心绪之中，话冲口而出：

“陛下，这是我最疼爱的表兄，在我贫苦的日子里，他卖了自己的田庄，给我买衣食。”

卡尔佩珀（Culpepper）扭过肩膀咕哝道：

“住口，凯特表妹。你还不知道么，在君王威严赫赫的驾前，须得肃静无哗。”

===Original===
Henry threw his head back and laughed, whilst the chair creaked for a minute’s space.

“Silence!” he said. “Before God, silence! Have ye ever heard this lady’s tongue?” He grew still and dreadful at the end of his mirth.

“Ye have done well,” he said. “Give me your sword. I will knight you. I hear you are a poor man. I give you a knight’s fee farm of a hundred pounds by the year. I hear you are a rough honest man. I had rather ye were about my nephew’s courts than mine. Get you to Edinbro’.” He waved his hand to Throckmorton. “See him disposed,” he said.

===Chinese===
亨利仰头大笑，那把椅子咯吱咯吱响了好一阵。

“静默！”他说。“上帝在上，静默！你可曾领教过这位小姐的舌头？”笑到末了，他蓦地静了下来，神色可怖。

“你做得好，”他说。“把剑给我。我要册封你为骑士。听说你是个穷人，我赐你一项骑士封地的年金，每年一百磅。听说你是个粗直的老实人，你在我侄儿的宫廷里当差，强似在我这里。去爱丁堡吧。”他朝斯罗克莫顿一挥手。“替他安置了，”他说。

===Original===
Culpepper uttered a sound of remonstrance. The King leaned forward in his seat and thundered:

“Get you gone. Be you this night thirty miles towards the Northland. I ha’ heard ye ha’ made brawls and broils here. See you be gone. By God, I am Harry of Windsor!”

He laid the heavy flat of the sword like a blow upon the green shoulders below him.

“Rise up, Sir Thomas Culpepper,” he said. “Get you gone!”

===Chinese===
卡尔佩珀鼻子里哼出一声抗辩。国王在座上俯身向前，雷霆般喝道：

“去吧。今夜之内，给我朝北方赶出三十英里去。我听说你在此地惹过斗殴纠纷。滚吧。上帝在上，我是温莎的哈利！”

他把那沉重宽平的剑面，像一记拍击似的落在下面那副绿色的肩膀上。

“起来吧，托马斯·卡尔佩珀爵士，”他说。“去吧！”

===Original===
Dazed and trembling still a little, Culpepper stuttered his way to the door. When he came by her Katharine cast her arms about his shoulder.

“Poor Tom,” she cried. “Best it is for thee and me that thou goest. Here thou hast no place.” He shook his head like a man in a daze and was gone.

“Art too patient with the springald,” the King said.

He thundered “Body of God!” again when he saw Throckmorton once more fall to his knees.

===Chinese===
卡尔佩珀晕晕乎乎，犹自微微发颤，结结巴巴地挨到门边。经过她身边时，凯瑟琳张开双臂搂住他的肩。

“可怜的汤姆，”她喊道。“你走了，对你我都好。此地没有你的立足之处。”他像失了魂似的摇摇头，去了。

“你对那毛头小子也太纵容了，”国王说。

看见斯罗克莫顿又一次跪倒在地，他又雷鸣般嚷了声“上帝的圣体！”

===Original===
“Sire,” he said﻿—and for the first time he faltered in his level tones﻿—“a very great treason has come to my ken this day!”

“Holy altar fires!” the King growled, “let your treasons wait. Here hath this lady been talking to me very reasonably of a golden age.”

“Sire,” Throckmorton said, and he leant one hand on the floor to support him. “This is a very great treason of men arming to sustain Privy Seal against thee! I have seen it; with mine own eyes I have seen it in thy town of London.”

Katharine cried out, “Ah!”

The King leapt to his feet.

===Chinese===
“陛下，”他说——头一回，他那平板的语调打了颤——“今日有一桩天大的叛逆，传到了我的耳中！”

“圣坛的圣火呀！”国王咆哮道，“你的叛逆且候一候。方才这位小姐正同我讲着黄金时代，讲得头头是道呢。”

“陛下，”斯罗克莫顿说，一手撑在地板上支住身子。“这是天大的叛逆：有人磨刀备甲，要扶持御玺大人来对抗你！我看见了；就在你伦敦城里，我亲眼看见的。”

凯瑟琳失声叫道：“啊！”

国王霍地站起身来。

===Original===
“Ho, I will arm,” he said, and grew pale. For, with a sword in his hand or where fighting was, this King had middling little fear. But, even as the lion dreads a little mouse, so he feared secret rebellions.

“Sire,” Throckmorton said, and his face was towards Katharine as if he challenged her:

“This is the very truth of the very truth, I call upon what man will to gainsay me. This day I heard in the city of London, at the house of the printer, John Badge﻿—” and he repeated the speech of the saturnine man﻿—“that ‘he would raise a thousand prentices and a thousand journeymen to shield Privy Seal from peril; that he could raise ten thousand citizens and ten thousand tenned again from the shires!’ ”

Katharine kept her eyes upon Throckmorton who, knowing her power to sway the King, nodded gravely and looked into her eyes to assure her that these words were true.

===Chinese===
“嗬，我要披挂备战，”他说，脸上却泛了白。因为手握着剑，或是在厮杀场上，这位国王怕得有限；可暗中的叛乱，却叫他惧怕得很，恰如狮子畏惧小鼠。

“陛下，”斯罗克莫顿说，脸却朝着凯瑟琳，仿佛在向她叫阵：

“这是真中之真、千真万确，随哪一个人不服，尽管来驳我。今天我在伦敦城里，在印书人约翰·巴奇（John Badge）的家里听见——”他把那阴沉汉子的话复述了一遍——“说他要唤起一千名学徒、一千名帮工，护卫御玺大人脱险；说他能唤起一万名市民，再从各郡召起一万名佃丁！”

凯瑟琳的目光始终钉在斯罗克莫顿身上；他深知她有拨动国王的能耐，便郑重颔首，直望进她的眼睛，教她确信这些话句句是真。

===Original===
But the King, upon his feet, marched towards the door.

“Let us arm my guard,” he said. “I will play Nero to London town.”

Nevertheless Throckmorton kept his knees.

“Majesty,” he said, “I have this man in my keeping.” And indeed, at his passing London Bridge he had sent men to take the printer and bring him to Hampton. “I pray your pardon that I took him lacking your warrant, and Privy Seal’s I dare not ask.”

===Chinese===
可国王已经站起身，大步朝门口走去。

“去给我的卫队披挂，”他说。“我要给伦敦城当一回尼禄。”

斯罗克莫顿却仍旧跪着。

“陛下，”他说，“此人已在我看管之中。”的确，他路过伦敦桥（London Bridge）的时候，早已派人去捉那印书人，押往汉普顿去了。“我未曾领你的拘票便拿了他，求你恕罪；御玺大人的拘票，我可不敢去讨。”

===Original===
The King stayed in his pacing.

“Thou art a jewel of a man,” he said. “By Cock, I would I had many like thee.” And at the news that the head of this confederacy was taken his sudden fear fell. “I will see this man. Bring him to me.”

“Sire,” Katharine said, “we spoke even now of Cinna. Remember him!”

“Madam,” Throckmorton dared to speak. “This is the man that hath printed broadsides against you. No man more hateth you in land or hath uttered more lewdnesses of your chastity.”

“The more I will have him pardoned,” Katharine said, “that his Highness and all people may see how little I fear his lyings.”

===Chinese===
国王止住了脚步。

“你真是人中之宝，”他说。“老天在上，但愿多有几个你这样的人。”一听说这伙同谋的首脑已经落网，他那阵突发的惊惧便落了地。“我要亲自见见这个人。带他来见我。”

“陛下，”凯瑟琳说，“我们方才还讲着秦纳。记住他！”

“夫人，”斯罗克莫顿大着胆子开了口。“这人印过毁谤你的传单。普天之下，没有人比他更恨你，也没有人散播过更多玷污你贞洁的秽语。”

“我偏要赦他，”凯瑟琳说，“好教陛下与众人都看一看，他的谎话我何曾放在心上。”

===Original===
Throckmorton shrugged his shoulders right up to his ears to signify that this was a very madness of Roman pardoning.

“God send you never rue it,” he said. “Majesty,” he continued to the King, “give me some safe conduct that for half-an-hour I may go about this palace unletted by men of Privy Seal’s. For Privy Seal hath a mighty army of men to do his bidding and I am one man unaided. Give me half-an-hour’s space and I will bring to you this captain of rebellion to your cabinet. And I will bring to you them that shall mightily and to the hilt against all countervail and denial prove that Privy Seal is a false and damnable traitor to thee and this goodly realm. So I swear: Throckmorton who am a trusty knight.”

He was not minded to utter before Katharine Howard the names of his other witnesses. For one of them was the Chancellor of the Augmentations, who was ready to swear that Cromwell, upon the barge when they went in the night from Rochester to Greenwich, had said that he would have the King down if he would not wed with Anne of Cleves. And he had Viridus to swear that Cromwell had said, before his armoury, to the Ambassador of the Schmalkaldners, that ne King, ne Emperor had such another armoury, yet were there twenty score great houses in England that had better, all ready to arm to defend the Protestant faith and Privy Seal. These things he was minded to lay before the King; but before Kat Howard he would not speak them. For, with her mad fury for truth and the letter of Truth that she had gained from reading Seneca till, he thought, her brains were turned, she would begin a wrangle with him. And he had no time to lose; for his ears were pricked up, even as he spoke, to catch any breaking of the silence from the next room where Viridus held Lascelles at the point of his dagger.

===Chinese===
斯罗克莫顿把双肩一直耸到耳根，表示这种罗马式的赦免，实在是疯得可以。

“愿上帝保你日后不悔，”他说。“陛下，”他接着对国王说，“给我一纸安全通行状，好让我在这宫里行走半个钟头，不受御玺大人手下人的阻拦。御玺大人麾下有的是听他号令的人马，我却只孤身一人。给我半个钟点的工夫，我就把这叛党的魁首带到你的私室来。我还要给你带来一班人，任凭怎样的抵赖与否认，他们都能力证到底、不容置辩：御玺大人对你、对这锦绣的邦国，是个弄虚作假、可咒可诅的叛贼。我以此起誓：斯罗克莫顿，一个忠实的骑士。”

他不打算当着凯瑟琳·霍华德的面说出其他证人的名字。因为其中一个便是增益法庭大法官（Chancellor of the Augmentations），那人愿意宣誓证明：那一夜从罗切斯特（Rochester）行船往格林尼治（Greenwich），在画舫上，克伦威尔曾说，国王若不肯娶克莱夫斯的安妮，他便要扳倒国王。他还有维里杜斯（Viridus）可以宣誓证明：克伦威尔曾在自己的兵器库前，对施马尔卡尔登人（Schmalkaldners）的使节说过，无论国王还是皇帝，都不曾有过第二座这样的兵器库；可英格兰还有四百座大宅，兵器还要精良，全都整装待发，誓要捍卫新教的信仰与御玺大人。这些话他本打算在国王面前一一摆出；可当着凯特·霍华德（Kat Howard），他决意不说。因为她读塞涅卡直读到，在他看来，脑子都转了向，添了一股为真理、为真理的每一个字而发狂动怒的性子；说出那些名字，她准要拉住他争辩一场。而他是一刻也耽误不起的；就在他说话的当儿，他的耳朵一直竖着，捕捉隔壁屋里任何打破静默的声响——那屋里，维里杜斯正拿匕首尖抵着拉塞尔斯（Lascelles）。

===Original===
The King said:

“Go thou. If any man stay thee in going whithersoever thou wilt, say that thou beest upon my business; and woe betide them that stay thee if thou be not in my cabinet in the half of an hour with them ye speak of.”

Throckmorton rose stiffly to his feet; at the door he staggered for a moment, and closed his eyes. His cause was won; but he leant against the doorpost and gazed at Katharine with a piteous and passionate glance, moving his fingers in his beard, as if he appealed to her in silence as with the eyes of a faithful hound, neither to judge him harshly nor to plead against him. This was the day of the most strain that ever was in his life.

And gazing back at him, Katharine’s eyes were filled with pity, so sick he appeared to be.

---

===Chinese===
国王说：

“去吧。你无论往哪里去，若有人拦你，就说你办的是我的差事；半个钟点之后，你若不能带着你说的人到我私室里来，那些拦你的人就遭殃了。”

斯罗克莫顿僵着腿脚站起身来；到了门口，踉跄了一步，闭上了眼睛。他这一局是赢了；可他倚着门柱，用一道哀切而灼热的目光望着凯瑟琳，手指在胡须里捻动，仿佛像一条忠犬，只用眼睛默默向她哀告：莫要苛断他，也莫要说他的不是。这是他一生中熬得最紧的一天。

凯瑟琳回望着他，眼里盛满了怜悯——他看来病得那样沉。

---

===Original===
“Body of God!” the King said in the silence that fell upon them. “Now I hold Cromwell.”

Katharine cried out, “Let me go; let me go; this is no world for me!”

He caught her masterfully in his arms.

“This is a golden world, and thou a golden Queen,” he said.

She held her head back from his lips, and struggled from him.

“I may not find any straightness here. I can see no clear way. Let me go.”

===Chinese===
落在他们之间的静默里，国王说道：“上帝的圣体！这一回我可拿住克伦威尔了。”

凯瑟琳喊道：“放我走；放我走；这世道不是为我设的！”

他不由分说，强横地把她搂进怀里。

“这是黄金的世界，你是黄金的王后，”他说。

她仰头避开他的唇，从他怀里挣出来。

“我在这里寻不出一点正直。我看不见一条清亮的路。放我走。”

===Original===
He took her again to him, and again she tore herself free.

“Listen to me,” she cried, “listen to me! There have been broadsides printed against the truth of my body; there have been witnesses prepared against me. I will have you swear that you will read of these broadsides, and consider of these witnesses.”

“Before God,” he said, “I will hang the printers, and slay the witnesses with my fist. I know how these things be made.” He shook his fist. “I love thee so that were they true, and wert thou the woman of Sodom, I would have thee to my Queen!”

She cried out “Ah!”

“Child,” he calmed himself, “I will keep my hands from thee. But I would fain have the kisses of thy mouth.”

She went to lean upon her table, for her knees trembled.

===Chinese===
他又把她揽过去，她又挣脱开来。

“听我说，”她喊道，“听我说！有人印了传单，谤毁我身子的清白；有人备下了指控我的证人。我要你起誓：那些传单你要一一读过，那些证人你要一一察考。”

“上帝在上，”他说，“我要吊死那些印书的，用这只拳头捶死那些证人。这些东西是怎样造出来的，我全明白。”他挥了挥拳头。“我爱你爱到这步田地：就算那些话句句是真，就算你是所多玛的妇人，我也要你做我的王后！”

她失声叫道：“啊！”

“孩子，”他平静下来，“我管住手，不碰你。可我一心想讨你唇上的吻。”

她走过去伏在她的桌上，双膝抖得立不住了。

===Original===
“Let me speak,” she said.

“Why, none hinders,” he answered her kindly.

“I swear I do love thee, so that thy voice is as the blows of hammers upon iron to me,” she said. “I may have little rest, save when I speak with thee, for that sustaineth thy servant. But I fear these days and ways. This is a very crooked riddle. So much I desire thee that I am tremulous to take thee. If it be a madness call it a madness, but grant me this!”

She looked at him distractedly, brushing her hands across her eyes.

“It feels within my heart that I must do a penance,” she said. “I have been wishful to feel upon my brow the pressure of the great crown. Therefore, grant me this: that I may not feel it. And be this the penance!”

===Chinese===
“容我说话，”她说。

“嗨，没有谁拦你呀，”他和颜悦色地答她。

“我起誓我真心爱你，连你的声音落到我身上，都像铁砧上的一声声锤击，”她说。“除了同你说话的时候，我难得有一刻安息，因为唯有那话语支撑着你的婢女。可我怕这样的日子，怕这样的行径。这是一道歪曲的哑谜。我这样渴慕你，以致伸手去接受你时都在发颤。倘若这是癫狂，就管它叫癫狂吧，只求你应许我这一件！”

她心乱如麻地望着他，两手在眼前拂来拂去。

“我心里觉得，我必须行一场苦赎，”她说。“我一直巴望着那顶大冠压上额头的分量。所以求你应许我：叫我永远不必感到它。就让这个做我的苦赎吧！”

===Original===
“Child,” he said, “how may you be a Queen, and not crowned with pomp and state?”

“Majesty,” she faltered, “to prepare myself against that high office I have been reading in chronicles of the lives of them that have been Queens of England. It was his Grace of Canterbury that sent me these books for another purpose. But there ye shall read﻿—in Asser and the Saxon Chronicles﻿—how that the old Queens of Saxondom, when that they were humble or were wives coming after the first, sat not upon the throne to be crowned and sacred, but﻿—so it was with Judith that was stepmother to King Alfred, and with some others whose names in this hurry I may not discover nor remember in my mind﻿—they were, upon some holidays, shewn to the people as being the King’s wife.”

She hung her head.

“For that I am humble in truth before the world and before my mother Mary in Heaven, and for that I am not thy first Queen, but even thy fifth; so I would be shewn and never crowned.”

She leaned back against the table, supporting herself with her hands against its edges; her eyes piteously devoured his face.

===Chinese===
“孩子，”他说，“不经盛仪威仪的加冕，你怎能算得王后呢？”

“陛下，”她讷讷地说，“为预备自己担当那至尊的职分，我一直在读英格兰历代王后的编年史传。坎特伯雷大主教阁下打发人送这些书来，本是为着别的用意。可在那里面你读得到——在阿塞尔（Asser）的书里，在《撒克逊编年史》里——撒克逊古时的王后们，但凡出身寒微，或是续娶的继室，便不上宝座去受加冕与祝圣之礼；她们——朱迪丝（Judith）便是如此，她是阿尔弗雷德王的继母；还有几位，情急之间我叫不出、也想不起名字了——只在某些节庆的日子，作为国王的妻子展示于万民之前。”

她垂下头去。

“因为我在世人面前、在天上的圣母马利亚面前，实在卑微；又因为我不是你的头一位王后，竟是第五位；所以我情愿只受展示，永不受加冕。”

她倚回桌边，两手撑住桌沿；一双眼睛凄楚地、几乎贪婪地吞看着他的脸。

===Original===
“Why, child,” he said, “so thou wilt be that fifth Queen; whether thou wilt be a Queen crowned or a Queen shewn, what care I?”

She no longer refused herself to his arms, for she had no more strength.

“Mary be judge between me and them that speak against me,” she said, “I can no more hold out against my joy or longings.”

“Sha’t wear a hair shirt,” he said tenderly. “Sha’t go in sackcloth. Sha’t have enow to do praying for me and thee. But hast no need of prayers.” He lulled her in his arms, swaying on his feet. “Hast a great tongue. Speakest many words. But art a very child. God send thee all the joy I purpose thee. And, an thou hast sins, weight me further down in hell therewith.”

The light of the candles threw their locked shadows along the wall and up the ceilings. Her head fell back, her eyes closed, so that she seemed to be dead and her listless hands were open in her skirts.

===Chinese===
“嗨，孩子，”他说，“横竖你就是那第五位王后；你是受加冕的王后，还是受展示的王后，与我有什么相干？”

她不再拒绝他的怀抱，因为她已经没有力气了。

“愿马利亚在我与毁谤我的人中间主持公道，”她说，“我的欢喜、我的渴念，我再也抵挡不住了。”

“你要穿刚毛衬衣，”他温柔地说。“你要披麻衣。你要多多为我为你祈祷，管保够你忙的。可你原就用不着祈祷。”他把她搂在怀里轻轻地摇，脚下缓缓地晃。“你有一条好大的舌头。话多得不休。可你到底是个孩子。愿上帝把你——连同我为你筹划的一切欢悦——都赐给你。你若有什么罪，就拿那些罪把我往地狱里再压沉几分。”

烛光把他们相拥的影子投上墙壁，又一直投上天花板。她的头向后仰落，双眼阖上，仿佛已然死去；一双绵软无力的手，摊开在裙裾之间。

===Original===
To

**Frau Laura Schmedding**

who has so often combated

my prejudices and corrected

my assertions

this with affection

===Chinese===
谨呈

**劳拉·施梅丁夫人（Frau Laura Schmedding）**

她屡屡驳斥我的成见，

匡正我的种种断言，

谨以此书

聊表深情

===Original===
> “Ille potens… et lætus cui licet in diem

> Dixisse: Vixi!…”

===Chinese===
> “福哉斯人……且欢欣自得，日日得以

> 道一声：我活过了！……”（拉丁语题辞，出自贺拉斯《颂诗集》卷一第九首）

### I / 一

===Original===
The Magister Udal sat in the room of his inn in Paris, where customarily the King of France lodged such envoys as came at his expense. He had been sent there to Latinise the letters that passed between Sir Thomas Wyatt and the King's Ministers of France, for he was esteemed the most learned man in these islands. He had groaned much at being sent there, for he must leave in England so many loves—the great, blonde Margot Poins, that was maid to Katharine Howard; the tall, swaying Katharine Howard herself; Judge Cantre's wife that had fed him well; and two other women, with all of whom he had succeeded easily or succeeded in no wise at all. But the mission was so well paid—with as many crowns the day as he had had groats for teaching the Lady Mary of England—that fain he had been to go. Moreover, it was by way of being a favour of Privy Seal's. The magister had written for him a play in English; the rich post was the reward—and it was an ill thing, a thing the magister dreaded, to refuse the favours of Privy Seal. He consoled himself with the thought that the writing of letters in Latin might wash from his mouth the savour of the play he had written in the vulgar tongue.

But his work in Paris was ended—for with the flight of Cardinal Pole, who had left Paris precipitately upon news that the King of England had sent a drunken roisterer to assassinate him, it was imagined that soon now more concord between Francis and England might ensue, and the magister sat in his room planning his voyage back to Dover. The room was great in size, panelled mostly in wood, lit with lampwicks that floated in oil dishes and heated with a sea-coal fire, for though it was April the magister was of a cold disposition of the hands and shins. The inn—of the Golden Astrolabe—was kept by an Englishwoman, a masterful widow with a broad face and a great mouth that smiled. She stood beside him there. Forty-seven she might have been, and she called herself the Widow Annot.

The magister sat over his fire with his gown parted from his legs to warm his shins, but his hands waved angrily and his face was crestfallen.
===Chinese===
乌达尔学士（Magister Udal）坐在巴黎客栈的房间里；凡由他出资接待的使节，法兰西国王照例都安置在这家客栈里。他奉派到此，为的是将托马斯·怀亚特爵士（Sir Thomas Wyatt）同法兰西国王诸大臣往来的书信译作拉丁文，因为在这片群岛之上，他素来被推为最有学问的人。接到差遣时他叫了好些苦，只因英格兰还有他那么多心上人撇不下：身材高大、一头金发的玛戈·庞兹（Margot Poins），凯瑟琳·霍华德（Katharine Howard）的使女；修长摇曳的凯瑟琳·霍华德本人；把他喂养得顶好的坎特尔法官（Judge Cantre）夫人；此外还有两个女人——这几位，他或轻易得手，或全然未能如愿。可这趟差事酬金实在丰厚——他如今每日所得的克朗，恰似他当年教导英格兰的玛丽小姐（Lady Mary）时每日所得的格罗特——故而他心甘情愿地去了。况且，这也算得是御玺大人（Privy Seal）的一桩恩典。学士曾为他用英文写过一部戏，这肥缺便是酬赏——而拂逆御玺大人的恩典是桩坏事，是学士打心底里发怵的事。他聊以自慰地想：写拉丁文书信，或许能洗去他口中那出以俗语写成的戏文留下的余味。

但他在巴黎的差事已经完结——波尔枢机（Cardinal Pole）一听到风声，说是英格兰国王差了个醉醺醺的浪荡儿来行刺，便仓皇离了巴黎；他这一走，人人都料想弗朗索瓦（Francis）与英格兰不久便可重修旧好，学士便坐在房间里筹划回多佛的行程。房间甚是宽敞，四壁大半是木镶板，以浮在油碟里的灯芯取亮，以海煤炉火取暖——虽是四月，学士的手掌和小腿却天生畏寒。这家客栈——字号「金星盘」（the Golden Astrolabe）——由一个英格兰女人掌管，是位精明泼辣的寡妇，阔脸盘，一张大嘴总挂着笑。此刻她便立在他身旁。年纪许有四十七岁，自称安诺特寡妇（Widow Annot）。

学士坐在炉火前，长袍自腿上撩开，烘着小腿，两只手却气恼地挥舞，满脸垂头丧气。

===Original===
"Oh, keeper of a tavern," he said. "It is set down in holy writ that it is not good for a man to be alone."

"That a hostess shall keep her tavern clean is writ in the books of the provost of Paris town," the Widow Annot answered, and the shadow of her great white hood, which she wore in the older English fashion, danced over the brown wooden beams of the ceiling.

"Nay, nay," he answered, "it is written there that it is the enjoined devoir of every hotelier to provide things fitting for the sojourners' ease, pleasure and recreation."

"The maid is locked in another house," the hostess answered, "and should have been this three week." She swung her keys on a black riband and gazed at him masterfully. "Will your magistership eat capon or young goat?"

"Capon will have a savour like sawdust, and young goat like the dust of the road," the magister moaned. "Give me the girl to wait upon me again."
===Chinese===
「噢，开酒馆的掌柜娘子，」他说，「经上写着：男人独居，终究不好。」

「店家娘子须把酒馆收拾干净，这话写在巴黎城治安长官的册子里，」安诺特寡妇答道。她那顶照英格兰旧式样戴的白色大兜帽，影子在天花板褐色的木梁间晃动。

「不，不，」他答道，「那上头写的是：凡开栈待客之人，依例有责，须备齐合宜的诸般物事，好教旅人安适、欢娱、消遣。」

「那丫鬟锁在另一所宅子里了，」女店家答道，「早三个礼拜就该锁了。」她把拴在黑缎带上的钥匙晃得叮当响，顾指气使地盯住他。「学士大人用饭，要阉鸡还是小山羊？」

「阉鸡吃起来必同锯末一般，小山羊必同路上尘土一般，」学士呻吟道。「把那丫头还来伺候我吧。」

===Original===
"No maid will wait upon thee," she answered.

"Even thou thyself?" he asked. He glanced across his shoulder and his eyes measured her, hers him. She had large shoulders, a high, full stomacher, and her cheeks were an apple-red. "The maiden was a fair piece," he tittered.

"Therefore you must spoil the ring of the coin," she answered.

He sighed: "Then eat you with me. '*Soli cantare periti Arcades.*' But it is cold here alone of nights."
===Chinese===
「没有丫鬟再来伺候你，」她答道。

「那你自己么？」他问。他侧过眼去一瞥，目光在她身上掂量，她的目光也在他身上掂量。她肩背宽阔，胸兜高耸饱满，双颊红得像苹果。「那小娘子原是枚好钱，」他吃吃地笑。

「好端端一枚钱，叫你敲哑了响，」她答道。

他叹了口气：「那你来陪我吃。『*Soli cantare periti Arcades.*』——唯阿卡狄亚人善歌。只是夜里独自个儿，冷得很。」

===Original===
They ate goat and green leeks sweetened with honey, and wood thrushes pickled in wine, and salt fish from the mouth of the Beauce. And because this gave the magister a great thirst he drank much of a warmed wine from Burgundy that the hostess brought herself. They sat, byside, on cushions on a couch before the warm fire.

"*Filia pulchra mater pulchrior!*" the magister muttered, and he cast his arms about her soft and plump waist. "The maid was a fair skewer, the hostess is a plumper roasting bit." She took his kisses on her fire-warmed cheeks, but in the end she thrust him mightily from her with a large elbow.

He gasped with the strength of her thrust, and she said:

"Greedy dogs getten them hard cuffs," and rearranged her neckercher. When he tried to come nearer her she laughed and thrust him aback.

"You have tried and tasted," she said. "A fuller meal you must pay for."
===Chinese===
两人吃了山羊肉、蜂蜜调和的青韭葱、酒渍的画眉，还有博斯河口来的咸鱼。这一顿叫学士渴得厉害，女店家亲自捧来温热的勃艮第酒，他喝了许多。随后两人并肩坐在软榻的垫子上，挨着暖融融的炉火。

「*Filia pulchra mater pulchrior!*」——女儿俏，母亲更俏！学士喃喃念着，双臂搂住她柔软丰腴的腰身。「那丫鬟不过是根漂亮的烤钎，女店家才是一块更肥的烤肉。」她由他吻着被炉火焐热的脸颊，末了却扬起粗壮的胳膊肘，将他猛地搡开。

这一搡力道十足，搡得他直喘，她却说：

「馋狗总得挨硬揍，」一面整了整颈巾。他再想凑近时，她笑着又把他推了回去。

「一口鲜也让你尝过了，」她说。「要吃整席，就得付整席的账。」

===Original===
He stood before her, lean and lank, his gown flapping about his calves, his eyes smiling humorously, his lips twitching.

"Oh soft and warm woman," he cried, "payment shall be yours"; and whilst he fumbled furiously in his clothespress, he quoted from Tully: "*Haec civitas mulieri redimiculum praebuit.*" He pulled out one small bag: "*Haec in collum.*" She took another. "*Haec in crines!*" and he added a third, saying: "Here is all I have," and cast the three into her lap. Whilst she counted the coins composedly on the table before her he added: "Leave me nevertheless the price to come to England with."

"Sir Magister," she said, turning her large face to him. "This is not one-tenth enough. You have tasted an ensample. Will you have the whole meal?"

"Oh, unconscionable," he cried. "More I have not!" He began to wave his hands. "Consider what you do do," he uttered. "Think of what a pest is love. How many have died of it. Pyramus, Thisbe, Dido, Medea, Croesus, Callirhoe, Theagines the philosopher … Consider what writes Gordonius: '*Prognosticatio est talis: si non succuratur iis aut in maniam cadunt: aut moriuntur.*' Unless lovers be succoured either they fall into a madness, either they die or grow mad. And Fabian Montaltus: 'If this passion be not assuaged, the inflammation cometh to the brain. It drieth up the blood. Then followeth madness or men make themselves away.' I would have you ponder of what saith Parthenium and what Plutarch in his tales of lovers."
===Chinese===
他站在她面前，又瘦又瘠，长袍在小腿四周扑扇，眼里闪着打趣的笑，嘴唇抽动不停。

「噢，温暖绵软的妇人，」他嚷道，「钱自然归你」；他一面在衣橱里拼命翻检，一面引图利（Tully，即西塞罗）的文句：「*Haec civitas mulieri redimiculum praebuit.*」——「此城曾为妇人备下头带。」他掏出一只小钱袋：「*Haec in collum.*」——「这条戴在颈上。」她另接了一只。「*Haec in crines!*」——「这条束在发间！」他又添上第三只，说道：「我全部家当尽在于此，」把三只袋子一齐丢进她怀里。她镇定自若，在面前桌上数着钱，他在一旁又道：「只求留下回英格兰的盘缠。」

「学士先生，」她说，把那张大脸盘转向他。「这连十分之一也不够。你尝到的不过是个样品。整席，你可还要？」

「噢，你这不知足的，」他嚷道。「再多可没有了！」他挥起双手。「你且想想你干的是什么，」他说。「想想爱是何等害人的瘟疫。多少人死于它：皮拉摩斯、提斯柏、狄多、美狄亚、克罗伊斯、卡利罗厄，还有哲学家忒阿革涅斯……且看戈尔多尼乌斯（Gordonius）如何写道：『*Prognosticatio est talis: si non succuratur iis aut in maniam cadunt: aut moriuntur.*』——情人若不得救助，非坠入癫狂，即告殒灭，再不然便是疯掉。法比安·蒙塔尔图斯（Fabian Montaltus）也说：『这情热若不加以平息，火热便上行入脑，耗干血液；随后不是疯癫，便是自寻短见。』帕特尼乌斯（Parthenium）的话，普鲁塔克《情人故事》里的话，还望你好生思量。」

===Original===
Her face appeared comely and smooth in his eyes, but she shook her head at him.

"These be woeful and pretty stories," she said. "I would have you to tell me many of them."

"All through the night," he said eagerly, and made to clasp her in his arms. But she pushed him back again with her hand on his chest.

"All through the night an you will," she said. "But first you shall tell a prettier tale before a man in a frock."

He sprang full four feet back at one spring.
===Chinese===
在他眼中，她的脸盘又端丽又光润，可她只朝他摇头。

「这些故事又凄婉又动听，」她说。「往后你要多多讲给我听。」

「讲你一整夜，」他急切地说，作势便要搂她入怀。她却一掌抵住他胸口，又把他推开了。

「一整夜也依你，」她说。「只是你得先当着一个穿法衣的男人，讲一段更好听的故事。」

他一纵身，足足蹦出去四英尺远。

===Original===
"I have wedded no woman, yet," he said.

"Then it is time you wed one now," she answered.

"Oh widow, bethink you," he pleaded. "Would you spoil so pretty a tale? Would you humble so goodly a man's pride?"

"Why, it were a pity," she said. "But I am minded to take a husband."

"You have done well this ten years without one," he cried out.
===Chinese===
「我至今不曾娶妻，」他说。

「那如今正是该娶的时候，」她答道。

「噢，寡妇，你且三思，」他央求道。「你要毁掉这么一段美事么？你要折辱这么一位好男儿的傲气么？」

「唉，那自然可惜，」她说。「可我存心要寻个丈夫。」

「这十年你没有丈夫，不也过得好好的，」他叫了起来。

===Original===
"Call it a woman's mad freak," she said.

"Six and twenty pupils in the fair game of love I have had," he said. "You shall be the seven and twentieth. Twenty and seven are seven and two. Seven and two are nine. Now nine is the luckiest of numbers. Be you that one."

"Nay," she answered. "It is time you learned husbandry who have taught so many and earned so little."

He slipped himself softly into the cushions beside her.

"Would you spoil so fair a tale?" he said. "Would you have me to break so many vows? I have promised a mort of women marriage, and so long as I be not wed I may keep faith with any one of them."
===Chinese===
「就当是女人一时的疯念头，」她说。

「在爱情的妙局里，我已收过二十六个弟子，」他说。「你来做第二十七个。二十七，是七加二；七加二是九；九是最吉利的数目。你来做那一个吧。」

「不成，」她答道。「你教了这么多弟子，挣的却这么少，如今也该学学为人丈夫、当家立计的本事了。」

他悄悄出溜到她身旁的坐垫里。

「你要糟蹋这么美的一段故事么？」他说。「你要叫我背弃这么多誓言么？我许过多少女子婚约，只要一日不娶，便能同她们哪一个都守着信义。」

===Original===
She held her face away from him and laughed.

"That is as it may be," she said. "But when you wed with me tonight you will keep faith with one woman."

"Woman," he pleaded. "I am a great scholar."

"Ay," she answered, "and great scholars have climbed to great estates."

She continued to count the coins that came from his little moneybags; the shadow of her hood upon the great beams grew more portentous.
===Chinese===
她把脸别开，笑了起来。

「那也由得你说，」她说。「可你今夜若娶了我，便是对一个女人守了信义。」

「夫人，」他央道。「我是个大学者。」

「是嘛，」她答道。「大学者之中，也有攀上高位厚禄的。」

她接着数那几只小钱袋里倒出的钱币；她的兜帽影子映在大梁上，愈显不祥。

===Original===
"It is thought that your magistership may rise to be Chancellor of the Realm of England," she added.

He clutched his forehead.

"Eheu!" he said. "If you have heard men say that, you know that wedded to thee I could never climb."

"Then I shall very comfortably keep my inn here in Paris town," she answered. "You have here fourteen pounds and eleven shillings."

He stretched forth his lean hands:
===Chinese===
「外间都传，学士大人有朝一日能升做英格兰王国的大法官呢，」她又添了一句。

他一把攥住自己的额头。

「*Eheu!*——唉！」他说。「你既听人这么说过，便该知道：娶了你，我就再也攀不上了。」

「那我便在巴黎城里安安稳稳守着我的客栈，」她答道。「这儿拢共是十四磅十一先令。」

他伸出枯瘦的双手：

===Original===
"Why, I will marry thee in the morning," he said, and he moistened his lips with the tip of his tongue. Outside the door there was a shuffling of several feet.

"I knew not other guests were in the house," he uttered, and fell again to kissing her.

"Knew you not an envoy was come from Cleves?" she whispered.

Her head fell back and he supported it with one trembling hand. He shook like a leaf when her voice rang out:

"*Au secours! Au secours!*"
===Chinese===
「那么，明儿一早我就娶你，」他说，舌尖润了润嘴唇。门外响起了几双脚拖拖沓沓的声音。

「我不知店里另有客人，」他说着，又低头吻起她来。

「你不知克莱夫斯（Cleves）来的使节到了么？」她悄声说。

她的头向后一仰，他用一只发颤的手托住。待她的声音骤然炸响，他抖得像风中一片叶子：

「*Au secours! Au secours!*」——救命呀！救命呀！

===Original===
There was a great jangle, light fell into the dusky room through the doorhole, and he found himself beneath the eyes of many scullions with spits, cooks with carving forks, and kitchenmaids with sharpened distaffs of steel.

"Now I will be wed this night," she laughed.

He moved to the end of the couch and blinked at her in the strong light.

"I will be wed this night," she said again, and rearranged her headdress, revealing, as her sleeves fell open, her white, plump arms.

"Why, no!" he answered irresolutely.
===Chinese===
一阵叮当乱响，灯光从门洞涌进昏暗的屋子，他发觉自己落在无数道目光之下：擎着烤叉的厨房小厮，攥着切肉叉的厨子，握着磨尖钢卷线杆的厨房丫头。

「今夜我就要成亲，」她大笑道。

他挪到软榻尽头，在强光下朝她直眨眼。

「今夜我就要成亲，」她又说了一遍，一面理正头上饰巾，袖口张开，露出一双白胖的臂膀。

「哎，不行！」他迟疑不决地答道。

===Original===
She said in French to her aids:

"Come near him with the spits!"

They moved towards him, a white-clad body with their pointed things glittering in the light of torches. He sprang behind the great table against the window and seized the heavy-leaden sandarach. The French scullions knew, tho' he had no French, that he would cleave one of their skulls, and they stood, a knot of seven—four men and three maids—in blue hoods, in the centre of the room.

"By Mars and by Apollo!" he said, "I was minded to wed with thee if I could no other way. But now, like Phaeton, I will cast myself from the window and die, or like the wretches thrown from the rock, called Tarpeian. I was minded to a folly: now I am minded rather for death."

"How nobly thy tongue doth wag, husband," she said, and cried in French for the rogues to be gone. When the door closed upon the lights she said in the comfortable gloom: "I dote upon thy words. My first was tongue-tied." She beckoned him to her and folded her arms. "Let us discourse upon this matter," she said comfortably. "Thus I will put it: you wed with me or spring from the window."
===Chinese===
她用法语吩咐手下人：

「拿烤叉逼上他！」

他们朝他逼近，一伙白衣人，手中的尖物在火把光里闪闪发亮。他一纵身躲到窗边的大桌后头，抄起那只沉甸甸的灌铅砂盒。那伙法国小厮不必听他开口——他本不会法语——也看得出他会劈碎他们中间谁的脑袋，便都站定了，七个人扎成一堆——四男三女，一律蓝兜帽——立在屋子中央。

「以玛尔斯、以阿波罗起誓！」他说，「我原想着实在无法，才要娶你。可如今，我要学法厄同（Phaeton），从这窗口纵身跳下去摔死；要不就学那些倒霉人，被从那座唤作塔尔皮亚的岩石上抛下去。我方才存的是个愚念，如今宁可存一个死念。」

「你的舌头摇得多气派呀，夫君，」她说，又用法语喝令那帮无赖退出去。灯光退出门外、门阖上之后，她在舒舒服服的昏暗里说：「我就迷你这些话。我头一个男人是个闷嘴葫芦。」她朝他招招手，双臂环抱。「咱们把这桩事商量商量，」她舒舒服服地说。「我把话摆在这儿：要么娶我，要么跳窗。」

===Original===
"I am even trapped?" he asked.

"So it comes to all foxes that too long seek for capons," she answered.

"But consider," he said. He sat himself by the fireside upon a stool, being minded to avoid temptation.

"I would have your magistership forget the rogues that be without," she said.

"They were a nightmare's tale," he said.
===Chinese===
「我就这样落了圈套？」他问。

「贪恋肥阉鸡的狐狸，到头来全是这个下场，」她答道。

「可是你想想，」他说。他在炉边一张凳子上坐定，存心躲开诱惑。

「还望学士大人忘了外头那帮无赖，」她说。

「他们是一场噩梦里的人物，」他说。

===Original===
"Yet forget them not too utterly," she answered. "For I am of some birth. My father had seven horses and never followed the plough."

"Oh buxom one!" he answered. "Of a comfortable birth and girth thou art. Yet with thee around my neck I might not easily climb."

"Magister," she said, "whilst thou climbest in London town thy wife will bide in Paris."

"Consider!" he said. "There is in London town a fair, large maid called Margot Poins."

"Is she more fair than I?" she asked. "I will swear she is."
===Chinese===
「可也别忘得太干净，」她答道。「我也有几分出身。我父亲有过七匹马，从没扶过犁。」

「噢，丰腴的人儿！」他答道。「出身也好，腰身也好，你都教人舒心。可你若挂在我脖子上，我怕就爬不高喽。」

「学士，」她说，「你在伦敦城里攀高的时候，你的妻子便在巴黎守着。」

「你再想想！」他说。「伦敦城里有个标致高大的姑娘，名叫玛戈·庞兹。」

「她比我标致么？」她问。「我敢赌咒，她准是。」

===Original===
He tilted his stool forward.

"No; no, I swear it," he said eagerly.

"Then I will swear she is more large."

"No; not one half so bounteous is her form," he answered, and moved across to the couch.

"Then if you can bear her weight up you can bear mine," she said, and moved away from him.
===Chinese===
他把凳子朝前一倾。

「不；不，我对天赌咒，」他急急地说。

「那我就赌咒，她比我高大。」

「不；她那身段连你一半丰盈也没有，」他答道，一边挪向软榻。

「那你驮得动她的分量，就驮得动我的，」她说着，从他身边挪开了。

===Original===
"Nay," he answered. "She would help me on," and he fumbled in the shadows for her hand. She drew herself together into a small space.

"You affect her more than me," she said, with a swift motion simulating jealousy.

"By the breasts of Venus, no!" he answered.

"Oh, once more use such words," she murmured, and surrendered to him her soft hand. He rubbed it between both of his cold ones and uttered:

"By the Paphian Queen: by her teams of doves and sparrows! By the bower of Phyllis and the girdle of Egypt's self! I love thee!"
===Chinese===
「不，」他答道。「她会托着我往上爬，」他在暗影里摸索她的手。她把身子缩作小小一团。

「你更喜欢的是她，不是我，」她说，倏地摆出一副佯装的妒态。

「以维纳斯的乳胸起誓，不！」他答道。

「噢，再赌一回这样的咒，」她喃喃道，把柔嫩的手送到他掌中。他用两只冰凉的手夹住搓着，说道：

「以帕福斯的女王起誓：以她驾前的鸽队雀群起誓！以菲利斯的凉亭、以埃及本土的宝带起誓！我爱你！」

===Original===
She gurgled "oh's" of pleasure.

"But this Margot Poins is tirewoman to the Lady Katharine Howard."

"I am tirewoman to mine own self alone," she said. "Therefore you love her better."

"Nay, oh nay," he said gently. "But this Lady Katharine Howard is mistress to the King's self."

"And I have been mistress to no married man save my husbands," she answered. "Therefore you love this Margot Poins better."
===Chinese===
她快活得「噢、噢」地直哼。

「可这个玛戈·庞兹，是凯瑟琳·霍华德小姐的更衣侍女。」

「我只给自己更衣，」她说。「可见你更爱她。」

「不，哦，不，」他温言道。「可这位凯瑟琳·霍华德小姐，是国王本人的情妇。」

「除了我那几位丈夫，我从不曾给有妇之夫做过情妇，」她答道。「可见你更爱这个玛戈·庞兹。」

===Original===
He fingered her soft palm and rubbed it across his own neck.

"Nay, nay," he said. "But I must wed with Margot Poins."

"Why with her more than with me or any other of your score and seven?" she said softly.

"Since the Lady Katharine will be Queen," he answered, and once again he was close against her side. She sighed softly.

"Thus if you wed with me you will never be Chancellor," she said.
===Chinese===
他用指头摩挲她柔软的掌心，又拉过那只手在自己脖颈上蹭。

「不，不，」他说。「可我非娶玛戈·庞兹不可。」

「为什么偏是她，不是我，也不是你那二十七个里随便哪个？」她柔声说。

「因为凯瑟琳小姐就要做王后了，」他答道，身子又一次贴上她的腰侧。她轻轻叹了口气。

「这么说，你若娶了我，便永远做不成大法官了，」她说。

===Original===
"I would not anger the Queen," he answered. She nestled bountifully and warmly against him.

"Swear even again that you like me more than the fair, large wench in London town," she whispered against his ear.

"Even as Jove prized Danaë above the Queen of Heaven, even as Narcissus prized his shadow above all the nymphs, even as Hercules placed Omphale above his strength, or even as David the King of the Jews Bathsheba above. …"

She murmured "Oh, oh," and placed her arms around his shoulders.

"How I love thy brave words!"
===Chinese===
「我不愿惹恼王后，」他答道。她丰润而温暖地偎着他。

「再赌一回咒，说你的喜欢在我，不在伦敦城里那个标致高大的丫头，」她贴着他耳朵低语。

「一如朱庇特爱达那厄胜过天后，一如那喀索斯爱自己的影子胜过众仙女，一如赫拉克勒斯把翁法勒看得比他的神力还重，又如犹太人的王大卫爱拔示巴胜过……」

她低声「噢，噢」，双臂环上他的肩头。

「你这些豪言壮语，教我怎么爱听！」

===Original===
"And being Chancellor," he swore, "I will come back to thee, oh woman of the sweet smiles, honey of Hymettus, Cypriote wine. …"

She moved herself a little from him in the darkness.

"And if you do not wed with Margot Poins. …"

"I pray a plague may fall upon her, but I must wed with her," he answered. "Come now; come now!"

"Else the Lady Katharine shall be displeased with your magistership?"

He sought to draw her to him, but she stiffened herself a little.
===Chinese===
「待我做了大法官，」他起誓道，「我一定回到你身边——噢，笑靥甘美的妇人，希梅塔斯的蜂蜜，塞浦路斯的酒浆……」

黑暗里，她从他身上稍稍挪开。

「那要是你不娶玛戈·庞兹呢……」

「我巴不得瘟疫降到她头上，可我非娶她不可，」他答道。「来吧；来吧！」

「不然，凯瑟琳小姐就要同学士大人过不去了？」

他想把她拉进怀里，她却把身子绷紧了些。

===Original===
"And this Lady Katharine is mistress to the King of England's realm?"

His hands moved tremblingly towards her in the darkness.

"And this Lady Katharine shall be Queen?"

A hiss of exasperation came upon his lips, for she had slipped from beneath his hands into the darkness.

"Why, then, I will not stay your climbing," she said. "Good night," and in the darkness he heard her sob.

The couch fell backwards as he swore and sprang towards her voice.
===Chinese===
「而这位凯瑟琳小姐，是英格兰举国之主的情妇？」

黑暗里，他的手颤颤地朝她探去。

「而这位凯瑟琳小姐就要做王后？」

他的唇间迸出一声恼恨的嘶声，因为她已从他掌下溜脱，遁入黑暗。

「好，那我不拦你的攀高了，」她说。「晚安。」黑暗里，他听见她啜泣。

他咒骂着朝她的声音扑去，软榻翻倒在地。

===Original===
"Magister!" she said. "Hands off! Unwed thou shalt not have me, for I have sworn it."

"I have sworn to wed seven and twenty women," he said, "and have wedded with none."

"Nay, nay," she sobbed. "Hands off. Henceforth I will make no vows—but no one but thee shall wed me."

"Then wed me, in God's name!" he cried, and, screaming:

"*Ho là! Apportez le prestre!*" she softened herself in his arms.
===Chinese===
「学士！」她说。「把手拿开！不成婚，你休想得到我，我是发过誓的。」

「我发过誓要娶二十七个女人，」他说，「却一个也还没娶。」

「不，不，」她抽噎着。「手拿开。打今儿起我再不发什么誓了——可除了你，谁也别想娶我。」

「那就娶我，看在上帝份上！」他喊道。她尖声叫道：

「*Ho là! Apportez le prestre!*」——喂！把神父给我请来！——随即在他怀里软了下来。

===Original===
The magister confronted the lights, the leering scullions and the grinning maids with their great mantles; his brown, woodpecker-like face was alike crestfallen and thirsty with desire. A lean Dominican, with his brown cowl back and spectacles of horn, gabbled over his missal and took a crown's fee—then asked another by way of penitence for the sin with the maid locked up in another house. When they brought the bride favours of pink to pin into her gorget she said:

"I long had loved thee for thy great words, husband. Therefore all these I had in readiness."

With that knot fast upon him, the magister, clasping his gown upon his shins, looked askance at the floor. Whilst they made ready the bride, with great lights and laughter, she said:

"I was minded to have a comfortable husband. And a comfortable husband is a husband much absent. What more comfortable than me in Paris town and thee in London city? I keep my inn here, thou mindest thy book there. Thou shalt here find a goodly capon upon occasion, and when thou hast a better house in London I will come share it."
===Chinese===
学士迎着灯光，迎着那些挤眉弄眼的小厮和披大斗篷、咧嘴直笑的丫头们；他那张褐色的小脸活像啄木鸟，又沮丧，又烧着欲望的干渴。一个瘦削的多明我会修士，褐色风帽推在脑后，架一副角质眼镜，对着弥撒书急急念念有词，收下一枚克朗的酬金——跟着又讨一枚，权当为他和锁在另一所宅子里的那丫鬟犯下的罪孽做补赎。众人捧来石竹花的婚礼花结，要给新娘别在领口上，她开了口：

「我早就爱你这一口豪言壮语了，夫君。所以这些物件我早都备下了。」

这一个结子既已牢牢拴在身上，学士把长袍拢住小腿，斜眼瞅着地板。众人张灯喧笑，替新娘装扮，她说：

「我存心要个舒心的丈夫。舒心的丈夫，便是常不在家的丈夫。还有比这更舒心的么——我在巴黎城，你在伦敦城？我在这儿守我的客栈，你在那儿守你的书本。你想开荤时，这儿不时有上好的阉鸡候着；等你将来在伦敦置下更好的宅子，我便来同住。」

===Original===
"Trapped! Trapped!" the magister muttered to himself. "Even as was Sir Launcelot!"

He considered of the fair and resentful Margot Poins whom it was incumbent indeed that he should wed: that Katharine Howard loved her well and was in these matters straitlaced. When his eyes measured his wife he licked his lips; when his eyes were on the floor his jaw fell. At best the new Mistress Udal would be in Paris. He looked at the rope tied round the thin middle of the brown priest, and suddenly he leered and cast off his cloak.

"Let me remember to keep an equal mind in these hard matters," he quoted, and fell to laughing.

For he remembered that in England no marriage by a friar or monk held good in those years. Therefore he was the winner. And the long, square room, with the cave bed behind its shutter in the hollow of the wall, the light-coloured, square beams, and the foaming basin of bride-ale that a fat-armed girl in a blue kerseymere gown served out to scullion after scullion; the open windows from which a little knave was casting bride-pennies to some screaming beggars and women in the street; the blind hornman whose unseeing eyes glanced along the reed of his bassoon that he played before the open door; the two saucy maids striving to wrest the bride's stockings one from the other—all these things appeared friendly and jovial in his eyes. So that, when one of the maids, wresting the stocking, fell hard against him, he clasped her in his arms and kissed her till she struggled from him to drink a mug of bride-ale.

"*Hodie mihi: mihi atque cras!*" he said. For it was in his mind a goodly thing to pay a usuress with base coins.
===Chinese===
「落网了！落网了！」学士暗自嘀咕。「兰斯洛特爵士也是这般下场！」

他心里想着那标致而衔恨的玛戈·庞兹——他确乎是非娶她不可：凯瑟琳·霍华德疼爱她，且在这类事上规矩极严。目光落在妻子身上时，他舔舔嘴唇；目光落到地板上时，他下巴便垂了下来。往顶好里说，新妇乌达尔太太也不过在巴黎。他望着系在褐衣神父细腰上的那根绳子，忽然色迷迷地一乐，把外袍一甩。

「『且让我记着，处此艰难诸事，当持不易之心。』」他引了一句古训，随即大笑起来。

只因他记起，那几年在英格兰，凡托钵僧或修士主持的婚配一概无效。这么算来，赢家正是他。再看这间长方的屋子——嵌在墙凹处、以木扉掩着的壁床，浅色的方木梁，穿蓝开司米呢长裙、粗胳膊的丫头把泛着泡沫的婚宴麦酒一碗碗舀给挨个儿上来的小厮；敞开的窗口，一个小顽童正向街上叫嚷的乞丐和女人们撒喜钱；盲眼的乐手，无神的眼睛顺着巴松的簧管望出去，在敞开的门前吹奏；两个俏皮丫头你夺我抢，争扯那双新娘袜——这一切在他眼里都透着亲热和欢腾。于是，当其中一个丫头抢夺袜子时重重撞在他身上，他便一把搂住，吻个不停，直到她挣扎脱身，去喝一大杯喜酒。

「*Hodie mihi: mihi atque cras!*」——今日归我：我且明日也归我！他说。在他想来，拿劣币去偿付放印子钱的女人，正是一桩美事。

### II / 二

===Original===
It was three days later, in the morning, that his captress said to the Magister Udal:
“Husband, it is time that I gave thee the bridal gift.”
The magister, happy with a bellyful of carp, bread and breakfast ale, muttered “Anan?” from above his copy of Lucretius. He sat in the window-seat of the great stone kitchen. Upon one long iron spit before the fire fourteen trussed capons turned in unison; the wooden shoes of the basting-maid clattered industriously; and from the chimney came the clank of the invisible smoke-vanes and the be-sooted chains. The magister, who loved above all things warmth, a full stomach, a comfortable woman and a good book, had all these things; he was well minded to stay in Paris town for fourteen days, when they were to slay a brown pig from the Ardennes, against whose death he had written an elegy in Sapphics.
===Chinese===
三日之后的一个早晨，那位擒获了他的女子对硕士乌达尔（Magister Udal）说道：
“丈夫，该是我把新婚之礼赠给你的时候了。”
硕士腹中盛满鲤鱼、面包与早麦酒，正自受用，从那部卢克莱修的书页上方咕哝了一声：“嗄？”他坐在大石厨房的窗座里。炉火之前，一根长长的铁烤叉上，十四只捆扎停当的肥阉鸡齐整地转动；浇汁的女仆木鞋嗒嗒，忙得起劲；烟囱深处传来看不见的风门板与熏得乌黑的铁链的铿锵之声。这位硕士平生所爱，莫过于温暖、饱腹、可意的女人和一本好书，此刻四者样样俱足；他很有意就在这巴黎城里住上十四天——到那时他们要宰一头阿登地方来的棕猪，他已预先为它的死写好了一首萨福体的挽歌。

===Original===
“For,” said his better half, standing before him with a great loaf clasped to her bosom, “if you turn a horse from the stable between full and half full, like as not he will return of fair will to the crib.”
“Oh Venus and Hebe in one body,” the magister said, “I am minded to end here my scholarly days.”
“I am minded that ye shall travel far erstwhile,” she answered.
He laid down his book upon a clean chopping-board.
“I know a good harbourage,” he said.
===Chinese===
“因为，”他的贤内助抱着一大条面包，立在他面前说道，“马若在半饱不饱之时牵出厩去，多半它会心甘情愿回到槽头来。”
“哦，维纳斯与赫柏合于一身，”硕士说道，“我倒有意就在此地终了我的书斋岁月。”
“我却有意叫你不久作一次远游，”她答道。
他把书搁在一块干净的砧板上。
“我知道一处好的避风港，”他说。

===Original===
She sat down beside him in the window and fingered the fur on his long gown, saying that, in this light, it showed ill-favouredly worm-eaten; and he answered that he never had wishes nor money for gowning himself, who cultivated the muses upon short commons. She turned rightway to the front the medal upon his chest, and folded her arms.
“Whilst ye have no better house to harbour us,” she said, “this shall serve. Let us talk of the to-come.”
He groaned a little.
“Let us love today that’s here,” he said. “I will read thee a verse from Lucretius, and you shall tell me the history of that fourth capon”—he pointed to a browned carcase that, upon the spit, whirled its elbows a full third longer than any of the line.
“That is the master roasting-piece,” she said, “so he browns there not too far, nor too close, for the envoy’s own eating.”
===Chinese===
她在窗边他身旁坐下，捻弄着他长袍上的毛皮，说这样的光线下看去，那毛皮已叫虫蛀得不成体统；他答道，他这等靠菲薄口粮供奉缪斯的人，从来既无心思亦无银钱讲究穿袍。她把他胸前的徽章拨回正面，随后抱起两臂。
“趁你还没有更好的宅子收留我们，”她说，“这里便先将就着。咱们且谈谈往后吧。”
他低低呻吟了一声。
“让我们爱这眼前的今天吧，”他说，“我念一节卢克莱修给你听，你再讲讲那第四只阉鸡的身世”——他指着一只烤得焦黄的鸡，那鸡在烤叉上抡着两条“胳膊肘”，比一排里哪一只都足足长出三分之一。
“那是烤件里的头一份，”她说，“挂得不远不近，火色恰好——那是预备使节大人亲口受用的。”

===Original===
He considered the chicken with his head to one side.
“It is the place of a wife to be subject to her lord,” he said.
“It is the place of a husband that he fendeth for ’s wife,” she answered him. She tapped her fingers determinedly upon her elbows.
“So it is,” she continued. “Tomorrow you shall set out for London city to make road towards becoming Sir Chancellor.” Whilst he groaned she laid down for him her law. He was to go to England, he was to strive for great posts: if he gained, she would come share them; if he failed, he might at odd moments come back to her fireside. “Have done with groaning now,” she said, stilling his lamentations. “Keep them even for the next wench that you shall sue to—of me you have had all you asked.”
===Chinese===
他偏着头，端详那只鸡。
“做妻子的本分，是顺服她的夫主，”他说。
“做丈夫的本分，是养活他的妻室，”她回敬他。她把手指笃笃地敲在自己的胳膊肘上，主意已定。
“正是如此，”她接着说。“明天你就动身去伦敦城，去铺设那条通往大法官尊位的进身之路。”他一路呻吟，她一路给他立下她的法度：他得去英格兰，得去钻营显要的职位；若得了手，她便来同享；若败了阵，他也不妨得闲时回到她的炉火边来。“如今且住了呻吟，”她说，止住他的哀叹，“留着它们，留给你下一个求婚的小娘子——我这里，你讨的都已讨全了。”

===Original===
He considered for five seconds, his elbow upon his crossed knees and his wrist supporting his lean brown face.
“It is in the essence of it a good bargain,” he said. “You put against the chance of being, you a chancellor’s madam, mine of having for certain a capon in Paris town.”
He tapped his long nose. “Nevertheless, for your stake you have cast down a very little: three nights of bed and board against the chaining me up.”
“Husband,” she answered. “More than that you shall have.”
He wriggled a little beneath his furs.
===Chinese===
他思量了五秒钟，胳膊肘架在盘起的膝头，手腕托着那张瘦削的褐色面孔。
“这买卖就其实质而论是桩好买卖，”他说。“你押上‘做’的指望——做大法官夫人；我押上‘有’的实货——在巴黎城里稳稳有一只阉鸡。”
他敲了敲自己那只长鼻子。“不过话说回来，为着这一注，你抛下的实在很少：三夜的床铺饭食，博的却是我这人被拴牢一辈子。”
“丈夫，”她答道。“你将得到的还不止这个。”
他在毛皮底下扭动了一下身子。

===Original===
“Husband is an ill name,” he commented. “It smarts.”
“But it fills the belly.”
“Aye,” he said. “Therefore I am minded to bide here and take with the sourness the sweet of it.”
She laughed a little, and, with a great knife, cut a large manchet from the loaf between them.
“Nay,” she said, “tomorrow my army with their spits and forks shall drive thee from the door.”
===Chinese===
“‘丈夫’是个扎人的名号，”他品评道。“叫人刺痛。”
“可它填得饱肚皮。”
“是啊，”他说。“所以我才有意守在此地，连酸带甜一并领受。”
她微微一笑，拿一把大刀从两人之间的那条面包上切下厚厚一大块精白面包。
“不，”她说，“明天我这支带着烤叉与肉叉的大军，就要把你逐出门去。”

===Original===
He grinned with his lips. She was fair and fat beneath her hood, but she was resolute. “I have it in me greatly to advance you,” she said.
A boy brought her a trencher filled with chopped things, and a man in a blue jerkin came to her side bearing a middling pig, seared to a pale clear pinkness. The boy held the slit stomach carefully apart, and she lined it with slices of bread, dropping into the hollow chives, nutmegs, lumps of salt, the buds of bergamot, and marigold seeds with their acrid perfume, and balls of honied suet. She bound round it a fair linen cloth that she stitched with a great bone needle.
“Oh ingenuous countenance,” the magister mused above the pig’s mild face. “Is it not even the spit of the Cleves envoy’s? And the Cleves envoy shall eat this adorable monster. Oh, cruel anthropophagist!”
===Chinese===
他咧着嘴唇笑了。兜帽底下的她白净而丰腴，可主意是拿定了的。“我浑身上下有大本事，能保举你高升，”她说。
一个小厮给她端来一只盛满切碎之物的木盘；一个穿蓝短褂的汉子捧着一头中等大小的猪来到她身旁，猪身燎得泛出淡而清亮的粉色。小厮小心翼翼地掰开剖开的猪肚，她往里衬上面包片，又往空腔里撒下细香葱、肉豆蔻、一块块盐、佛手柑的花苞、带着辛烈香气的万寿菊籽，还有一团团蜜渍板油。她拿一块上好的麻布把它裹紧，用一根大骨针缝合起来。
“哦，这张老实的脸，”硕士对着猪那温驯的面容沉吟，“这不活脱脱是克利夫斯（Cleves）使节的一副模子么？而克利夫斯使节还要把这条可爱的怪物吃下肚去。哦，残忍的食人者！”

===Original===
She resigned her burden to the spit and gave the loaf to the boy, wiped her fingers upon her apron, and said:
“That pig shall help thee far upon thy road.”
“Goes it into my wallet?” he asked joyfully.
She answered: “Nay; into the Cleves envoy’s weam.”
“You speak in hard riddles,” he uttered.
“Nay,” she laughed, “a baby could unriddle it.” She looked at him for a moment to enjoy her triumph of mystery. “Husband mine, a pig thus stuffed is good eating for Cleves men. I have not kept a hostel for twelve years for envoys and secretaries without learning what each eats with pleasure. And long have I thought that if I wed a man it should be such a man as could thrive by learning of envoys’ secrets.”
===Chinese===
她把这件重负交托给烤叉，把面包交给小厮，在围裙上擦净手指，说道：
“这口猪会送你上路，送得远远的。”
“它可是要装进我的行囊？”他欢天喜地问。
她答道：“不；是装进克利夫斯使节的肚腹。”
“你打的是难猜的哑谜，”他说。
“不，”她笑道，“娃娃也猜得破。”她端详了他片刻，好细细受用这份神秘莫测的得意。“我的丈夫，这样填法的猪，正是克利夫斯人的口福。我开了十二年客栈，接待使节与秘书之流，若还学不来各人爱吃什么，岂不白开。再者，我早就在盘算：我若嫁人，就该嫁一个能靠探听使节们的机密发迹的人。”

===Original===
He leaned towards her earnestly.
“You know wherefore the man from Cleves is come?”
“You are, even as I have heard it said, a spy of Thomas Cromwell?” she asked in return.
He looked suddenly abashed, but she held to her question.
“I pass for Privy Seal’s man,” he answered at last.
===Chinese===
他朝她凑近了些，一脸恳切。
“你可知道克利夫斯来的人为的是什么？”
“你呢，正像听人所讲，是托马斯·克伦威尔（Thomas Cromwell）的探子？”她反问。
他忽然面露惭色，她却咬定她的问题不放。
“外间把我当作御玺大人（Privy Seal）的人，”他终于答道。

===Original===
“But you have played him false,” she said. He grew pale, glanced over his shoulder, and put his finger on his lips.
“I’ll wager it was for a woman,” she accused him. She wiped her lips with her apron and dropped her hands upon her lap.
“Why, keep troth to Cromwell if you can,” she said.
“I do think his sun sets,” he whispered.
“Why, I am sorry for it,” she answered. “I have always loved him for a brewer’s son. My father was a brewer.”
===Chinese===
“可你已经对他不忠了，”她说。他脸色发白，回头望了一眼，把手指竖在唇上。
“我敢打赌，是为了个女人，”她数落他。她拿围裙擦了擦嘴唇，两手落在膝头。
“唉，你若守得住对克伦威尔的忠心，只管守去，”她说。
“我确实觉得他的太阳正在西沉，”他低语。
“唉，那我倒惋惜，”她答道。“我一向因为他是酿酒匠的儿子而喜欢他。我父亲也是酿酒的。”

===Original===
“Cromwell was begotten even by the devil,” Udal answered. “He made me write a comedy in the vulgar tongue.”
“Be it as you will,” she answered. “You shall know on which side to bite your cake better than I.”
He was still a little shaken at the thought of Privy Seal.
“If you know wherefore cometh Cleves’ envoy, much it shall help me to share the knowledge,” he said at last, “for by that I may know whether Cromwell or we do rise or fall.”
===Chinese===
“克伦威尔简直是魔鬼亲手生的，”乌达尔回答。“他逼我用村言俗语写了一部喜剧。”
“随你怎么说，”她答道。“你的蛋糕哪边该下口，你总比我明白。”
一想到御玺大人，他仍有些心神不定。
“克利夫斯的使节为何而来，你若知道，让我也分享这份见识，于我大有裨益，”他终于说道，“因为由此我便能断定，克伦威尔或是我们这班人，是起是落。”

===Original===
“If you have made a pact with a woman, have very great cares,” she answered dispassionately. “Doubtless you know how the dog wags its tail; but you are always a fool with a woman.”
“This woman shall be Queen if Cromwell fall,” the magister said, “and I shall rise with her.”
“But is no woman from Cleves’ Queen there now?” she asked.
“Cicely,” he answered highly, “you know much of capons and beeves, but there are queens that are none and do not queen it, and queans that are no queens and queen it.”
“And so ’twill be whilst men are men,” she retorted. “But neither my first nor my second had his doxies ruling within my house, do what they might beyond the door.”
===Chinese===
“你若同女人立了约，务须万分小心，”她不动声色地答道。“狗尾巴怎么摇，你无疑一清二楚；可你一碰上女人，就永远是个傻子。”
“克伦威尔一旦倒了，这个女人便做王后，”硕士说，“我也将随她一同起来。”
“可如今坐在那王后位上的，不正是克利夫斯家的女子么？”她问。
“西塞莉（Cicely），”他高声答道，“阉鸡与肉牛你懂得许多，可是有的‘王后’算不得王后，也不曾行王后之事；有的娼妇并非王后，倒行起王后之事来。”
“男人是男人一天，便一天如此，”她顶了回去。“不过我的头一个也好、第二个也好，任他们在门外面为所欲为，从不曾让他们的姘妇在我屋里做主。”

===Original===
He tried to impart to her some of the adoration he had for Katharine Howard—her learning, her faith, her tallness, her wit, and the deserved empiry that she had over King Henry VIII; but she only answered:
“Why, kiss the wench all you will, but do not come to tell me how she smells!”—and to his new protests: “Aye, you may well be right and she may well be Queen—for I know you will sacrifice your ease for no wench that shall not help you somewhere forwards.”
The magister held his hands above his head in shocked negation of this injustice—but there came from the street the thin wail of a trumpet; another joined it, and a third; the three sounds executed a triple convolution and died away one by one. Holding his thin hand out for silence and better hearing, he muttered:
“Norfolk’s tucket! Then it is true that Norfolk comes to Paris.”
===Chinese===
他设法让自己那份对凯瑟琳·霍华德（Katharine Howard）的倾慕感染她几分——她的学问、她的虔信、她的颀长、她的才思，还有她对国王亨利八世（Henry VIII）那份受之无愧的统驭之权；可她只答了一句：
“唉，那小娘子你只管去亲，可别跑来告诉我她闻着是什么香！”——面对他新的抗辩，她又说：“是啊，你或许不错，她或许真会做王后——因为我知道，哪个小娘子若不能在什么地方推你一把，你决计不肯为她牺牲安逸。”
硕士把双手举过头顶，对这桩冤枉表示惊骇的否认——街上却传来号角细而哀的一声长鸣；另一支应和上来，又添了第三支；三个声音绕出一个三重的回环，逐一消散。他伸出瘦手示意肃静，好听得更真切，口中咕哝道：
“诺福克公爵（Norfolk）的号调！这么说，诺福克来巴黎是真的了。”

===Original===
His wife slipped down from her seat.
“Gave I you not the ostler’s gossip from Calais three days since?” she said, and went towards her roastings.
“But wherefore comes the yellow dog to Paris?” Udal persisted.
“That you may go seek,” she answered. “But believe always what an innkeeper says of who are on the road.”
===Chinese===
他妻子从座位上溜了下来。
“三日之前，我岂没把加莱（Calais）马夫的闲话讲给你听？”她说着，朝她的烤件走去。
“可那条黄狗来巴黎做什么？”乌达尔追问。
“这该由你去查究了，”她答道。“不过，路上有谁在走动，客栈掌柜的话，你永远要信。”

===Original===
Udal too slipped down from the window-seat; he buttoned his gown down to his shins, pulled his hat over his ears and hurried through the galleried courtyard into the comfortless shadows of the street. There was no doubt that Norfolk was coming; round the tiny crack that, two houses away, served for all the space that the road had between the towering housefronts, two men in scarlet and yellow, with leopards and lions and fleurs-de-lis on their chests, walked between two in white, tabarded with the great lilies of France. They crushed round the corner, for there was scarce space for four men abreast; behind them squeezed men in purple with the Howard knot, bearing pikes, and men in mustard yellow with the eagle’s wing and ship badge of the Provost of Paris. In the broader space before the arch of Udal’s courtyard they stayed to wait for the horsemen to disentangle themselves from the alley; the Englishmen looked glumly at the tall housefronts; the French loosened the mouthplates of their helmets to breathe the air for a minute. Hostlers, packmen and pedlars began to fill the space behind Udal, and he heard his wife’s voice calling shrilly to a cook who had run across the yard.
The crowd a little shielded him from the draught which came through the arch, and he waited with more contentment. Undoubtedly there was Norfolk upon a great yellow horse, so high that it made his bonnet almost touch the overhanging storey of the third house; behind him the white and gold litter of the provost, who, having three weeks before broken his leg at tennis-play, was still unable to sit in a saddle. The duke rode as if implacably rigid, his yellow, long face set, listening as if with a sour deafness to something that the provost from below called to him with a great, laughing voice.
The provost’s litter, too, came up alongside the duke’s horse in the open space, then they all moved forward at the slow processional: three steps and a halt for the trumpets to blow a tucket; three more and another tucket; the great yellow horse stepping high and casting up his head, from which flew many flakes of white foam. With its slow, regularly interrupted gait, dominated by the impassive yellow face of Norfolk, the whole band had an air of performing a solemn dance, and Udal shivered for a long time, till amidst the train of mules bearing leathern sacks, cupboards, chests and commodes, he saw come riding a familiar figure in a scholar’s gown—the young pedagogue and companion of the Earl of Surrey. He was a fair, bearded youth with blue eyes, riding a restless colt that embroiled itself and plunged amongst the mules’ legs. The young man leaned forward in the saddle and craned to avoid a clothes chest.
===Chinese===
乌达尔也从窗座上溜了下来；他把袍扣一路系到小腿，把帽子拉下来盖住耳朵，匆匆穿过带回廊的院子，走进街上凄冷的暗影里。诺福克来了，这已无可置疑；两幢开外，高耸的房屋立面之间，那道细缝便是整条街仅有的空隙——两个穿红黄号衣的人走在两个白衣人中间：红黄号衣的胸前绣着豹、狮与百合花徽，白衣人则身罩缀有法兰西大百合的纹章罩衣。他们挤挤挨挨拐过墙角，因为四人并行已嫌局促；身后挤着穿紫衣的汉子，佩着霍华德家的绳结纹章，扛着长矛，还有芥末黄号衣的汉子，佩着巴黎警备总监的鹰翼与船形徽记。在乌达尔院子拱门前的稍开阔处，他们停下来，等候骑马的人从窄巷里脱身出来；英格兰人阴沉着脸打量高高的房屋立面；法兰西人松开头盔的护面，畅快地呼吸了一分钟。马夫、行商与小贩开始在乌达尔身后聚拢，他听见妻子尖声唤一个跑过院子的厨子。
人群稍稍替他挡住了从拱门灌进来的风，他等得也自在了几分。毫无疑问，那正是诺福克，骑在一匹高大的黄马上，马身之高，几乎叫他头上的软帽碰到第三家房子悬挑出来的楼层；他身后是警备总监那乘白金二色的轿舆——总监三周前打网球戏摔断了腿，至今上不得马鞍。公爵骑在马上，僵直得不近人情，蜡黄的长脸绷紧着；警备总监在下头扯着洪亮的大嗓门、笑着向他喊话，他听着，仿佛带着一肚子酸楚的重听。
警备总监的轿舆也在空地上赶上前来，与公爵的马并辔而行；随后全队以迟缓的行进仪节向前挪动：走三步便停一停，让号手吹一通号调；再三步，再一通号调；那匹大黄马高抬细步，昂首甩脑，喷出片片白沫。步伐迟缓而有节地时断时续，又统摄于诺福克那张不动声色的黄脸之下，全队人马透着一股庄重起舞的神气。乌达尔战栗了许久，直到在驮着皮囊、柜橱、箱笼与抽屉柜的骡队中间，他看见一个熟悉的身影纵马而来——一个穿学者长袍的年轻塾师，萨里伯爵（the Earl of Surrey）的伴读。那是个白净的蓄须青年，生一双蓝眼睛，骑着一匹不安分的驹子，那驹子夹在骡腿之间缠夹不清，又蹦又蹿。年轻人在鞍上俯下身，伸长脖子躲一口衣箱。

===Original===
The magister called to him:
“Ho, Longstaffe!” and having caught his pleased eyes: “*Ecce quis sto in arce plenitatis. Veni atque bibe! Magister sum. Udal sum. Longstaffe ave.*”
Longstaffe slipped from his horse, which he left to be rescued by whom it might from amongst the hard-angled cases.
“Assuredly,” he said, “there is no love between that beast and me as there was betwixt his lord and Bucephalus,” and he followed Udal into the galleried courtyard, where their two gowned figures alone sought shelter from the March showers.
===Chinese===
硕士朝他喊道：
“嗬，朗斯塔夫（Longstaffe）！”接住他那双欣然的眼睛之后，又道：“*Ecce quis sto in arce plenitatis. Veni atque bibe! Magister sum. Udal sum. Longstaffe ave.*”（看哪，我立于丰足之堡。来，且饮一杯！我是硕士，是乌达尔。朗斯塔夫，万福。）
朗斯塔夫翻身下马，把那匹马撇下不管，听凭随便哪个人去从棱角坚硬的箱笼堆里把它解救出来。
“千真万确，”他说，“那畜生与我之间可没什么情分，不像它主人同布西发拉斯（Bucephalus）那样。”他跟着乌达尔走进带回廊的院子——三月的阵雨里，唯有他们两个穿袍的身影在廊下避雨。

===Original===
“News from overseas there is none,” he said. “Privy Seal ruleth still about the King; the German astronomers have put forth a tract *De Quadratura Circuli*; the lost continent of Atlantis is a lost continent still—and my bones ache.”
“But your mission?” Udal asked.
The doctor, his hard blue eyes spinning with sardonic humour beneath his black beretta, said that his mission, even as Udal’s had been, was to gain some crowns by setting into the learned language letters that should pass between his ambassador and the King’s men of France. Udal grinned disconcertedly.
“Be certified in your mind,” he said, “that I am not here a spy or informer of Privy Seal’s.”
“Forbid it, God,” Doctor Longstaffe answered good-humouredly. None the less his jaw hardened beneath his fair beard and he answered, “I have as yet written no letters—*litteras nullas scripsi: argal nihil scio*.”
===Chinese===
“海外的消息，一丝也无，”他说。“御玺大人照旧在御前当权；德意志的天文学家刊出了一篇 *De Quadratura Circuli*（《化圆为方》）的论文；沉没了的亚特兰蒂斯大陆依旧是片沉没的大陆——还有，我的骨头酸痛。”
“可你的使命呢？”乌达尔问。
博士一双硬朗的蓝眼睛在黑色贝雷帽底下滴溜溜转着讥诮的眼波，说道：他的使命一如乌达尔先前的使命，不过是把他那位大使与法兰西国王的臣僚之间往来的函件译成学问的语言，好挣几枚克朗用度。乌达尔窘迫地咧嘴一笑。
“你心里给我记定了，”他说，“我到此地来，可不是御玺大人的探子或告密人。”
“上帝不容，”朗斯塔夫博士和颜悦色地答道。虽则如此，他白净的胡须底下腮帮一硬，又答了一句：“我一封信也不曾写过——*litteras nullas scripsi: argal nihil scio*（一函未书：故一无所知）。”

===Original===
“Why, ye shall drink a warmed draught and eat a drippinged soppet,” Udal said, “and you shall tell me what in England is said of this mission.”
He led the fair doctor into the great kitchen, and felt a great stab of dislike when the young man set his arm round the hostess’s waist and kissed her on the red cheeks. The young man laughed:
“Aye indeed; I am *mancipium paucae lectionis* set beside so learned a man as the magister.”
The hostess received him with a bridling favour, rubbing her cheek pleasantly, whilst Udal was seeking to persuade himself that, since the woman was in law no wife of his, he had no need to fear. Nevertheless rage tore him when the doctor, leaning his back against the window-side, talked to the woman. She stood between them holding a pewter flagon of mulled hypocras upon a salver of burnished pewter.
===Chinese===
“那好，你要喝一盏温热的酒，吃一块浸透肉汁的小面包，”乌达尔说，“再告诉我，英格兰那边对这趟使命都说些什么。”
他把这位白净的博士领进大厨房；那年轻人一把搂住女主人的腰，在她红润的脸颊上亲了一口，他顿觉一股厌恶狠狠扎进心头。年轻人笑道：
“可不是；比起硕士这等博学的人物，我不过是 *mancipium paucae lectionis*（一个读书无多的奴仆）罢了。”
女主人拿腔作态、恩宠有加地接待了他，一面快活地揉着自己的脸颊；乌达尔却在一旁竭力说服自己：这女人在法律上既非他的妻子，他便无须害怕。然而，当博士背靠窗沿、同那女人攀谈起来的时候，怒火仍把他撕扯着。她立在两人中间，端着一只锡壶，壶里盛着温热的希帕拉斯香料酒，底下垫一方擦得锃亮的锡盘。

===Original===
“Who I be,” he said, gazing complacently at her, “is a poor student of good letters; how I be here is as one of the amanuenses of the Duke of Norfolk. Origen, Eusebius telleth, had seven, given him by Ambrosius to do his behest. The duke hath but two, given him by the grace of God and of the King’s high mercy.”
“I make no doubt,” she answered, “ye be as learned as the seven were.”
“I be twice as hungry,” he laughed; “but with me it has always been ‘*Quid scribam non quemadmodum*,’ wherein I follow Seneca.”
===Chinese===
“我是什么人，”他扬扬得意地端详着她，说道，“是个研习佳文的穷书生；我怎么会在这里，是做了诺福克公爵的抄书手之一。优西比乌告诉我们，奥利金曾有七名抄书手，是安布罗西乌斯赠与他差遣的。公爵只有两名，是蒙上帝的恩典与国王隆盛的慈悲赐予的。”
“这毫无疑问，”她答道，“你们俩同那七位一样有学问。”
“我却比他们饿上一倍，”他笑道；“不过我向来奉行‘*Quid scribam non quemadmodum*’（只问写什么，不问怎么写），这一层我师法塞内加。”

===Original===
“Doctor,” the magister uttered, quivering, “you shall tell me why this mission—which is a very special embassy—at this time cometh to this town of Paris.”
“Magister,” the doctor answered, wagging his beard upon his poor collar to signify that he desired to keep his neck where it was, “I know not.”
“Injurious man,” Udal fulminated, “I be no spy.”
The doctor surveyed his perturbation with cross-legged calmness.
“An ye were,” he said—“and it is renowned that ye are—”ye could get no knowledge from where none is.
===Chinese===
“博士，”硕士颤巍巍地说道，“你要告诉我，这趟使命——这可是一席非常特命的使团——为何在这时候来到巴黎城。”
“硕士，”博士答道，胡须在寒酸的领口上方摆了又摆，示意他极愿叫脖颈安守原位，“我不知道。”
“好个无礼之徒，”乌达尔喝道，“我不是探子。”
博士盘着腿，安安稳稳地端详他的慌乱。
“就算你是，”他说——“况且你素有此名，尽人皆知——”没有消息的地方，你也掏不出消息来。

===Original===
“Why, tell me of a woman,” the hostess said. “Who is Kat Howard?”
The doctor’s blue eyes shot a hard glance at her, and he let his head sink down.
“I have copied to her eyes a sonnet or twain,” he said, “and they were writ by my master, Surrey, the Duke o’ Norfolk’s son.”
“Then these rave upon her as doth the magister?” she asked.
===Chinese===
“那好，给我讲个女人的事，”女主人说。“凯特·霍华德（Kat Howard）是谁？”
博士的蓝眼睛朝她射出冷硬的一瞥，随即垂下了头。
“我替她那双眼睛誊写过一两首十四行诗，”他说，“诗是我主人写的——萨里，诺福克公爵的公子。”
“这么说，那些诗也同硕士一样，为她如痴如狂？”她问。

===Original===
“Why, an ye be jealous of the magister here,” the doctor clipped his words precisely, “cast him away and take me who am a proper sweetheart.”
“I be wed,” she answered pleasantly.
“What matters that,” he said, “when husbands are not near?”
===Chinese===
“这个嘛，你若为眼前这位硕士吃醋，”博士一板一眼、咬字清晰地说，“把他撇了，换上我——我可是个像样的情人。”
“我是嫁了人的，”她答得和和气气。
“那有什么要紧，”他说，“丈夫又不在跟前？”

===Original===
The magister, torn between his unaccustomed gust of jealousy and the desire to hide his marriage from a disastrous discovery in England, clutched with straining fingers at his gown.
“Tell wherefore cometh your mission,” he said.
“We spoke of a fair woman,” the doctor answered. “Shame it were before Apollo and Priapus that men’s missions should come before kings’ mistresses.”
“It is true, then, that she shall be queen?” Udal’s wife asked.
===Chinese===
硕士一半受着那阵不惯而起的醋意煎熬，一半又想瞒住这门婚事、免得日后在英格兰败露成祸，手指死死攥住了自己的长袍。
“告诉我你们的使命为的什么，”他说。
“我们方才讲的是一位美人，”博士答道。“人的使命倘若竟要排在国王们的情妇前头来讲，那在阿波罗与普里阿波斯跟前可是羞煞人也。”
“那么，她将做王后，这是真的了？”乌达尔的妻子问。

===Original===
The fall of a great dish in the rear of the tall kitchen gave the scholar time to collect his suspicions—for he took it for an easy thing that this woman, if she were Udal’s leman, might be, she too, a spy in the service of Privy Seal.
“Forbid it, God,” he said, “that ye take my words as other than allegorical. The lady Katharine may be spoken of as a king’s mistress since in truth she were a fit mistress for a king, being fair, devout, learned, courteous, tall and sweet-voiced. But that she hath been kind to the King, God forbid that I should say it.”
“Aye,” Udal said, “but if she hath sent this mission?”
Panic rose in the heart of the doctor; he beheld himself there, in what seemed a spy’s kitchen, asked disastrous questions by a man and woman and pinned into a window-seat. For there was no doubt that the rumour ran in England that this mission had been sent by the King because Katharine Howard so wished it sent. In that age of spies and treacheries no man’s head was safe on his shoulders—and here were Cromwell’s spies asking news of Cromwell’s chief enemy.
===Chinese===
高大的厨房后部哐啷一声跌落一只大碟子，给了这位学者收束疑心的工夫——他心中暗想：这女人若果真是乌达尔的情妇，多半也是御玺大人手下的一名探子，此事再容易想到不过。
“上帝不容，”他说，“不容诸位从我的话里听出寓言之外的意思。凯瑟琳小姐尽可以称作国王的意中人，因为论实情，她配做一位国王的意中人——她姣好、虔诚、博学、知礼、颀长、嗓音甜美。至于她可曾对国王施过恩好，上帝不容我说这话。”
“是啊，”乌达尔说，“可这趟使命若是她遣来的呢？”
博士心头涌起一阵恐慌；他眼见自己身在这个形同探子窝的厨房里，被一男一女问着足以惹祸的问题，还钉死在窗座上。因为毫无疑义，英格兰正盛传：这趟使命出自国王的差遣，只因凯瑟琳·霍华德心愿如此。在那奸细与叛逆横行的年月，谁的头颅安在肩上都不敢保——而眼前正是克伦威尔的探子，在打听克伦威尔头号仇敌的消息。

===Original===
He stretched out a calm hand and spoke slowly:
“Madam hostess,” he said, “if ye be jealous of the magister ye may well be jealous, for great beauty and worship hath this lady.” Yet she need be little jealous, for this lady was nowadays prized so high that she might marry any man in the land—and learned men were little prized. Any man in the land of England she might wed—saving only such as were wed, amongst whom was their lord the King, who was happily wed to the gracious lady whom my Lord Privy Seal did bring from Cleves to be their very virtuous Queen.
Here, it seemed to him, he had cleared himself very handsomely of suspicion of ill will to Privy Seal or of wishing ill to Anne of Cleves.
===Chinese===
他安然伸出一只手，缓缓说道：
“老板娘夫人，”他说，“你若为这位硕士吃醋，那醋也吃得有理，因为这位小姐容色倾城，尊荣隆重。”不过她大可不必怎么吃醋：如今这位小姐身价之高，国中哪个男子都配她下嫁——而有学问的男子却身价低微。英格兰国中的男子她尽可以嫁——只是已娶之人除外；已娶者之中，便有他们尊贵的主上国王，而国王已经欢欢喜喜地同那位仁慈的夫人完婚——那位夫人，便是御玺大人从克利夫斯迎来、立为他们至为贤德的王后的那一位。
在他想来，经此一席话，他已把对御玺大人怀有恶意、咒诅克利夫斯的安妮（Anne of Cleves）的嫌疑，撇得干干净净、体体面面。

===Original===
“For the rest,” he said, sighing with relief to be away from dangerous grounds, “your magister is safe from the toils of marriage with the Lady Katharine.” Still it might be held that jealousy is aroused by the loving and not by the returning of that love; for it was very certain that the magister much had loved this lady. Many did hold it a treachery in him, till now, to the Privy Seal whom he served. But now he might love her duteously, since our lord the King had commanded the Lady Katharine to join hands with Privy Seal, and Privy Seal to cement a friendly edifice in his heart towards the lady. Thus it was no treason to Privy Seal in him to love her. But to her it was a treason great and not to be comprehended.
He ogled Udal’s wife in the gallant manner and prayed her to prepare a bed for him in that hostelry. He had been minded to lodge with a Frenchman named Clement; but having seen her…
“Learned sir,” she answered, “a good bed I have for you.” But if he sought to go beyond her lips she had a bodyguard of spitmen that the magister’s self had seen.
The doctor kissed her agreeably and, with a great sigh of relief, hurried from the door.
“May Bacchus who maketh mad, and the Furies that pursued Orestes, defile the day when I cross this step again,” he muttered as he swung under the arch and ran to follow the mule train.
For the magister, by playing with his reputation of being Cromwell’s spy, had so effectually caused terror of himself to pervade those who supported the old faith that he had much ado at times to find company even amongst the lovers of good letters.
===Chinese===
“至于其余，”他说，庆幸脱离了危险的地面，暗暗舒了一口气，“你们这位硕士要陷进同凯瑟琳小姐结姻的罗网，是断然不至于的。”话虽如此，也可以说：醋意生于爱，而非生于被爱；因为确凿无疑，这位硕士深爱过这位小姐。直到如今，许多人还认定这是他对他所侍奉的御玺大人的叛逆。可如今他尽可以名正言顺地爱她了：既然我们尊贵的主上国王已吩咐凯瑟琳小姐同御玺大人携手，也吩咐御玺大人在心中为这位小姐筑起一座友谊的殿堂。这样，他爱她，于御玺大人便算不得叛逆。而于她，那却是一桩大得叫人无从索解的叛逆。
他殷勤多情地朝乌达尔的妻子丢了个眼风，求她在这家客栈里替他备一张床铺。他本有意寄宿在一个名叫克莱芒（Clement）的法国人家里；可是既已见着她……
“博学的先生，”她答道，“上好的床铺我这里有一张，伺候您。”可他若想越过了嘴唇还不肯干休，她手底下有一队执烤叉的壮汉护卫——硕士亲眼见识过的。
博士称心遂意地亲了她一下，长长舒了一口如释重负的气，匆匆夺门而去。
“愿叫人癫狂的巴克斯，与追逐俄瑞斯忒斯的复仇女神，一同污毁我重跨这道门槛的日子，”他一面咕哝，一面闪身钻过拱门，跑去追赶骡队。
原来这位硕士素来把“克伦威尔探子”的名声当把戏耍弄，竟叫拥护旧信仰的人们对他怕入骨髓，以至于他不时想寻个人做伴都大费周章，纵使是在爱好佳文妙字的人们中间。

### III / 三

===Original===
In the kitchen the spits had ceased turning, the dishes had been borne upstairs to the envoy from Cleves, the scullions were wiping knives, the maids were rubbing pieces of bread in the dripping pans and licking their fingers after the succulent morsels. The magister stood, a long crimson blot in the window-way; the hostess was setting flagons carefully into the great armoury.

“Madam wife,” the magister said to her at last, when she came near, “ye see how weighty it is that I bide here.”

“Husband,” she said, “I see how weighty it is that ye hasten to London.”

His rage broke—he whirled his arms above his head.

“Naughty woman!” he screamed harshly. “Shalt be beaten.” He strode across to the basting range and gripped a great ladle, his brown eyes glinting, and stood caressing his thin chin passionately.
===Chinese===
厨房里，烤叉已经停了转动，菜肴已给捧上楼去，供奉克莱夫斯（Cleves）的使节了；帮厨的正擦着刀子，女仆们拿面包片在油盘里揩拭，咽下那些肥美的碎屑之后，还吮着手指头。学士先生（magister）立在窗口，像一道长长的猩红渍痕；女店主正小心翼翼地把大酒壶一只只收进大壁柜。

“夫人娘子，”学士先生等她走近了，终于开口，“你可瞧见了，我留在这儿是何等要紧。”

“夫君，”她说，“我也瞧见了，你火速赶去伦敦是何等要紧。”

他勃然大怒——双臂抡起，在头顶乱晃。

“刁妇！”他声气粗厉地尖叫。“该打！”他大步跨到浇汁的灶台边，攥住一柄大长勺，棕色的眼睛闪闪发亮，站在那儿发狂似的摩挲着自己尖瘦的下巴。
===Original===
She folded her arms complacently.
